         to Paramount+, then outbidding RTL to secure the UEFA Europa League and
         Conference League in the next four-year cycle. (357)
(318)    Accordingly, even if each of the affected potential segments were to be considered
         separate markets, they would nonetheless face strong out-of-market constraints
         by other segments.
(319)    Sixth, the Commission acknowledges that certain concerns were raised by some
         competitors of the Parties in the market for the acquisition of sports rights. In this
         context, the Commission notes that while licensors often package media rights so
         as to allow fragmentation of certain sports events/competitions/league for several
         purchasers, the exclusivity associated with sports licenses results in each package
         of rights being held only by one player (with some exceptions related to the
         possibility of sublicensing of rights). Accordingly, the concerns raised by
         competitors in relation to a stronger market position in the acquisition market,
         would not fit into a traditional theory of harm relating to increased buyer power
         (which should cause concerns by rights holders in the first place), but essentially
         relate to a potential exclusion of competing purchasers by the merged entity, in
         view of a stronger financial capacity to acquire certain exclusive sports rights.
         Arguably, such potential effects would be produced rather in the downstream
         markets for wholesale supply of TV/AV channels and services and AV retail to
         end customers and could therefore also be described as vertical effects, namely
         input foreclosure. Nonetheless, the Commission also addressed such concerns in
         the context of horizontal unilateral effects, namely whether the transaction would
         lead to the elimination of significant competitive constraints in the market for the
         acquisition of sports rights, leading to increased license fees and exclusion of
         competition purchasers.
(320)    Specifically, competitors have emphasized, for instance, that “[T]he merged
         entity would control a dominant share of premium sports rights (in particular
         football rights), reducing [our] negotiating power and potentially limiting access
         to essential sports content”, (358) and that “[T]he merged entity will be able to
         apply significant financial and commercial leverage in the acquisition of sports
         rights. This could lead to the merged entity gaining considerable market power,
         potentially making it more difficult or even impossible for competitors to acquire
         sports rights”. (359)
(321)    In addition, one competitor of the Parties submits that the merged entity would
         benefit from the most comprehensive portfolio of regular national club football
         competitions, as well as a diverse set of premium sports rights to other major
         European sports (such as Formula 1, NFL and MMA fighting), combined with
         other premium AV content in nearly all genres (e.g. films, series, reality TV and
         shows)”. (360)
(322)    The Commission considers the Transaction does not raise serious doubts as to the
         elimination of significant constraints in the market for the acquisition of sports
         rights.

         Submission, these would arguably constitute an even narrower market segment of
         ‘regular national club football competitions’ – in which the increment coming
         from RTL is, as previously mentioned, negligible. In any case, the increment
         resulting from the transaction is small (lower than [5-10]%) in the market in which
         competitors have raised concerns.
(324)    Secondly, sports rights’ holders hold the leverage in the negotiations for the
         acquisition of sports rights, namely in what concerns price and other license
         conditions, and consider that the bargaining power of the merged entity will
         remain similar after the Transaction, thus not leading to an increased buyer power
         which would lead to a significant impediment of competition. This is also
         supported by the fact that the majority of market respondents (also including
         competitors of the Parties), confirmed the existence of sufficient alternatives for
         each of the market and the increasing competitive pressure of global streaming
         platforms.
(325)    Thirdly, sports rights holders have expressed positive views for the impact of the
         Transaction in the market for the acquisition of sports rights. For instance,
         respondents have stated that:
         (a)       “[…] a merged Sky DACH and RTL will likely become more competitive
                   in the market and represent a greater competitive constraint to
                   international streaming companies when [sports rights’ holder] sells its
                   live broadcasting rights in the relevant markets, increasing the level of
                   competition for [sports rights’ holder] rights. […] a merged Sky DACH
                   and RTL may be able to offer a better product for its customers and
                   cross-promoter their respective offerings, which is something the parties
                   do not currently do pursuant to their existing sub-licensor/sub-licensee
                   relationship”; (361)
         (b)       “[…] merged Sky–RTL could moderately increase buyer leverage
                   through integrated reach and monetization, but auction architecture and
                   competing global/European players will likely keep markets
                   competitive”; (362)
         (c)       “Creates a more competitive environment for sports rights”. (363)
(326)    Fourthly, as explained above, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         would continue to face high competitive constraints from several market players,
         specifically from global streaming platforms.
(327)    Accordingly, the Commission considers that the Transaction will not lead to an
         increase in buyer power by the merged entity or otherwise elimination of
         significant competitive constraints, resulting in a significant impediment to
         effective competition in Germany.

6.1.3.3.1.3.    Conclusion
(328)    In light of the above, and for the purposes of this Decision, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction is does not to give rise to serious doubts as to the
         compatibility of the Transaction with the internal market as a result of horizontal
         unilateral effects in the market for the acquisition of sports rights in Germany, or
         any affected potential market segments (i.e. acquisition of commissioned and pre-
         produced sports content, pre-produced sports content, football, other sports and
         regular non-premium sports).

        6.1.3.3.2.      German-speaking Territories

6.1.3.3.2.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(329)    The Notifying Party submits the combination of the Parties’ activities does not
         pose a risk to effective competition in the market for the acquisition of sports
         content in the German-speaking Territories. (364)
(330)    The Notifying Party’s assessment is the same for the potential market of overall
         German-speaking Territories as for the potential national market for Germany, as
         sport content rights are often acquired in multi-territorial bundles covering the
         whole of the German-speaking Territories, and most often cover at least Germany
         and Austria. Therefore, the arguments presented in Section 6.1.3.3.1.1, namely on
         the existence of a dynamic competitive market for sports content, with a multitude
         of players, the role of tender procedures held by sophisticated licensors, the
         increasing importance of global streamers, as well as the Parties not being close
         competitors, are fully applicable to the overall German-speaking Territories. (365)

6.1.3.3.2.2.    Commission assessment
(331)    The Commission considers the Transaction is unlikely to lead to an increase of
         buyer power by the merged entity, which would result in a significant impediment
         of effective competition, in the market for the acquisition of sports AV content or
         any potential affected acquisition market segments in the German-speaking
         Territories (i.e., acquisition of pre-produced sports content, football, other sports
         and regular non-premium sports).
(332)    First, the Commission notes that when asked about the geographic scope of the
         market for the licensing of AV content, a large number of market participants
         considered the market to consist of at least the German speaking Territories (see
         Section 5.1.2.3). For respondents that consider the market to consist at least of
         German-speaking Territories, by definition, the conditions of competition in
         Germany are considered to be the same as in the German-speaking Territories.
         This applies to almost half of the respondents that provided a view on the
         geographic scope of the market for the licensing of AV content.
(333)    Second, the Commission notes that the Transaction gives rise to small market
         share increments in all affected potential market segments, except for regular non-
         premium rights. The Parties have a combined market shares [40-50]% in regular
         non-premium sports, [30-40]% in football, [30-40]% in overall pre-produced
         sports content and [20-30]% in other sports. However, the increment coming from



(364)   Form CO, paras 270, 463.
(365)   Form CO, paras 568.
                                             88
         RTL is [5-10]% or lower for all such segments, except in the case of regular non-
         premium sports, which amounts to [10-20]%, as per Table 6 above.
(334)    Third, considering the Transaction gives rise to the same affected market
         segments as in Germany (with the exceptions of the overall market for
         commissioned and pre-produced sports content), the Commission’s assessment
         with regards to Germany, set forth in Section 6.1.3.3.1 above, namely in what
         concerns the Parties’ not being close competitors, the results of the market
         investigation – in which the majority of respondents confirmed their replies would
         be applicable to all German-speaking Territories – (366), the existence of a
         dynamic competitive market with a multitude of players and the high competitive
         pressure from global streaming platforms, is fully applicable to the German-
         speaking Territories.
(335)    Accordingly, the Commission considers that the Transaction will not lead to an
         increase in buyer power by the merged entity, resulting in a significant
         impediment to effective competition in the German-speaking Territories.

6.1.3.3.2.3.    Conclusion
(336)    In light of the above, and for the purposes of this Decision, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction is does not give rise to serious doubts as to the
         compatibility of the Transaction with the internal market as a result of horizontal
         unilateral effects in the market for the acquisition of sports rights in the German-
         speaking Territories, or any affected potential segments (i.e. acquisition of pre-
         produced sports content, football, other sports and regular non-premium sports).

        6.1.3.3.3.      Austria

6.1.3.3.3.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(337)    The Notifying Party notes that the only affected market in Austria refers to
         potential market segment of sports content for Pay TV/SVoD distribution, in
         which the Transaction does not raise competition concerns. (367)
(338)    The Notifying Party submits that the combined market share in the potential
         segment (of [60-70]%) is hardly merger-related, considering that the increment is
         only [0-5]%. (368)
(339)    The Notifying Party further submits there is competition among FTA and pay-
         TV/SVoD players. There is thus significant competition to Sky DACH’s portfolio
         by publicly funded players, like ORF or ServusTV, which have substantial
         budgets, as well as German players, such as ARD/ZDF, which are also active in
         Austria, with substantial rights such as Bundesliga and DFB-Pokal. (369) . (370)
(340)    Finally, the Notifying Party’s arguments with regards to the existence of a
         dynamic competitive market for sports content, with a multitude of players, the
         role of tender procedures held by sophisticated licensors, the increasing
         importance of global streamers, as well as the Parties not being close competitors,

6.1.3.3.3.2.    Commission assessment
(341)    The Commission considers the Transaction is unlikely to lead to an increase of
         buyer power by the merged entity, which would result in a significant impediment
         of effective competition, in the potential market segment for the acquisition of
         SVoD/pay-TV sports content in Austria.
(342)    First, the Commission notes that the assessment undertaken for Germany could
         apply by analogy to all other German speaking territories because, when asked
         about the geographic scope of the market for the licensing of AV content, almost
         half of the market participants considered the market to consist of at least the
         German speaking Territories (see Section 5.1.2.3).
(343)    Second, the Commission notes that the Transaction gives a negligible increment
         of [0-5]% coming from RTL,(372) as per Table 8 above, and delta HHI of [0-100],
         which is deemed insusceptible to lead to an increase in buyer power, in
         exceptional circumstances such as those in the present case. (373)
(344)    Third, in relation to the potential segment of SVoD/pay-TV, the market
         investigation results also confirm that: (i) purchasers do not have the leverage on
         license negotiations; (374) (ii) there are sufficient alternative purchasers (currently
         and likely in the future); (375) and (iii) sports rights holders consider the Parties’
         bargaining power will remain the same after the Transaction. (376) The majority
         of respondents who expressed a view confirmed their replies would not be
         different for Austria. (377) In addition, the Commission notes that this segment
         will likely be the most impacted by the increase of competitive pressure from
         global streamers, regardless of the type of sports they acquire, as any sports
         content would feature in their SVoD streaming platforms.
(345)    Fourth, the Commission’s assessment with regards to Germany, set forth in
         Section 6.1.3.3.1.2 above, namely in what concerns the Parties’ not being close
         competitors, the existence of a dynamic competitive market with a multitude of

6.1.3.3.3.3.    Conclusion
(348)    In light of the above, and for the purposes of this Decision, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction is does not to give rise to serious doubts as to the
         compatibility of the Transaction with the internal market as a result of horizontal
         unilateral effects in the potential market segment for the acquisition of SVoD/pay-
         TV sports content in Austria.

        6.1.3.3.4.      Liechtenstein

6.1.3.3.4.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(349)    The Notifying Party submits that the combination of the Parties’ activities in each
         of the potential market segments for the acquisition of sports, football, other
         sports, regular non-premium sports as well as for the distribution on SVoD/pay,
         in Liechtenstein does not pose a risk to effective competition. (378)
(350)    The Notifying Party does not consider it appropriate to define a market for media
         rights for Liechtenstein, as it is not aware of licensors offering such licences on a
         stand-alone basis, considering that rights for Liechtenstein are usually a part of
         rights for the whole of the German-speaking Territories. Accordingly, the
         Notifying Party contests any concerns might be inferred from the market share
         estimates provided by them for Liechtenstein. In any case,
(351)    The Notifying Party’s arguments with regards the existence of a dynamic
         competitive market for sports content, with a multitude of players, the increasing
         importance of global streamers, as well as the Parties not being close competitors,
         also apply to Liechtenstein. (379)

6.1.3.3.4.2.    Commission assessment
(352)    The Commission considers the Transaction is unlikely to lead to an increase of
         buyer power by the merged entity, which would result in a significant impediment
         of effective competition, in any potential market segments for the acquisition of
         pre-produced sports, football, other sports, regular non-premium sport and
         SVoD/pay-TV sports content in Liechtenstein.
(353)    First, the Commission notes that the assessment undertaken for Germany could
         apply by analogy to all other German speaking territories because, when asked
         about the geographic scope of the market for the licensing of AV content, almost

(379)   Form CO, para. 617.

         half of the market participants considered the market to consist of at least the
         German speaking Territories (see Section 5.1.2.3).
(354)    Second, the Commission notes that the Transaction gives a negligible increments
         below [0-5]% coming from RTL, (380) in all potential segments, and delta HHI
         equal or below [0-100], (381) except for other sports which is [0-5]%, even though
         the combined market shares would be above 40% for most segments, reaching
         [60-70]% in SVoD/pay-TV sports, as per Table 11 above.
(355)    Nonetheless, the Commission notes that that the market shares for the acquisition
         of sports content in smaller countries such as Liechtenstein are likely overstated,
         as the Notifying Party indicated it could not comprehensively account for all
         sports licenses in these countries, as these are essentially acquired as part of
         licenses with a broader geographic scope, namely for the German-speaking
         Territories, (382), which is coherent with the fact that a large number of
         respondents to the market investigation consider the relevant market to be at least
         the German-speaking Territories.
(356)    Third, considering the Transaction gives rise to the same affected market
         segments as in Germany (with the exception of the overall market for
         commissioned and pre-produced sports content, which is not affected and
         SVoD/pay-TV sports, which is affected), the Commission’s assessment with
         regards to Germany, set forth in Section 6.1.3.3.1.2 above, namely in what
         concerns the Parties’ not being close competitors, the results of the market
         investigation – in which the majority of respondents confirmed their replies would
         be applicable to Liechtenstein –,(383) the existence of a dynamic competitive
         market with a multitude of players and the high competitive pressure from global
         streaming platforms, is applicable to Liechtenstein. In what concerns the potential
         affected segment for SVoD-pay-TV sports, the market investigation results
         presented above in relation to Austria (Section 6.1.3.3.3) are also applicable to
         Liechtenstein.
(357)    Fourth, respondents to the market investigation have not raised specific concerns
         with regards to the impact of the Transaction in Liechtenstein. To the extend the
         concerns raised by some competitors (see Section 6.1.3.3.1.2) would also extend
         to Liechtenstein, the Commission’s assessment in that section is also applicable.
(358)    Accordingly, the Commission considers that the Transaction will not lead to an
         increase in buyer power by the merged entity, resulting in a significant
         impediment to effective competition in Liechtenstein.

        6.1.3.3.5.        Luxembourg

6.1.3.3.5.1.     Notifying Party’s views
(360)    The Notifying Party notes that the combination of the Parties’ activities in the
         potential market segments for the acquisition of sports, football, other sports,
         regular non-premium sports, as well as for the exhibition in SVoD/pay-TV, in
         Luxembourg does not pose a risk to effective competition. (384)
(361)    The Notifying Party submits that the high combined market shares in
         Luxembourg relate to Sky DACH sports rights, as RTL’s share leads to a
         negligible increment (and mainly refers to [business/commercial information of
         RTL] rights, and some lower tier sports). (385) The Notifying Party clarifies that it
         is not aware of any players specifically competing for German-language media
         rights in Luxembourg (these are usually “appendices” to the rights acquired for
         the larger German-speaking audiences in Germany and Austria). Since the Parties
         do not know in all instances whether their competitors in, for example, Germany
         and Austria also purchased media rights including Luxembourg, the Notifying
         Party considers Sky DACH’s market share is likely an overestimation. (386)
(362)    The Notifying Party’s arguments with regards to the existence of a dynamic
         competitive market for sports content, with a multitude of players, the increasing
         importance of global streamers, as well as the Parties not being close competitors,
         also apply to Luxembourg. (387)

6.1.4.2. Market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels

        6.1.4.2.1.      Germany
(382)    Based on the market shares provided in Section 6.1.4.1, the Notifying Party has
         identified one potentially horizontally affected market in Germany related to the
         wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels, namely the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content.

6.1.4.2.1.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(383)    The Notifying Party submits that the horizontal overlap between RTL and Sky
         DACH on the potential market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV
         channels for general entertainment content will not lead to any competition
         concerns in Germany for the following reasons.
(384)    First, the Notifying Party recalls that they do not agree with the scope of the
         relevant product market, segmenting the market for the wholesale supply of TV
         channels by genre, as competitive conditions do not differ for different
         genres. (399)
(385)    Second, the Notifying Party submits that every wholesale supplier of premium
         pay-TV channels offers a general entertainment channels. (400)
(386)    Third, significant competitors will remain post-Transaction on all mentioned
         affected markets. These competitors include at least P7S1, Warner, Hearst (incl.
         A&E), High View, which supply TV channels comparable to the Parties. (401)
(387)    Fourth, the Notifying Party submits that the Parties’ combined share is
         decreasing in the market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels
         carrying general entertainment content. (402)
(388)    Fifth, the Parties’ market shares overstate their competitive position. The
         Notifying Party submits there is a convergence of linear and non-linear media
         consumption, as a result of which pay-TV channels compete with VoD services
         also at the wholesale level. Distributors of AV channels not only purchase linear
         pay-TV channels but also offer access to different VoD services through their
         platform. (403)
(389)    Sixth, Sky DACH’s activities in the wholesale supply of TV channels is
         [business/commercial information of Sky DACH] limited and commercially
         selective [business/commercial information of Sky DACH] This approach is not
         expected to change post-Transaction. (404)

6.1.4.2.1.2.    Commission assessment
(390)    The Commission considers that, for the reasons set out below, the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal effects in the market for the wholesale supply of premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content.


(399)   Form CO, paras 928, 951 and 967.
(400)   Form CO, para. 928 and 951.
(401)   Form CO, para. 929 and 951.
(402)   Form CO, para. 928 and 951.
(403)   Form CO, para. 930 and 951.
(404)   Form CO, paras 931 and 951.
                                             99
(391)    First, the Parties’ combined share on the market for the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content remains
         moderate, at [20-30]% in 2024. (405)
(392)    In addition, the Parties’ combined share has seen a decreasing trend in the last
         three years (decrease from [20-30]% in 2022 to [20-30]% in 2024).
(393)    Second, a sufficient number of competitors will remain post-Transaction.
(394)    The merged entity will continue to compete with Warner which would have a
         much stronger market position (with a market share exceeding [40-50]% in 2024
         as well as other competitors including A&E ([5-10]%), Disney ([10-20]%), High
         View ([0-5]%) and P7S1 ([5-10]%) with relatively stable market shares in the last
         3 years.
(395)    This is confirmed by the results of the Commission’s market investigation, in
         which the majority of respondents that expressed a view indicated that there are
         sufficient alternative companies active in the market for the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in
         Germany. (406)
(396)    Third, RTL and Sky DACH are not close competitors.
(397)    RTL operates and offers at wholesale level only four premium pay-TV channels
         (RTL Crime, RTL Passion, RTL Living and GEO TV), two of which (RTL
         Passion and RTL Living) specifically attracting a female audience. In contrast,
         Sky DACH’s premium pay-TV channels (Sky One, Sky Sport Kompakt, 13th
         Street, Universal TV and SyFy) focus on sports and general entertainment
         (movies and series) and its typical pay-TV audience is predominantly male. (407)
         Specifically, Sky DACH’s viewership share is highest in the M14-29 segment
         (males aged between 14 to 29) whereas RTL’s viewership share is highest in the
         F14-59 segment (female aged between 14 and 59). (408) The genre and target
         audience of the Parties’ premium pay-TV channels are therefore different.
(398)    This is also confirmed by the results of the market investigation, in which the
         majority of respondents that expressed a view confirmed that RTL and Sky
         DACH are not close competitors for the wholesale supply of paid TV channels or
         any of the potential sub-segments, including the wholesale supply of premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content, in Germany.(409)
         Instead, market participants referred to Warner, P7S1, Disney and Viacom as
         closest competitors to both RTL and Sky DACH. (410)
(399)    In support of market participants’ submissions that the Parties are not close
         competitors, respondents explained, for example, that “Their offers do not
         compete closely but they compete […]” (411) and “Sky focuses more on providing
         channels catered for a more affluent audience than RTL, although distributor

                                                 100
         might still see it in their interest to carry both to cater to a wide set of
         customers.” (412) One respondent explained that, even though, RTL and Sky
         DACH have previously not been close competitors – they have become closer
         competitors over time but it is unclear how their positioning will shift in future:
         “In the past, the two parties addressed more clearly distinct target groups.
         However, due to the broader transformation of the TV market in recent years,
         their audiences have become increasingly comparable. It remains an open
         question whether this convergence will continue or even accelerate in the
         future”. (413)
(400)    Furthermore, for those competitors identified by market participants as close
         competitors to the Parties, and as set out in Section 6.1.4.1, markets shares have
         been stable, if not growing, in the last three years in Germany.
(401)    Fourth, while the majority of respondents to the Commission’s market
         investigation consider there are high barriers to entry for the wholesale supply of
         TV channels (and any of its potential sub-segments, including the wholesale
         supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content) in
         Germany, (414) the main barrier cited by market participants relates to the fact that
         the market for the wholesale supply of TV channels is saturated and characterised
         by strong market players. (415) However, the Commission considers that such
         barrier would rather reflect the current competitive nature of the market.
(402)    Other barriers cited by multiple market participants are regulatory and
         administrative barriers and the high costs for the investment necessary to
         accumulate sufficient AV content and channels. As regards regulatory and
         administrative barriers – those market participants alleging the existence of such
         barriers do not provide any arguments to substantiate their response. (416) In
         contrast, several market participants explained why there would be no high
         regulatory and administrative barriers. For example, one respondent to the market
         investigation submitted that “[t]here are no significant barriers to entry […]
         regulatory requirements for supplying linear or non-linear channels are low, and
         new providers – including thematic channels, FAST operators and OTT players
         – have been able to enter the market without difficulty.” (417) Another respondent
         explained that “new players would need to obtain a broadcast license to run a TV
         channel in Germany (which, however, does not take a significant time to obtain
         (approx. 6 months) and is not too costly (approx. EUR 10,000)”. (418)
(403)    Fifth, AV retailers seeking to acquire TV channels are able to switch between
         wholesale suppliers of TV channels. The majority of market respondents that

         expressed a view consider that switching from one TV channel supplier to another

(404)    A few of the respondents to the Commission’s market investigation indicated that,
         while technically possible, switching does not make sense commercially due to
         the limited number of alternatives to RTL and Sky DACH’s respective TV
         channels. (420) However, and as confirmed above, the Parties would not be close
         competitors and there remain sufficient alternatives on the markets for the
         wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment
         content post-Transaction.
(405)    Sixth, the majority of market participants that expressed a view indicated that the
         Transaction would have a neutral or positive impact on the market for the
         wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment
         content in Germany. (421)

6.1.4.2.1.3.    Conclusion
(406)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal effects in the markets for the wholesale supply of premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content.

(408)    The Notifying Party submits that the horizontal overlap between RTL and Sky
         DACH on the potential market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV
         channels for general entertainment content will not lead to any competition
         concerns in Austria for the same reasons as in Germany. (422)
(409)    First, the Notifying Party recalls that they do not agree with the scope of the

         genres. (423)
(410)    Second, the Notifying Party submits that every wholesale supplier of premium
         pay-TV channels offers a general entertainment channel. (424)
(411)    Third, significant competitors will remain post-Transaction on all affected
         markets identified in this Section. These competitors include at least P7S1,

         decreasing when looking at the market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-

(413)    Fifth, the Parties’ market shares overstate their competitive position. The
         Notifying Party submits there is a convergence of linear and non-linear media

6.1.4.2.2.2.    Commission assessment
(415)    The Commission considers that, for the reasons set out below, the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal effects in the potential market for the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels for general entertainment content, in Austria.
(416)    First, the Parties’ combined share on the market for the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content remains
         relatively moderate, at [20-30]% in 2024 (429)
(417)    Moreover, the Parties’ combined shares are steady in the last three years(the
         Parties’ combined share was [20-30]%,in 2022 and [20-30]% in 2024).
(418)    Second, a sufficient number of competitors will remain post-Transaction to exert
         competitive pressure on the merged entity.
(419)    The merged entity will face Warner which would have a much stronger market
         position (with a market share exceeding [40-50]% in 2024) as well Disney
         ([10-20]%), A&E ([5-10]%) and P7S1 ([5-10]%).
(420)    This is confirmed also by the results of the Commission’s market investigation,
         in which the majority of respondents that expressed a view indicated that there
         are sufficient alternative companies active in the market for the wholesale supply
         of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in
         Austria. (430)
(421)    Third, RTL and Sky DACH are not close competitors.
(422)    RTL operates and offers at wholesale level only four premium pay-TV channels
         (RTL Crime, RTL Passion, RTL Living and GEO TV), two of which (RTL
         Passion and RTL Living) specifically attracting a female audience. In contrast,

         Sky DACH’s premium pay-TV channels (Sky One, Sky Sport Kompakt, 13th
         Street, Universal TV and SyFY) focus on sports and general entertainment and its
         typical pay-TV audience is predominantly male. (431) Specifically, Sky DACH’s
         viewership share is highest in the M14-29 segment (males aged between 14 to 29)
         whereas RTL’s viewership share is highest in the F14-59 segment (female aged
         between 14 and 59). (432) The genre and target audience of the Parties’ premium
         pay-TV channels are therefore different.
(423)    This is also confirmed by the results of the market investigation, in which the
         majority of respondents that expressed a view confirmed that RTL and Sky
         DACH are not close competitors for the wholesale supply of paid TV channels or
         any of the potential sub-segments, including the wholesale supply of premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in Austria.(433) Instead,
         market participants referred to Warner, P7S1, Disney and Viacom as closest
         competitors to both RTL and Sky DACH for all mentioned affected markets. (434)
(424)    Furthermore, for those competitors identified by market participants as close
         competitors to the Parties, and as set out in in Section 6.1.4.1, markets shares have
         been relatively stable, if not growing, in the last three years in all mentioned
         affected markets in Austria.
(425)    Fourth, while the majority of respondents to the Commission’s market
         investigation consider there are high barriers to entry for the wholesale supply of
         TV channels (and any of its potential sub-segments) in Austria, (435) the only
         substantiated response claiming high barriers related to Austria specifically rather
         highlights the competitive market dynamics in Austria. Specifically, the relevant
         market respondent submits that “there are notable barriers to entry in the market
         for wholesale supply of TV-signal as the Austrian TV landscape seems very
         complete already and strong negotiating power of channels and strong
         competition between traditional providers and international platforms.” (436)
(426)    Fifth, AV retailers seeking to acquire TV channels are able to switch between
         wholesale suppliers of TV channels. The majority of market respondents that
         expressed a view consider that switching from one TV channel supplier to another
         is a real option albeit technically complex and requiring some time. (437) The only
         substantiated response relating to Austria specifically claims that “the switch from
         one TV channel supplier to another is not complex technically but often doesn't
         make sense commercially, as there are few alternatives.” (438) However, as
         confirmed above, the Parties would not be close competitors and there remain
         sufficient alternatives on all mentioned affected markets in Austria post-
         Transaction.

6.1.4.2.2.3.    Conclusion
(428)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in the market for the wholesale supply
         of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in Austria.

        6.1.4.2.3.      German-speaking territories
(429)    Based on the market shares provided in Section 6.1.4.1, the Notifying Party has
         identified one potentially horizontally affected market in the German-speaking
         territories related to the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels, namely
         the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying general
         entertainment content.

6.1.4.2.3.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(430)    The Notifying Party submits that the horizontal overlap between RTL and Sky
         DACH on the potential market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV
         channels for general entertainment content will not lead to any competition
         concerns in the EU’s German-speaking territories. As the national markets in
         Germany and Austria represent the majority of the German-speaking territories in
         the EEA, the same arguments apply for the German-speaking territories as those
         for Germany and Austria separately. (440)

6.1.4.2.3.2.    Commission assessment
(431)    The Commission considers that, for the reasons set out below, the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal effects in the potential market for the wholesale supply of
         premium pay-TV channels for general entertainment content in the EU’s German-
         speaking territories.
(432)    First, the Parties’ combined share remains relatively moderate, with [20-30]% on
         the market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying
         general entertainment content.
(433)    Second, as the Transaction gives rise to the same affected market segment as in
         Germany and Austria, the Commission’s assessment with regards to Germany and
         Austria, set forth in Sections 6.1.4.2.1 and 6.1.4.2.2 above, namely in what
         concerns the Parties’ not being close competitors, the existence of sufficient
         alternatives, the ability of AV retailers seeking to acquire TV channels to switch
         between wholesale supplier of TV channels and the results of the market
         investigation – in which the majority of respondents confirmed their replies would




(439)   Replies to Questionnaire “04_Wholesale and Retail TV channels/AV services (Phase 1)”, questions
        E.2 and E.3.
(440)   Form CO, para. 951.
                                                 105
         be applicable to all German-speaking territories, (441) is fully applicable to the
         German-speaking territories.
(434)    Accordingly, the Commission considers that the Transaction will not raise serious
         doubts in the Germany-speaking territories.

6.1.4.2.3.3.    Conclusion
(435)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in the market for the wholesale supply
         of premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in the EU’s
         German-speaking territories.

6.1.4.3. Market for the wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels in
         Austria
(436)    Based on the market shares provided in Section 6.1.4.1, the Notifying Party has
         identified two potentially affected market for the wholesale supply of basic and
         premium pay-TV channels in Austria, namely the wholesale supply of basic and
         premium pay-TV channels overall and the wholesale supply of basic and premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content.

        6.1.4.3.1.      Notifying Party’s views
(437)    The Notifying Party submits that the horizontal overlap between RTL and Sky
         DACH on the potential market for the wholesale supply of basic and premium
         pay-TV channels, including the potential sub-segment for general entertainment
         content, will not lead to any competition concerns in Austria for the following
         reasons.
(438)    First, the Notifying Party recalls that it does not agree with the scope of the
         relevant product market, segmenting the market for the wholesale supply of TV
         channels by genre. (442)
(439)    Second, the Parties’ combined share remains low on any potentially affected
         market.
(440)    Third, the merged entity will continue to compete with significant competitors
         post-Transaction, including at least P7S1, RTLZWEI, Warner and public
         broadcasters. Each of these supply several general entertainment channels. (443)
(441)    Fourth, the Parties’ market shares overstate their competitive position. The
         Notifying Party submits there is a convergence of linear and non-linear media
         consumption, as a result of which pay-TV channels compete with VoD services
         also at the wholesale level. Distributors of AV channels not only purchase linear
         pay-TV channels but also offer access to different VoD services through their
         platform. (444)




(441)   Replies to Questionnaire “04_Wholesale and Retail TV channels/AV services (Phase 1)”, questions
        D.A.C.6 and D.A.C.11.
(442)   Form CO, para. 961.
(443)   Form CO, para. 961.
(444)   Form CO, para. 961.
                                                 106
        6.1.4.3.2.      Commission assessment
(442)    The Commission considers that, for the reasons set out below, the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal effects in the markets for the wholesale supply of basic
         and premium pay-TV channels and the wholesale supply of basic and premium
         pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in Austria.
(443)    The Commission notes that Parties’ activities do not overlap as regards the
         wholesale supply of basic TV channels because only RTL, and not Sky DACH,
         is active in this market segment. Therefore, any change on the market resulting
         from the Transaction in Austria is driven by Sky DACH’s presence in the market
         for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels.
(444)    For the market for d the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels carrying
         general entertainment content, the Commission has already assessed and found
         that the Transaction would not give rise to serious doubts in Austria. As any
         changes resulting from the Transaction in Austria on the broader market for the
         wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels carrying general
         entertainment content are limited only to Sky DACH’s activities in the wholesale
         supply of premium pay-TV channels, the Commission’s assessment with regards
         to the market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels, as set forth
         in Section 6.1.4.2.2, namely in what concerns closeness of competition, the
         existence of sufficient alternatives and the ability of AV retailers seeking to
         acquire TV channels to switch between wholesale suppliers of TV channels, is
         fully applicable also to the market for the wholesale supply of basic and premium
         TV channels.
(445)    The only potential market segment which has not been assessed in
         Section 6.1.4.2.2 above, but where the Transaction would give rise to an affected
         market when conducting the assessment on a market combining the wholesale
         supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels in Austria, is the market for the
         wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels overall.
(446)    Also on such market, the Commission does not consider the Transaction would
         give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market, for the
         following reasons.
(447)    First, the Parties’ combined shares remain relatively moderate and the increment
         resulting from the Transaction is small.
(448)    The Parties’ combined share on the market for the wholesale supply of basic and
         premium pay-TV channels in Austria in 2024 was [20-30]% and the increment
         resulting from the Transaction is [5-10]%.
(449)    Furthermore, and as set out in Table 19 above, the Parties’ combined shares and
         the increments have both been decreasing in the last three years.
(450)    Second, the merged entity would continue to face a sufficient number of
         competitors post-Transaction.
(451)    In particular, the merged entity will continue to face P7S1 ([20-30]%) as well as
         Warner ([10-20]%), Disney ([0-5]%) and RTL2 ([5-10]%). (445)
(452)    This is also confirmed by the results of the Commission’s market investigation.
         The majority of respondents that expressed a view indicated that there are

(445)   See Table 19 above, based on Tables 111-113 and 117 Form CO and Annex 13b to the Form CO.
                                               107
         sufficient alternative suppliers in Austria for the wholesale supply of linear TV
         channels, including both basic and premium pay-TV channels. (446)
(453)    Third, RTL and Sky DACH would not be close competitors on the market for the
         wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels.
(454)    In the first place, RTL focuses mainly on the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV
         channels in Austria as opposed to the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV
         channels. In 2024, RTL’s revenues derived from the wholesale supply of basic
         pay-TV channels in Austria amounted to EUR […] million, compared to EUR
         […] million derived from the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels. In
         contrast, and as also set out above, Sky DACH is not active in the wholesale
         supply of basic pay-TV channels, deriving all of its revenues as regards the
         wholesale supply of TV channels in Austria from premium pay-TV channels. (447)
(455)    In the second place, the Commission’s assessment as regards closeness of
         competition between the Parties with respect to premium pay-TV channels, in
         particular having regard to the different genre and target audiences of the Parties’
         respective premium pay-TV channels, as set out in Section 6.1.4.2.2 above, also
         applies fully here.
(456)    The lack of closeness is also confirmed by the results of the market investigation
         as, with reference to Section 6.1.4.2.2 above, the majority of respondents that
         expressed a view confirmed that RTL and Sky DACH are not close competitors
         for the wholesale supply of paid TV channels or any of the potential sub-
         segments, including the wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV channels,
         in Austria.(448) Instead, market participants referred to Warner, P7S1, Disney and
         Viacom as closest competitors to both RTL and Sky DACH for all mentioned
         affected markets. (449)
(457)    Fourth, AV retailers seeking to acquire basic and premium pay-TV channels are
         able to switch between wholesale suppliers of TV channels. The majority of
         market respondents that expressed a view consider that switching from one TV
         channel supplier to another is a real option albeit technically complex and
         requiring some time. (450)
(458)    Fifth, the majority of market participants that expressed a view consider that the
         Transaction would have a positive or neutral impact on the market for the
         wholesale supply of basic and premium pay-TV in Austria.

        6.1.4.3.3.      Conclusion
(459)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in the markets for the wholesale supply
         of basic and premium pay-TV channels and the wholesale supply of basic and
         premium pay-TV channels carrying general entertainment content in Austria.

6.1.5.   Market for the supply of advertising space in AV content
(460)    The Parties’ activities overlap in the market for the supply of advertising space in
         AV content (in TV, SAVoD and online video advertising) in Germany and
         Austria. The Transaction gives rise to affected markets/segments in Germany
         (when considered separately) and in Germany and Austria (combined).

6.1.5.1. Affected markets and market shares
(461)    The Transaction gives rise to horizontally affected markets for the supply of
         advertising space in AV content (and segment thereof) in Germany (when
         considered separately) and in Germany and Austria (combined).
(462)    The Parties sell their own advertising space to media buying agencies or
         advertisers. The Parties’ ad sales houses (through their sales houses AdAlliance,
         RaDA and Sky Media) also sell advertising space of third-party content owners.
         The Commission assessed the Parties’ market shares in the supply of advertising
         space as content owners which reflect the value of the Parties’ own advertising
         inventory. Further, the Commission assessed the Parties’ market shares at the
         level of their ad sales houses as these reflect the value of the intermediation
         service provided by the Parties to third parties.

6.1.5.2. Germany

        6.1.5.2.1.       Unilateral effects

6.1.5.2.1.1.    Notifying Party’s views
         All AV advertising, TV and SAVoD advertising or TV advertising
(467)    The Notifying Party submits that on an “all-AV advertising” market
         encompassing the supply of TV advertising, SAVoD advertising and online video
         advertising, the Parties’ combined market share is below [20-30]% (451). The
         Notifying Party considers that this potential market reflects market reality where
         there is already a strong substitution between advertising on TV and on streaming
         services such as Amazon Prime Video, Netflix and Disney + which are
         increasingly switching their SVoD offering into SAVoD offerings.
(468)    The Notifying Party also submits that, due to the mentioned market dynamics, the
         Parties’ market shares are decreasing. In a market for the supply of TV and
         SAVoD (where the Parties’ market share is [40-50]% (452)), the Parties estimate
         their combined market share decreased to [30-40]% in 2025 and will further
         decrease to [30-40]% in 2026. (453) In a market for the supply of TV advertising
         (including both linear FTA and pay-TV, where the Parties’ combined market
         share is [40-50]%), RTL’s market share decrease in TV advertising sales in 2025
         compared to 2024 ([0-5]%) is larger than Sky DACH’s annual TV advertising
         sales. The Parties expect this decrease trend to accelerate in the coming years. (454)
(469)    The Notifying Party submits that, other than in artificial market segmentations
         that do not reflect market reality, the increment resulting from the Transaction
         would be small or limited (approx. [0-5]% in the market for the supply of TV and
         SAVoD advertising and the market for the supply of TV advertising). (455)
         Further, Sky DACH will foreseeably lose some of its market share due to
         [information about Sky DACH’s activity in the market]. (456) The Notifying Party
         underlines that Sky DACH’s attractiveness for advertisers is also greatly
         dependent on its sports content, in particular the rights for the Bundesliga, which
         are sold by the rights owner in competitive tenders. In case another competitor
         was to acquire the Bundesliga media rights in the next tender, advertisers would
         switch to such competitor and Sky DACH’s market share would decrease even
         further (to less than [0-5]% in TV advertising). (457)
(470)    The Notifying Party submits that sufficient credible alternatives will remain in the
         market. In a market for the supply of TV and SAVoD advertising, alternatives
         include P7S1 ([30-40]%), public broadcasters ([5-10]%), Amazon ([0-5]%) and

(451)   The Parties’ combined share on such overall all-AV advertising, as proposed by the Notifying Party,
        would remain below [5-10]% in Austria. Form CO, para. 1485 and Tables 178 and 197. Unless
        otherwise specified, the market shares referenced in this section refer to the year 2024.
(452)   The Notifying Party considers that long-format YouTube video inventory should also be included
        in this market which would decrease the Parties’ combined market share to [30-40]%. Form CO,
        para. 1490.
(453)   Form CO, paras 1485-1488.
(454)   Form CO, paras 47 and 1491.
(455)   Form CO, paras 1486 and 1488.
(456)   Form CO, para. 1501. Currently, ad inventory resulting from broadcasting Warner content
        corresponds to approx. […]% of Sky DACH’s ad sales. Warner launched HBO Max in Germany in
        2026, as a result of which Sky DACH will lose such advertising revenue.
(457)   Form CO, paras 49, 1493, 1494, 1500. The Notifying Party submits that […]% of Sky DACH’s TV
        ad sales are Bundesliga-related and […]% of Sky DACH’s TV ad sales are sports-related.
                                                   118
         other national TV broadcasters such as RTL2, Warner, Visoon Media and
         Paramount add up to around [5-10]% of the market. In a market for TV advertising
         (or segments thereof), alternatives include P7S1, El Cartel Brothers (RTL2 and
         Warner), Visoon Media, ARD and ZDF. In a market for AVoD advertising (and
         segments thereof), alternatives include Amazon Prime Video, Netflix, Disney +,
         Joyn, Paramount+, HBO Max, DAZN and DYN. (458)
(471)    The Notifying Party considers that the Parties are not close competitors (in a
         market for TV and SAVoD advertising or TV advertising), as they focus on
         different AV content and therefore have different segments of viewers which in
         turn attracts different types of advertisers. Sky DACH earns […]% of its TV ad
         sales from sales of sports ad inventory, compared to […]% for RTL (whose focus
         is on fiction and non-fiction content). (459) In term of viewers, RTL can deliver
         reach in broad target groups and Sky DACH is a niche player that can reach
         narrower (mostly male) target groups. (460) With regards to type of advertisers,
         while Sky DACH has significant sales to bookmakers, RTL advertises mostly
         fast-moving consumer goods (“FMCG”), financial services, telecommunications,
         etc. (461) The Notifying Party also notes that the majority of RTL’s ad sales are
         made via media buying agencies whereas Sky DACH sells its ad space directly to
         customers. (462) Further, [business/commercial information of both Parties]. (463)
(472)    The Notifying Party submits that customers have countervailing buying power. A
         small number of large and sophisticated global media buying agencies administer
         the advertising budget of hundreds if not thousands of advertisers/customers and
         negotiate with TV broadcasters/publishers on their behalf. Whilst the importance
         of TV has been decreasing in the past years, the buyer power of the media buying
         agencies has grown. Further, media buying agencies and advertisers are agnostic
         to the type of media across which ads are placed, so long as they reach their target
         audience. Therefore, media buying agencies are indifferent about significant
         budget shifts from TV to other AV advertising segments. (464)
(473)    Finally, the Notifying Party submits that market entry is not only likely but
         currently already taking place. Established streaming services like Amazon,


(458)   Form CO, paras 1486 and 1530.
(459)   Form CO, paras 1504-1506.
(460)   Form CO, paras 50, 1514-1519 and 1524-1526. The Notifying Party submits that, while [60-70]%
        of Sky DACH’s advertiser customers in Germany are also RTL advertiser customers, several other
        competitors in the AV content retail market have much higher overlaps (e.g., Walt Disney:
        [90-100]%, RTL2/El Cartel: [80-90]%, ARD: [80-90]%, Visoon: [80-90]%, or ZDF: [70-80]%).
        The Notifying Party also submits an economic analysis by RBB Economics that finds that [reference
        to documents submitted by the Parties] of RTL’s advertisers regard Sky DACH as their next best
        alternative. The closest competitors of RTL in TV advertising are [reference to documents
        submitted by the Parties]. When including SAVoD advertising, the closest competitors to RTL are
        [reference to documents submitted by the Parties]. As for Sky DACH, the closest competitor in the
        market for pay-TV advertising is [reference to documents submitted by the Parties].

         Netflix or Disney are increasingly switching their SVoD offering into
         SAVoD. Market entry from both Paramount and HBO Max has occurred in 2025
         and 2026 respectively. DF1, AWE and Hohenrausch are relevant market entrants
         in TV channels. (465)
         FTA and pay-TV advertising
(474)    The Notifying Party submits that the Parties are not close competitors in the
         potential market for pay-TV advertising. Whereas RTL sells its ad breaks to
         advertisers seeking broad reach, Sky DACH relies strongly on advertisers from
         the betting industry and seeking contacts in more male target group paying above-
         average prices. The Notifying Party also submits that the closest competitor of
         Sky DACH in the market for pay-TV advertising is Warner whereas the closest
         competitors to RTL are P7S1, RTL2 and Warner. The overlap of customers
         between the Parties in this potential market is also limited. Further, the Notifying
         Party submits that, besides the fact that media buying agencies have
         countervailing buyer power, leading suppliers like Amazon, Netflix and Disney
         switched all or parts of their offerings into an ad-supported business model which
         out competitive pressure on the Parties. (466)
(475)    The Notifying Party also submits that, in FTA advertising, Sky DACH’s market
         share is negligible and results from the sales of third-party inventory of a limited
         number of advertisers. Mostly, these activities related to the inventory of one
         operator of small FTA channels for music videos with which Sky has an
         agreement of short duration ([business/commercial information of Sky]) and if
         such agreements were to be terminated or expired, Sky would not have a market
         share in FTA advertising. (467)
         Direct sales and intermediation
(476)    The Notifying Party submits that intermediation of SAVoD advertising has not
         yet developed any meaningful volumes. RTL’s market share in the potential
         market segment for TV and SAVoD advertising (intermediation) and SAVoD
         advertising (intermediation) both for mobile and desktops currently only results
         from [business/commercial information of RTL]. Both represent very small
         volumes (468) and have no impact on the competitive landscape given steeply
         increasing direct sales of Amazon, Netflix, Disney and other SAVoD services, as
         well as the fact that competition from TV advertising is a direct substitute for
         SAVoD advertising. In addition, Sky DACH has a limited business of
         intermediates sales for third parties and the increment brought about by the
         Transaction is small (not exceeding [0-10]%) except in a market for
         intermediation in TV and SAVoD advertising. (469)

         does not raise serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a
         result of horizontal unilateral effects in the market for the supply of AV

6.1.5.2.1.2.1. Markets for the supply of TV advertising, pay-TV advertising, direct sales
               of TV advertising, direct sales of pay-TV advertising, TV and SAVoD
               advertising and direct sales of TV and SAVoD advertising
(478)    As described, the Commission will, in this Section, assess the potentially affected
         markets, looking at the supply of TV and/or SAVoD advertising space overall
         (i.e., including both direct and intermediated sales) and at the supply of TV and/or
         SAVoD advertising space via direct sales only. Specifically, in Germany the
         Transaction gives rise to potentially affected markets for the supply of: (i) TV
         advertising space (i.e., including both direct and intermediated sales), (ii) TV
         advertising space sold via direct sales, (iii) pay-TV advertising space
         (i.e., including both direct and intermediated sales), (iv) pay-TV advertising space
         sold via direct sales, (v) TV and SAVoD advertising space (i.e., including both
         direct and intermediated sales), and (vi) TV and SAVoD advertising space sold
         via direct sales. (471)
(479)    At the outset, the Commission notes that for the mentioned affected markets, the
         size of markets as well as the Parties’ activities are driven by the supply of TV
         advertising space. In Germany, the market size for the supply of TV advertising
         amounts to EUR […] million whereas the market size for the supply of SAVoD
         advertising amounts to EUR […] million in 2024. Similarly, RTL’s 2024
         revenues amounted to EUR […] million for TV advertising compared to EUR
         […] million for SAVoD advertising and Sky DACH’s 2024 revenues amounted
         to EUR […] million for TV advertising compared to EUR […] million for
         SAVoD advertising. (472)

         advertising space via direct sales, [40-50]% for the supply of TV and SAVoD

(490)    In the second place, switching does not involve big costs on the side of the
         advertisers. A large majority of respondents to the Commission’s market
         investigation that expressed a view confirmed that switching from one supplier of
         advertising space to another is quick and easy for all mentioned affected
         markets. (487) For example, one market participant explained that only
         cancellation deadlines need to be respected (488), and another clarified that “For
         the linear TV sector, there are annual commitments with several marketers. These
         remain valid throughout the year. Exceptions are subsequent additional
         investments, which are renegotiated each time.” (489) Another market participant
         explained that “We choose and switch depending on the rights and adequate
         opportunities for our campaigns.” (490) and yet another noted “We would have
         multiple suppliers in our mix in a given year (based on deals & campaign
         requirements) and would re-evaluate the mix on regular basis - usually every
         year”. (491)
(491)    The fact that switching is easy is supported also by the fact that the vast majority
         of advertisers responding to the Commission’s market investigation confirmed
         they do switch in practice between different suppliers of advertising space for
         advertising space on both TV channels and SAVoD services. (492) While this
         applies to all mentioned affected markets, certain market participants noted
         greater difficulties in switching as regards pay-TV advertising; for example,
         noting that alternatives to Sky DACH are limited. (493). (494) The Commission also
         points to the fact that sufficient alternative suppliers will remain post-Transaction,
         including for the supply of advertising space on pay-TV channels, as confirmed
         in this Section.

(492)    Fourth, as regards the markets for the supply of TV advertising space (and its
         sub-segments), the merged entity will not only face competition from competing
         suppliers on those mentioned affected markets, but also advertising on SAVoD
         platforms and advertising in other online videos will exercise some form of out-
         of-market constraint on the merged entity.
(493)    In the first place, while the Parties’ internal documents show that TV advertising
         remains crucial, (495) sales in the market for the supply of TV advertising space
         are decreasing or, at best, stagnated, including in Austria and Germany. (496)
(494)    This decrease in TV advertising spend was also confirmed by all respondents to
         the Commission’s market investigation who expressed a view. (497)
(495)    In the second place, digital players including SAVoD players like Netflix and
         Amazon, but also certain online video services, are increasingly competing for
         the same advertising budgets as TV networks as these follow consumers’ shift
         online. (498)
(496)    This shift in spending from TV advertising towards SAVoD or social media was
         also confirmed by the results of the Commission’s market investigation. In
         Germany, a majority of respondents who expressed a view indicated that ad spend
         previously allocated to linear TV advertising has been reallocated either to
         SAVoD, to online video advertising (e.g., video advertisements displayed online
         for example on YouTube or other online websites) or to both. A few respondents
         also indicated that ad spending previously allocated to linear TV advertising is
         now reallocated to social media. In Austria, the majority of respondents who
         expressed a view considered that such ad spending has been reallocated to online
         video advertising. (499) In their explanations, several respondents indicate that
         advertising revenues in formats other than TV are likely to continue
         increasing. (500)




(495)   For example, a publication on RTL’s own website states the following: “For 66 per cent of
        Europeans, brands appearing in ads on TV for the first time make them feel or appear more
        trustworthy. TV is considered to provide the most credible media environment with the least amount
        of misinformation and highest level of authenticity. […] In order for a brand to gain trust, it must
        be famous, which means being known by people. And linear TV continues to bring the highest reach
        in combination with time spent, as individuals worldwide spend 2 hours 19 minutes per day
        watching linear TV. The consumption and reach of linear TV are high throughout Europe. […] Ads
        seen within professionally produced content are considered 44 per cent more trustworthy than those
        in non-professional content. TV creates the highest level of trust out of all media […]” (emphasis
        added) available at https://rtl-adalliance.com/article/rtl-adalliances-tv-key-facts-2025-highlights-
        broadcasters-vital-role-shaping-brand-trust, last accessed 1 April 2026.

         away from linear tv to several other digital and non-linear options.” (501) while

         hold significant value, particularly for broad reach campaigns, there are

(498)    This competition constraint posed by advertising space on SAVoD services or
         online video is also evidenced in the Commission’s market investigation. By way
         of example, when asked about potential alternatives to the Parties’ TV advertising
         space, certain market participants consider that SAVoD advertising space can be
         a substitute to both FTA and pay-TV advertising space. (503) Similarly, when
         asked about potential switching by advertisers from the Parties’ TV advertising
         space to YouTube, certain market participants confirmed that they would consider
         doing so. (504)
(499)    In the third place, the out of market constraint from the supply of advertising space
         on SAVoD platforms and in the context of other online videos on the market for
         the supply of TV advertising has the potential to increase in future. (505)
(500)    In particular, SAVoD and online videos still have unused capacity to display ads
         in both Germany and Austria. While advertising activities of private broadcasters
         have legal limitations as regards the total amount of advertising minutes on TV
         channels, the same does not apply to SAVoD services or other services offering
         online video advertising space such as YouTube. On average, private broadcasters
         can allocate about 12 minutes of advertising per hour in Germany and Austria.
         The same legal limitations apply to SAVoD or online video services, only to the
         extent the service contains linear broadcasting, but does not apply to the non-
         linear parts of the SAVoD service. (506)

         much lower than the 12 minutes in linear TV. For example, the average ad load
         for RTL+ in 2025 was [business/commercial information of RTL]. Sky DACH’s

        legislator transposed Art. 23 AMSD in § 70(1) of the Medienstaatsvertrag as follows: (i) between
        6am and 6pm, the proportion of advertising shall not exceed 20 % of that period – as in the AMSD;
        and (ii) the second period has been shortened so that the proportion of advertising spots between
        6pm and 11pm shall not exceed 20 % of that period. The Austrian legislator transposed Art. 23

         Other SAVoD services between had similar ad loads in 2025 in Germany,
         approximately: [information on ad loads of competitors]. (507) In light of this,
         SAVoD platforms such as Amazon, Netflix or Disney, as well as online video
         services such as YouTube seem to be exercising a growing out-of-market

(502)    Fifth, RTL and Sky DACH are not close competitors in the supply of TV
         advertising space.
(503)    In the first place, RTL and Sky DACH focus on different AV content.
         Specifically, Sky DACH earns […]% of its TV ad sales from the sale of sports
         content advertising inventory, which represents only […]% of RTL’s ad sales.
         Conversely, RTL generates approximately […]% of its TV ad sales from fiction
         and non-fiction programming, which represents only […]% of Sky DACH’s ad
         sales. (508)
(504)    In the second place, the result of the Parties’ different content focus is that there
         is also a significant difference in their viewership. Sky DACH is stronger in older
         and male target groups and its viewership share is highest in the M14-29 segment
         (males aged between 14 to 29) whereas RTL’s viewership share is highest in the
         F14-59 segment (female aged between 14 and 59). (509)
(505)    In the third place, the result of the Parties’ different AV content and viewership is
         that there is a strong difference in the type of advertisers purchasing the Parties’
         respective advertising space. Advertisers generally select the most appropriate
         location to place their advertisement based on the audience they aim to reach,
         which is in turn influenced by the AV content shown and the channel on which it
         is available. From the perspective of a media planner, different target groups can
         be found in many places, the task is to achieve the optimal combination of
         channels and AV content to achieve the desired gross and net reach within the
         desired target group at the lowest possible spend. (510) Therefore, the type of
         audience that can be reached via a particular channel or AV content plays a strong
         role in an advertiser’s choice when purchasing specific advertising space.
(506)    For RTL, almost all bookings ([90-100]%) are made by a vast group of advertisers
         of fast-moving consumer goods, financial services, telecommunications, cars and
         other general consumer-related offerings. By contrast, bookings from
         bookmakers play hardly any role (less than [0-5]%). In contrast, […] of the top
         10 advertising customers of Sky DACH are bookmakers which, together with
         teleshopping, amount to approximately […] of Sky DACH’s advertising
         turnover. (511)
(507)    As a result, RTL and Sky DACH would not compete closely for the same
         advertisers.
(508)    In the fourth place, RTL and Sky DACH also differ as regards the route by which
         their advertising space is sold to advertisers. While in Germany RTL sells more

         than […]% of its TV advertising inventory to media buying agencies, the majority
         ([…]%) of Sky DACH’s advertising inventory is sold directly to advertisers. (512)
(509)    In the fifth place, RTL and Sky DACH differ as regards the way in which their
         advertising space is priced. Sky DACH’s Bundesliga inventory, its most
         important inventory in terms of advertising revenue given that […]% of Sky
         DACH TV ad sales in 2024 were directly Bundesliga-related, (513) is priced at least
         […] as high as RTL’s most costly advertising inventory. Specifically, Sky
         DACH’s Bundesliga had an average net CPM in 2024 of EUR […], compared to
         RTL channel with an average net CPM in 2024 of EUR […], VOX channel with
         average net CPM in 2024 of EUR […] and SuperRTL channel with net CPM in
         2024 of EUR […]). (514) Net CPMs for Sky DACH’s Sky Fiction are closer to
         those of RTL’s channels [business/commercial information of RTL], however
         Sky Fiction is not the focus of Sky DACH’s activities in the supply of advertising
         space as this relates to non-sports content whereas sports inventory represents
         nearly […]% of Sky DACH’s advertising revenues. (515)
(510)    In the sixth place, an analysis conducted on behalf of the Parties, confirms that
         the Parties are not close competitors and that the Parties’ advertising inventories
         fulfil complementary roles in TV advertising campaigns. The analysis relies on
         media planner Omnicom’s TV media planning tool, which is used in the ordinary
         course of business to inform advertisers’ budget allocation decisions across TV
         channels so as to maximize reach. This analysis simulates a 10% price increase
         across RTL’s ad inventory and lets the media planning tool re-optimize the budget
         allocation for four advertising campaigns targeting common target groups. (516)
         Following the 10% price increase, [business/commercial information of the
         Parties] which implies that Sky DACH does not exert a significant competitive
         constraint on RTL. [business/commercial information of the Parties] which
         suggests that RTL and Sky DACH have complementary roles in TV advertising
         campaigns. (517)
(511)    In the seventh place, the results of the market investigation did not conclusively
         confirm that RTL and Sky DACH are close competitors. While a majority of
         respondents that expressed a view consider that the Parties focus on different
         customer needs in Germany, nearly the same number of respondents consider that
         they compete closely. (518)
