(512)    Looking at the reasons behind market participants’ responses, the same elements
         are used to support opposite conclusions for several market participants. Certain
         market participants point towards the type of target audiences and reach to support
         the conclusion that RTL and Sky DACH focus on different customer needs, while
         others point to target audiences and reach to support the conclusion that RTL and
         Sky DACH compete closely.
(513)    For example, one market participant explained that “In our view, the Parties are
         not particularly close competitors in the supply of AV advertising space in
         Germany. Their advertising inventories differ significantly in terms of reach,
         content offering and overall portfolio. RTL operates a broad portfolio of free-to-
         air TV channels and digital video services and therefore offers large-scale reach
         across a wide range of audiences and content genres. Sky, by contrast, is
         primarily positioned in premium pay-TV environments and is particularly strong
         in sports content, where it can achieve significant audience reach among specific
         viewer groups. […] From both a viewer and an advertising perspective, the
         offerings of the Parties are therefore often complementary rather than directly
         interchangeable.” (519) Other respondents noted that “While Sky is primarily
         focussing on live football RTL is primary focusing on entertainment (shows,
         movies, TV series etc).”, that “RTL is a mass medium reaching a lot of consumers
         and different target audiences. Sky DACH is […] more niche and can be used
         complemantary to linear TV” or that “As one is PTV and the other FTV they have
         a different focus as well as a different viewership.” (520) However, other market
         participants noted that “Both parties targeting similar audiences: Male and
         Female, Young and Old, around fiction/shows and sport.” while another
         explained that “Both offer premium video inventory with comparable pricing
         structures and target similar advertiser groups seeking high-quality, brand-safe
         environments. As a result, they overlap significantly in terms of audience reach,
         inventory offerings, and the types of clients they attract.” (521)
(514)     In light of the mixed results, the Commission does not consider the results of the
         market investigation conclusive. Nonetheless, taking into account the evidence
         overall, the Commission considers that the Parties are not close competitors.
(515)    Sixth, for the sale of TV advertising space, a portion of the merged entity’s
         customers for TV advertising would be large and sophisticated media agencies
         that could provide at least a certain level of bargaining power.
(516)    The Parties both use media buying agencies to sell their TV advertising inventory.
(517)    RTL relies on media buying agencies, as it sells more than […]% of its advertising
         inventory to or via media agencies. (522) Such media agencies administer the
         media budgets of advertisers, which can involve advising on media strategies,
         recommending budget allocation for media spend as well as engaging in
         negotiations with the relevant content holders to acquiring advertising

         inventory. (523) Sky DACH is less reliant on media buying agencies, as it has
         [business/commercial information of Sky DACH] direct-to-advertiser contacts
         compared to RTL. Nonetheless, […]% of its TV advertising inventory is sold to
         or via media agencies. (524) These media agencies have a certain level of
         bargaining power.
(518)    In the first place, media agencies can in some instances aggregate individual
         advertisers’ demand for advertising space in order to negotiate more favourable
         terms with content owners.
(519)    Media buying agencies can purchase advertising inventory in two ways: either
         they negotiate advertising budgets for individual advertisers, or they engage in
         ‘trading’ practices. Trading refers to the situation where the media agency buys
         inventory at a wholesale price and with no particular advertiser-client in mind, in
         order to sell it on to advertisers at a later stage.
(520)    Such trading practices represent an increasing percentage of the Parties’ ad sales
         revenues. For RTL – advertising revenues derived from trading grew from […]%
         of RTL’s gross booking in 2016 to […]% of in 2024, and from […]% of RTL’s
         net booking in 2016 to […]% in 2024. (525) For Sky DACH – trading sales
         represented […]% of total gross inventory/revenue generated with media buying
         agencies across AV advertising in 2025, and approximately […]% for TV
         advertising. Sky DACH’s TV advertising revenues derived from trading grew
         from […]% in 2022 to […]% in 2025. (526) When asked about trading practices
         in the market investigation, the majority of media agencies that expressed a view
         indicated that media agencies can aggregate the demand of several advertisers and
         as a result are able to buy advertising space from content owners in bulk. (527)
(521)    In response to the question whether media buying agencies are able to obtain
         better terms and conditions from aggregating the demand of several advertisings
         when negotiating with content owners such as the Parties, media agencies
         provided mixed answers. Some indicated that the number of advertisers
         represented by a media buying agency does not have a direct impact on the
         agency’s negotiating power vis-à-vis content owners, as a result of which the
         media agency would be able to negotiate better prices or conditions for its
         clients. (528) Others however agreed that the more advertisers a media agency
         represents the better the terms and conditions that it can obtain from content
         owners for all of its clients. (529) For one market participant, the answer was
         different in Germany as compared to Austria. (530)
(522)    Even if media agencies are not all aligned, the results of the market investigation
         do indicate that negotiating on behalf of a high number of advertisers can give an
         advantage to media buying agencies in their negotiations with content owners.

(523)    In the second place, as evidenced when looking at the Parties’ own data, media
         agencies can obtain higher discounts, and therefore better net prices, if they
         purchase advertising space by “trading” as compared to when they purchase
         advertising space on behalf of individual advertisers.
(524)    The terms at which Sky DACH sells advertising inventory to media agencies via
         “trading” are significantly discounted compared to advertising inventory sold to
         media agencies acting on behalf of an individual advertiser. The “trading” payrate
         stands at about […]% (meaning that the price for inventory is […]% discounted
         compared to initial gross prices) compared to the average […]% (meaning that
         gross prices are only […]% discounted) when Sky DACH’s advertising inventory
         is sold to media agencies not engaged in “trading”. (531)
(525)    A similar situation applies to RTL, where the discounts negotiated by media
         agencies for TV advertising inventory are higher when those media agencies
         engage in “trading” practices (on average […]% discount in 2025) compared to
         when they do not engage in such trading practices(on average […]%
         discount). (532)
(526)    In the third place, looking more broadly at customers to include not only media
         agencies but also individual advertisers, a large majority of market participants
         that expressed a view to the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that
         these would have the bargaining power to react in case of a price increase of the
         Parties’ advertising space. (533)
(527)    For example, one market participant explained that “In the case of linear TV, a
         price increase would lead to an increased migration of advertising investment
         towards digital alternatives.” (534) while another explained that “A price increase
         must be enforceable in the market. If advertisers and/or agencies lack sufficient
         negotiating power to adjust prices, the market offers enough alternative options
         to act accordingly.” (535)
(528)    Some respondents believe that the level of bargaining power may differ between

         agencies than with the advertisers” (536)) or depending on the genre of AV content
         in which advertisers seek to place their ads (e.g., “Difficult in the genre of live
         sports due to limited availability, in other genres easier to negotiate as there are

(529)    In the fourth place, and as referred above, the Commission notes that for the

         SAVoD advertising space.
(531)    Seventh, a majority of market participants that expressed a view indicated that
         the Transaction would have a neutral or positive impact on all mentioned affected
         markets listed at the start of this Section. (539)

(533)    While a number of market participants consider that the Transaction could give
         the merged entity increased market power and hence negotiation leverage vis-à-
         vis advertisers and/or media buying agencies, (546) and with references to its
         assessment above, the Commission considers that there are sufficient alternatives
         on all affected markets and switching remains possible for advertisers and media
         agencies.
(534)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market,
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in the following markets in Germany, or
         in Austria and Germany combined: (i) TV advertising, (ii) pay-TV advertising,
         (iii) direct sales of TV advertising, (iv) direct sales of pay-TV advertising, (v) TV
         and SAVoD advertising, and (vi) direct sales of TV and SAVoD advertising.

6.1.5.2.1.2.2. Markets for the supply of all AV advertising (sold via intermediation), TV
               and SAVoD (sold via intermediation), SAVoD advertising (sold via
               intermediation), SAVoD advertising on desktops (sold via intermediation),
               SAVoD advertising on mobile devices (sold via intermediation), TV
               advertising (sold via intermediation), pay-TV advertising (sold via
               intermediation), FTA advertising (sold via intermediation).
(535)    The Transaction gives rise to affected markets in Germany for the supply of: (i) all
         AV advertising (sold via intermediation), (ii) TV and SAVoD advertising (sold
         via intermediation), (iii) SAVoD advertising (sold via intermediation), (iv)
         SAVoD advertising on desktops (sold via intermediation), and (v) SAVoD
         advertising on mobile devices (sold via intermediation). The Transaction also
         gives rise to affected markets in a market for Austria and Germany combined for
         the supply of: (i) all AV advertising (sold via intermediation), (ii) TV and SAVoD
         advertising (sold via intermediation), (iii) SAVoD advertising (sold via
         intermediation), (iv) SAVoD advertising on desktops (sold via intermediation
         (i) TV advertising (sold via intermediation), (ii) pay-TV advertising (sold via
         intermediation) and (iii) FTA advertising (sold via intermediation). The
         Transaction does not give rise to affected markets as regards intermediated sales
         in a potential geographic market of Austria.
(536)    As regards the supply of TV advertising sold via intermediation, the Commission
         notes that only Sky is active in Germany and only RTL is active in Austria (selling
         Sky’s inventory). However, the Transaction gives rise to affected markets only
         when combining the Parties’ shares on the market for the supply of TV advertising
         sold via intermediation (and any subsegment thereof) on a market for Germany
         and Austria combined.

(537)    As regards the supply of SAVoD advertising sold via intermediation, the
         Commission further notes that the Parties are both active in such a market in
         Germany but. neither of the Parties is active in such market in Austria. Therefore,
         the Parties’ combined market shares on the market for the supply of SAVoD
         advertising sold via intermediation (and any sub-segment thereof) in a market for
         Austria and Germany combined are driven by their activities in the narrower
         market for the supply of SAVoD (intermediated sales) in Germany. As a result,
         and combined with the fact that the Parties’ activities for the supply of TV
         advertising space (via intermediated sales) do not overlap at national level, the
         Commission notes that any assessment for this narrower market for the supply of
         SAVoD via intermediated sales in Germany would apply equally to the markets
         for the supply of SAVoD via intermediated sales for Austria and Germany
         combined as well as to the markets (in Germany, and Austria and Germany
         combined) for the supply of all AV advertising (sold via intermediation) and for
         the supply of TV and SAVoD advertising (sold via intermediation).
(538)    RTL has been selling SAVoD advertising inventory in Germany for RTL2 (in
         which RTL is a non-controlling shareholder) [business/commercial information
         of RTL], for Warner (Discovery+), [business/commercial information of RTL],
         and for HBO Max and Deutsche Telekom (Magenta TV) [business/commercial
         information of RTL]. As regards RTL2’s advertising inventory, RTL sells […]
         the advertising space related to RTL2’s content on RTL’s own SAVoD platform
         RTL+. Sky DACH has been selling SAVoD advertising inventory for Hearst
         Networks Germany and Warner channel VoD inventory [business/commercial
         information of Sky DACH], Mainstream Media [business/commercial
         information of Sky DACH], High View/Channel Factory (Jukebox)
         [business/commercial information of Sky], and [business/commercial
         information of Sky DACH]. (547) In total, the market for the supply of SAVoD
         advertising space via intermediated sales in Germany was EUR […] million in
         2024.
(539)    With regards to the narrower market for the supply of SAVoD advertising
         (intermediated sales) and segments thereof (on desktops or on mobile devices) in
         Germany, as well as with regards to the supply of TV advertising (intermediated
         sales) and segments thereof (FTA and pay-TV) in Austria and Germany
         combined, the Commission considers that the Transaction does not give rise to
         serious doubts for the following reasons.
(540)    First, the market for the supply of SAVoD advertising space sold via
         intermediation is a dynamic market.
(541)    While SVoD platforms have existed for several years in Germany, the market for
         the supply of SAVoD, and by extension the supply SAVoD advertising space, has
         only in the last few years achieved a burst of growth by the introduction of ad-
         supported tiers in major streaming services’ offers. For example, in the last
         four years, the market in Germany has seen the following SVoD platforms add
         ad-supported tiers (and hence become SAVoD platforms): Discovery+
         (June 2022), Freevee (August 2022), Netflix (November 2022), WOW




(547)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 16, tranche 1, Tables 1 and 2 and para. 8.
        [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s
        RFI 19, footnote 55.
                                               134
         (July 2023), Disney+ (November 2023), Amazon Prime Video (February 2024),
         RTL+ (August 2024), Paramount (June 2025) and HBO Max (January 2026). (548)
(542)    In light of the size of the market for the supply of SAVoD advertising space via
         intermediation and the limited number of contracts on the market, the
         Commission notes that a move by any of the Parties’ current customers to another
         supplier of intermediated services would have a significant impact on the Parties’
         respective shares. Such customers would not be prevented in switching away from
         the Parties’ intermediation services, as further set out below, and the limited value
         of these intermediation contracts as compared to the overall sale of TV advertising
         means that other ad sales houses would likely have the capacity to take on the
         business.
(543)    Second, a large number of content owners (SAVoD platforms and/or TV
         broadcasters) sell their own advertising inventory themselves directly, as
         evidenced by the size of the market and the number of competitors for the supply
         of TV and SAVoD advertising via direct sales. Such direct sales constitute an
         alternative route to market that constrain the Parties’ activities in intermediation
         in two ways. On the one hand, they show that direct sales are a credible alternative
         that is chosen in fact by the vast majority of content providers. On the other hand,
         it shows that many players have the capacity and ability to sell advertising space
         and that these players effectively constrain providers of intermediation services
         as they could enter the market.
(544)    In the first place, as regards the supply of TV advertising via direct sales in
         Germany, the market size is approximately EUR […] million in 2024 (compared
         to EUR […] million for the supply of TV advertising via intermediation). On such
         market, several competitors are active including at least P7S1 ([30-40]%), ZDF
         ([0-5]%), ARD ([5-10]%), RTL2 ([0-5]%), Warner ([0-5]%). Each of these
         players would have similar shares looking at narrower markets for the supply of
         pay-TV or FTA TV advertising via direct sales. (549) Similarly in Austria, the
         market for the supply of TV advertising via direct sales was approximately EUR
         […] million in 2024 (compared to EUR […] million for the supply of TV
         advertising via intermediation). On such market, several competitors are active

(572)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market,
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in Germany in the supply SAVoD via
         intermediated sales, overall or segmented into desktop and mobile devices. Given
         that the Parties’ combined market shares are driven by their activities in the
         market for the supply of SAVoD advertising (intermediated sales) in Germany
         which does not give rise to serious doubts for the reasons explained above, the
         Commission considers that the Transaction also does give rise to serious doubts
         in the markets with a wider product (namely intermediated sales for both TV and
         SAVoD advertising space, or intermediated sales for all AV advertising) or
         geographic scope (namely market comprised of Germany and Austria combined).

6.1.5.2.1.3.    Conclusion
(573)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal unilateral effects in the following markets in Germany, or
         in Austria and Germany combined, for the supply of: (i) TV advertising, (ii) pay-
         TV advertising, (iii) direct sales of TV advertising, (iv) direct sales of pay-TV
         advertising, (v) TV and SAVoD advertising, and (vi) direct sales of TV and
         SAVoD advertising, (vii) AV advertising (sold via intermediation), (viii) TV and
         SAVoD (sold via intermediation), (ix) SAVoD advertising (sold via
         intermediation), (x) SAVoD advertising on desktops (sold via intermediation),
         (xi) SAVoD advertising on mobile devices (sold via intermediation), (xii) TV
         advertising (sold via intermediation), (xiii) pay-TV advertising (sold via
         intermediation) and (xiv) FTA advertising (sold via intermediation).

        6.1.5.2.2.      Coordinated effects

6.1.5.2.2.1.    Notifying Party’s views
(574)    The Notifying Party submits that the market conditions do not favour a common
         understanding of RTL and P7S1 due to the disruptive transformation of the
         advertising markets. Over the last 15 years, the market for the supply of TV
         advertising has shrunken dramatically while online video advertising has risen
         exponentially. RTL and P7S1 reacted to these changes differently (569) and, as a
         result RTL went from having, in 2019, a market share with only [0-5]% difference
         in relation to P7S1, to having a lead over P7S1 of [5-10]% in 2025. This is proof
         of existing competition between the two companies while the decrease in volume
         of each of them is proof of competitive pressure by online video advertising. The
         audience shares of RTL and Vox remained stable whereas the audience shares of
         P7S1 have been decreasing continuously since 2005. Therefore, RTL and P7S1
         do not have symmetric market strategies in the market for the supply of TV
         advertising. (570)



(569)   The Notifying Party submits that, while RTL focused on pay models and developed its current
        RTL+ offering to compete with Netflix and Disney, P7S1 focused on building advertising reach
        with non-pay models and monetized its streaming offering Joyn which is almost exclusively ad
        supported and competes with Magenta TV.
(570)   Form CO, paras. 1582-1586.
                                                140
(575)    The Notifying Party submits that the market for the supply of TV advertising is
         not transparent given that the content owners’ net pricing is not public. RTL
         publishes an annual price list for TV advertising vis-à-vis a registered group of
         professional buyers. Prices are published by spot/time of day/channel etc. on a
         gross list price level and categorized into price groups. Gross prices at best only
         roughly indicate the value of an ad break because they translate the expected reach
         of the ad break into a value in Euros. These gross prices say however next to
         nothing about the net amount media buyers actually pay for their campaigns that
         are run across tens or hundreds of spots and for four to six weeks using several
         TV channels (and online video inventory). The net budget for the supply of
         airtime for this depends on non-public denominators such as overall annual
         budgets, cash rebates, free sports, number of campaigns, etc. Rebates are
         individually negotiated and highly confidential leading to very substantial
         deviations between published gross prices and individually paid net amounts for
         a campaign. The Notifying Party adds that digitalization trends in TV advertising
         such as programmatic buying, real-time bidding and CPM based pricing will
         further impact traditional negotiation patterns. Further, media agencies do not
         normally cross-reference offers they have from competing suppliers more than is
         customary in market economy negotiations. (571)
(576)    The Notifying Party also submits that media agencies have buyer power and this
         would jeopardize any results of coordination. Media agencies have many
         competing AV advertising offerings and can choose the inventory at the price
         point that suits them best. Media buyers can buy from one (or none) of RTL and
         P7S1 and procure the additional reach they need from SAVoD and online video
         (in particular YouTube), which has led to a steep decline of TV advertising. This
         countervailing buyer power is further show by the expansion of media agencies’
         own ‘trading’ (i.e., bulk buying) business to the detriment of content owners such
         as the Parties. (572)
(577)    The Notifying Party considers that the symmetry between RTL and P7S1 will
         decrease as a result of the Transaction, and the risk of coordination will therefore
         not only remain unaltered but will appreciably decrease. First, the relevance of
         advertising revenues for RTL’s overall business will decrease and there will be
         more focus on subscription-based revenues (with Sky DACH, paid services will
         be around […]% of the overall revenue of the merged entity). Second, symmetry
         between RTL and P7S1’s programming/inventory will decrease as Sky DACH
         focuses strongly on live sports content (in particular Bundesliga) whereas P7S1
         has a focus on fiction and non-fiction programming with a strong position in inter
         alia non-scripted content. This lowers the chances that RTL and P7S1 can monitor
         each other’s deviations from a hypothetical coordination. Third, the pricing of
         Sky DACH’s advertising inventory differs compared to the pricing of RTL’s
         inventory. Sky DACH’s net CPM are […] as RTL’s net CPM on RTL’s main
         channel with its highest CPM (RTL channel). If AdAlliance were to sell Sky
         DACH’s […] post-Transaction, RTL’s incentive and ability to implicitly
         coordinate its airtime sales/prices with P7S1 would therefore decrease. (573)
(578)    The Notifying Party submits that there are no deterrent mechanisms pre-
         Transaction and the Transaction does not increase the likelihood of there being
         such deterrent mechanism. Since RTL will be less dependent on advertising post-

(571)   Form CO, paragraph 1587 and 1821-1824.
(572)   Form CO, paras 1588-1592.
(573)   Form CO, paras 1593-1599.
                                                 141
         Transaction, its vulnerability to any form of retaliation exerted by P7S1 will be
         decreased. RTL’s own strategic shift towards the subscription-based business will
         also decrease the likelihood of RTL selling to deploy measures of deterrence. (574)

6.1.5.2.2.2.    Commission assessment
(579)    The Commission notes that RTL and P7S1 together hold [70-80]% of the market
         for linear TV advertising in Germany while other competitors hold less than
         [5-10]%. (575) These market shares have been relatively stable for at least the years
         2022-2024. (576) The General Court has held that a collective market share above
         50% is, in the absence of exceptional circumstances, a clear indication of the
         existence of a collective dominant position as a collective entity with such market
         share has the power to behave, to an appreciable extent, independently of its
         competitors, customers and ultimately consumers. (577) The General Court also
         held that a market share of 60% over a period of three years ‘cannot be regarded
         as prima facie insufficient to give rise to a presumption of a dominant
         position’. (578)
(580)    The Commission considers that it cannot be excluded that there is at least some
         degree of transparency in the market for the supply of linear TV advertising in
         Germany. In the market investigation, the vast majority of content owners who
         expressed a view indicated that they are not aware of the price offered by


(574)   Form CO, para. 1599.
(575)   The Commission further notes that the German Competition Authority (Bundeskartellamt) has
        found that RTL and P7S1 have a dominant duopoly in the market for the supply of TV advertising
        in Germany in Decision of 19 January 2006, B6-103/05 Axel Springer/ProSiebenSat.1 Media AG,
        and Decision of 17 March 2011, B6-94/10 ProSiebenSat.1 Media AG/RTL interactive GmbH. The
        first case concerned a merger between Axel Springer and P7S1 where the Bundeskartellamt
        considered that “with the merger the newspaper BILD would lose its substitute function as currently
        the only economic alternative to national TV advertising for advertising customers”. The second
        case concerned an antitrust proceeding related to a joint-venture between RTL interactive GmbH
        and PS71 for the creation and operation of an online video platform. The Bundeskartellamt found
        that the joint-venture would have further strengthened the dominant duopoly held by RTL and P7S1
        on the German TV advertising market and enable the two companies to coordinate and harmonize
        important parameters of competition for them. The present case, however, does not concern a
        transaction involving the two companies that have been found to constitute a dominant duopoly
        RTL and P7S1. Instead, the Transaction involves only RTL and a company that has not been found
        to be part of a dominant duopoly. The Commission highlights that its assessment is limited to
        determining whether this specific Transaction increases the likelihood that firms coordinate or
        makes coordination easier, more stable or more effective for firms that may have been coordinating
        already before the Transaction.

(578)   Joined Cases T-191/98 and T-212/98, Atlantic Container Line and Others v Commission, ECJ

                                                   142
         competitors. (579) Respondents indicated that TV broadcasters have public gross
         price lists (i.e., rate cards) but the final net price (i.e., after rebates) is individually
         negotiated with customers. (580) There is no evidence resulting from the market
         investigation that suppliers of linear TV advertising in Germany know each
         other’s final net prices. The majority of media agencies and advertisers who
         expressed a view indicated that they ask for price quotations from several TV
         broadcasters to compare conditions (581) and advertisers are aware of rebates that
         media agencies obtain from content owners/ad sales houses. (582) However, it is
         not clear from the market investigation whether advertisers know each others’
         final net prices. One respondent did mention that media auditors (e.g., IMEDIAG,
         EBiquity, etc.) also ensure a certain level of price transparency in the market. (583)
(581)    The Commission considers however that the Transaction does not raise serious
         doubts as a result of horizontal coordinated effects in the market for linear TV
         advertising in Germany by making coordination more likely for the following
         reasons.
(582)    First, as regards the high market shares of RTL and P7S1, the Commission notes
         that their high combined share already exists prior to the Transaction. A merger
         in a concentrated market can give rise to competition concerns if it increases the
         likelihood that firms coordinate or when it makes coordination easier, more stable
         or more effective for firms that were already coordinating before the merger. (584)
         Thus, the likelihood of coordination before the Transaction is, in itself, not
         decisive as the Commission assesses the change brought about by the Transaction.
         However, evidence of past coordination will be taken into account when assessing
         whether the merger may make coordination more likely. (585)
(583)    Second, in the market investigation, the vast majority of market respondents who
         expressed a view answered that they are not aware of examples of past
         coordination (on prices or commercial terms) between suppliers active in the
         market for linear TV advertising space in Germany (586).
(584)    Third, as mentioned above in Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, media buying agencies have
         a certain level of bargaining power in the market for linear TV advertising in
         Germany, which may render more difficult any eventual coordination.
(585)    Fourth, the majority of respondents who expressed a view considered that the
         Transaction would not make it possible, easier and/or more likely for some or all
         of the suppliers of linear TV advertising space in Germany to coordinate their
         commercial behaviour in such a way that would not be possible without the
         Transaction. (587) One customer mentioned that “we do not see that the
         transaction has any impact on the ability of market participants to coordinate on
         prices or other conditions.” Media buying agencies indicated that “compared to
         the overall market and the number of AV advertising providers, the effect of the
         transaction is too small to have any impact” and “linear TV advertising remains
         subject to competitive negotiations, audience fragmentation and declining reach,

                                                 143
         which constrain suppliers’ ability to coordinate commercial behaviour.” One
         bookmaker mentioned “[e]ven if RTL and Sky Germany merge the TV landscape
         remains competitive with Pro7Sat1 and DAZN and public broadcasters.” (588)
(586)    Fifth, nothing in the market investigation or the Commission’s case file indicates
         that Sky DACH has prevented or disrupted potential coordination between RTL
         and P7S1 in the past. Furthermore, as described in more detail in
         Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, Sky DACH is not a particular close competitor to RTL. As
         such, it has not been a viable alternative for many advertisers that would, before
         the Transaction, switch to Sky DACH in case of coordination between RTL and
         P7S1.
(587)    Sixth, the Transaction will decrease the symmetry between RTL and P7S1’s
         advertising activities. Currently, both RTL and P7S1 have an advertisement
         revenue-based model and their advertising inventory relates to entertainment
         programming (including fiction and non-fiction). As mentioned in
         Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, Sky DACH is not a close competitor to RTL (or P7S1) in
         the market for the supply of linear TV advertising. Sky DACH is focused on
         subscription-based revenues, and its advertising inventory is mainly related to
         sports. Therefore, the acquisition of Sky DACH by RTL will differentiate RTL
         and P7S1’s (currently very similar) advertising activities which also lowers the
         likelihood of coordination in the market for the supply of linear TV advertising in
         Germany.
(588)    Seventh, as mentioned above in Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, the market for linear TV
         advertising in Germany is undergoing significant changes, due to developments
         in neighbouring markets, namely with the increasingly important role played by
         suppliers of SAVoD and online video advertising on YouTube. The Commission
         considers that the fact that the market environment is not completely stable in
         linear TV advertising and in neighbouring markets renders coordination more
         difficult to reach. (589)
(589)    In light of the above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction will not
         change the market structure in such a way as to make coordination in the market
         for linear TV advertising easier, more stable or more effective.
(590)    Given that the Transaction does not raise serious doubts in relation to coordinated
         effects in the narrower market for linear TV advertising for the reasons explained
         above, on the basis of similar considerations, the Commission considers that the
         Transaction also does not raise concerns in the potential broader market including
         linear TV advertising and SAVoD or TV advertising, SAVoD and online video
         advertising on YouTube.

6.1.5.2.2.3.    Conclusion
(591)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of horizontal coordinated effects in the market for the supply of linear
         TV advertising, the market for linear TV advertising and SAVoD or the market

6.2.      Vertical non-coordinated effects

6.2.1.    Legal framework
(592)     The Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines describe several scenarios in which
          vertical mergers may significantly impede effective competition. In particular,
          non-coordinated effects may principally arise when vertical mergers give rise to
          foreclosure. (590) The Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines distinguish between two
          forms of foreclosure: (i) where the merger is likely to raise the costs of
          downstream rivals by restricting their access to an important input (input
          foreclosure); and (ii) where the merger is likely to foreclose upstream rivals by
          restricting their access to a sufficient customer base (customer foreclosure). (591)

6.2.1.1. Input foreclosure
(593)     According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, input foreclosure arises
          where, post-merger, the new entity would likely restrict access to the products or
          services that it would have otherwise supplied absent the merger, thereby raising
          its downstream rivals’ costs by making it harder for them to obtain supplies of the
          input under similar prices and conditions as absent the merger. (592)
(594)     For input foreclosure to be a concern, the vertically integrated firm post-merger
          must have a significant degree of market power in the upstream market. Only in
          these circumstances the merged firm can be expected to have a significant
          influence on the conditions of competition in the upstream market and thus,
          possibly, on prices and supply conditions in the downstream market. (593)
(595)     In assessing the likelihood of an anticompetitive input foreclosure scenario, the
          Commission examines, first, whether the merged entity would have, post-merger,
          the ability to substantially foreclose access to inputs, second, whether it would
          have the incentive to do so, and third, whether a foreclosure strategy would have
          a significant detrimental effect on competition downstream. In practice, these
          factors are often examined together since they are closely intertwined. (594)

6.2.1.2. Customer foreclosure
(596)     According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, customer foreclosure may
          occur when a supplier integrates with an important customer in the downstream
          market. Because of this downstream presence, the merged entity may foreclose
          access to a sufficient customer base to its actual or potential rivals in the upstream
          market (the input market) and reduce their ability or incentive to compete. (595)
(597)     For customer foreclosure to be a concern, the vertical merger must involve a
          company which is an important customer with a significant degree of market
          power in the downstream market. If, on the contrary, there is a sufficiently large
          customer base, at present or in the future, that is likely to turn to independent

(592)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, para. 31.
(593)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, para. 35.
(594)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, para. 32.
(595)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, para. 58.
                                                  145
          suppliers, the Commission is unlikely to raise competition concerns on that
          ground. (596)
(598)     In assessing the likelihood of an anticompetitive customer foreclosure scenario,
          the Commission examines, first, whether the merged entity would have the ability
          to foreclose access to downstream markets by reducing its purchases from its
          upstream rivals, second, whether it would have the incentive to reduce its
          purchases upstream, and third, whether a foreclosure strategy would have a
          significant detrimental effect on consumers in the downstream market. (597)

6.2.2.    Overview of affected vertical relationships
(599)     The Transaction establishes vertical relationships between the Parties’ business
          activities throughout all levels of the AV value chain. However, only the
          following pairs of markets and their respective segments listed below create
          vertically affected links:
          (1) supply of AV content (upstream), and acquisition of AV content
              (downstream);
          (2) supply of AV content (upstream), and wholesale supply of TV channels/AV
              services (downstream);
          (3) wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (upstream), and wholesale
              acquisition of TV channels/AV services (downstream);
          (4) wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (upstream), and paid AV retail
              (downstream); and
          (5) acquisition of AV content (upstream), and wholesale supply of TV
              channels/AV services and paid AV retail (downstream).
(600)     The Parties’ market shares for the five pairs of vertically affected relationships
          are listed in Table 25 below. For each pair, Table 25 includes the following
          information: (i) upstream market; (ii) range of the Parties’ combined market share
          in the upstream market and its potential affected segments; (598) (iii) affected
          plausible geographic markets for the upstream market and its potential affected
          segments; (iv) affected plausible geographical markets for the downstream market
          and its potential affected segments ; (v) range of the Parties’ combined market
          share in the downstream market and its potential affected segments; (599) and (vi)
          downstream market.

(601)     The Transaction also results in vertical links for a number of other markets.
          However, none of these overlaps or links give rise to affected markets within the
          meaning of recital 25(g) of Annex I to Regulation (EU) 2023/914, as they meet
          the conditions for review under point 5 or benefit from the flexibility clauses of
          point 8 of the Notice on Simplified Procedure. (602) Therefore, these overlaps will
          not be further discussed in this decision.

6.2.3.    Vertical relationship between the markets for the supply of AV content (upstream),
          and the markets for the acquisition of AV content (downstream)
(602)     The Transaction gives rise to vertical links between the Parties’ supply of AV
          content in the potential segment for pre-produced AV content in Germany and in
          a number of potential upstream segments in Austria, South Tyrol, Liechtenstein
          and Luxembourg and the Parties’ acquisition of AV content in the potential
          segment for pre-produced AV content in Germany and in a number of potential
          downstream segments in Austria, South Tyrol, Liechtenstein and Luxembourg.

6.2.3.1. Notifying Party’s views
(603)     The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in an
          input foreclosure strategy of competing acquirers of commissioned and pre-
          produced AV content.
(604)     First, the combined market share of the Parties for the supply of AV content
          remains below [5-10]%, even when considering any plausible market sub-
          segmentation. (603)
(605)     Second, the Parties cannot block competing acquirers of AV content. They lack
          the necessary market power to do so, and their commissioned and pre-produced
          AV content does not qualify as an important input for either entertainment or
          sports-related content. (604) The Parties provide pre-produced content only in rare
          cases and typically sublicense content that is not unique, as it can be obtained
          from other production studios and initially acquired for their own use. (605)
          Moreover, the content offered by RTL’s subsidiary Fremantle (i.e. primarily
          factual programming, documentaries, and series) does not stand out from
          offerings by other production studios, making it highly interchangeable with
          many suppliers in the market (e.g., major US studios and local production
          studios). (606)
(606)     Third, the rights to pre-produce AV content are typically licensed for limited
          periods. After these licenses expire, rights revert to the licensor, allowing for new
          exhibition opportunities. With a constant influx of new AV content and varied
          exploitation options, there is always enough supply in each market segment.
          License agreements for non-sports content usually cover German-speaking
          Territories. International SVoD services like Amazon and Netflix often provide
          synchronized or subtitled content in multiple languages, further enhancing
          availability in various national markets. In terms of sports media rights, there is a

         (EC) No 139/2004 on the control of concentrations between undertakings (2023/C 160/01).
(603)    Form CO, para. 1989.
(604)    According to the Commission’s Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, an important input is one that
         significantly impacts the cost of a downstream product or is essential for its production, and the
         Parties’ supply of AV content does not meet these criteria.
(605)    Form CO, para. 1990.
(606)    Form CO, paras 1991 and 1992.
                                                    150
         strong supply available. Most rights are awarded through regular tenders for
         limited durations, specifically for the DACH region. However, only a few major
         rights holders, such as FIFA and UEFA, segment markets nationally to maximize
         revenue. The popularity of foreign sports, such as American football, has also
         risen in Germany and Europe, leading to increased media rights for these events.
         Additionally, sublicensing options allow unsuccessful bidders to acquire rights
         later if licensees choose to refinance or manage their risk. (607)
(607)    Finally, the largest five suppliers for pre-produced AV content cover
         approximately [80-90]% of the market and they are not RTL or Sky DACH. (608)

6.2.3.2. Commission’s assessment
(608)    The Commission’s investigation did not find any evidence indicating that the
         Transaction would lead to input foreclosure for competing acquirers of AV
         content in Germany. Furthermore, neither Party has the ability or incentive to
         become the exclusive supplier for the other Party after the Transaction. As a
         result, there is no risk of customer foreclosure.
(609)    First, as regards the possibility of engaging in input foreclosure, the Commission
         considers that for the supply of individual AV content, it seems unlikely that the
         Parties’ content can be considered an important input. The Parties currently make
         their own AV content only sporadically available for inclusion in competing TV
         channels or retail TV services. Their combined market shares in pre-produced AV
         content are under [0-10]% in any plausible market subsegment and many other
         suppliers exist. (609) Therefore, it seems likely that even in case of a foreclosure
         of the Parties’ content, rival acquirers would still have access to a wide range of
         content.
(610)    In the first place, the Commission notes that the Parties lack the ability or
         incentive to pursue a foreclosure strategy because any loss of revenue from
         stopping the supply of commissioned and pre-produced AV content to third
         parties cannot be offset by revenue from other levels in the AV value chain. The
         licensed content from RTL and Sky DACH is not considered essential. RTL,
         through its subsidiary Fremantle, offers some pre-produced AV content in
         German-speaking areas, [business/commercial information of the Parties] Sky
         DACH has never provided commissioned AV content and is only
         opportunistically involved in pre-produced AV content. Competing acquirers
         have multiple alternatives and can easily switch away from the Parties. (610)
(611)    In the second place, the Commission notes that the Parties aim to sublicense
         certain rights held by RTL or Sky DACH, expected to account for less than
         [10-20]% of the anticipated content-related synergies. [business/commercial
         information of RTL] Sublicensing requires approval from the licensor, making it
         a preliminary plan. Most of the expected content synergies will come from the
         cessation and restructuring of rights. (611)
(612)    In the third place, the Commission conducted a market investigation to assess
         whether, post-Transaction, the Parties would have the ability to stop supplying or

(608)   Form CO, para. 1997. The five largest suppliers of pre-produced non-sports AV content in Germany
        and Austria are Disney, Paramount, Sony, Universal and Warner.
(609)   Annex 8 to the Form CO, and Form CO para.1989.

(611)   Form CO, para. 2000.
                                                  151
         degrade the quality and conditions of their AV content supply in Germany (612).
         The investigation also considered whether the Parties would have an incentive to
         engage in such strategies in Germany (613) and if these strategies might negatively
         affect any potential market segments for supplying and acquiring AV content in
         Germany. (614) The results of the investigation were inconclusive.
(613)    Firstly, most respondents indicated that they do not know whether the merged
         entity would have the ability to foreclose competing acquirers of AV content.
         Additionally, among respondents with a view, the results were also inconclusive,
         as the same percentage (28%) answered “yes” and “no” to whether the Parties had
         the ability to do so. (615) One respondent that indicated that the merged entity
         would have the ability to engage in an input foreclosure strategy in Germany,
         explained that: “By combining RTL’s and Sky DACH’s entertainment content with
         Sky DACH’s premium sports, the merged entity would control essential content
         while operating its own OTT platforms (TV retail). We expect that the merged
         entity could deny or degrade access to key content rights, impose discriminatory
         terms, and leverage cost-free OTT distribution to accelerate customer migration
         away from competing TV retail platforms to their OTT-TV retail platform. […].”
         However, another respondent indicated that: “Competition in Germany is so
         strong that we do not believe that as a result of the merger the market position of
         combined entity will be able to afford a degradation of the quality or the terms
         and conditions of their supply of AV content.” (616)
(614)    Secondly, the market investigation could not confirm that the Parties would have
         an incentive to pursue such strategies, as only 30% of respondents expressed this
         view, compared to those who disagreed or had no opinion on the matter. (617) In
         this regard, a competitor explained that: “The Parties could easily use the
         essential (must have) nature of popular RTL Free TV channels to enforce the
         preferential distribution of RTL Pay channels and Sky premium channels, content
         and services.” However, another competitor indicated that: “Probably they (the
         Parties) cannot rely on their own products alone so they need to supply.” (618)

(615)    Thirdly, the findings related to whether these strategies would have a negative
         impact on the potential segments of the market for the supply of AV content were
         not conclusive, only 25% of the respondents taking a view expect a negative
         impact whereas 5% were positive and 70% neutral.
(616)    Fourthly, the findings on whether these strategies might negatively affect certain
         segments of the market for acquiring AV content were also inconclusive.
         Specifically, 32% of respondents anticipated a negative impact, 5% had a positive
         outlook, and 63% remained neutral. (619)
(617)    Second, the Commission considers that acquirers of AV content, such as AV
         retail service providers and wholesalers, must offer a variety of content to meet
         customer needs. Therefore, they need to purchase from multiple producers to
         remain competitive. The merged entity will not be able to engage in customer
         foreclosure due to the unique AV market dynamics. (620)
(618)    In the first place, the Commission noted in a previous case that competition in AV
         content production is different from other vertical markets; relying on a single
         content producer would not enhance retail competitiveness because diverse
         offerings are essential for appealing to different audiences. (621) Consequently, the
         Parties will need to continue acquiring AV content from various suppliers,
         particularly for sports content tied to specific events and leagues.
(619)    In the second place, [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. As such,
         the Parties cannot withdraw from the market as content buyers, especially in
         premium fiction and sports segments. (622)

        6.2.3.2.1.      The Commission’s analysis does not change for other potentially
                        relevant geographic areas
(620)     The Commission has found no indications that its competitive assessment
         regarding the vertical effects of the Transaction, particularly concerning the
         relationship between the supply of AV content (upstream) and the acquisition of
         AV content (downstream), would differ in any of the other potentially relevant
         geographic areas. These areas include all German-speaking Territories together,
         Austria, Luxembourg, Liechtenstein, and South Tyrol.
(621)    More precisely, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that there are
         no specific concerns for any of the other potentially relevant geographic areas. All
         respondents who shared their views indicated that their responses would remain
         the same when considering Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg, and South Tyrol
         in relation to the supply and acquisition of AV content. (623) The majority of these
         respondents reinforced that their answers would not change in that context.
(622)    In this context, the Commission concludes that its assessment regarding potential
         input foreclosure concerns for competing acquirers of AV content and customer
         foreclosure concerns—concerning the Parties potentially becoming the exclusive
         supplier of AV content to each other—applies equally to all other potentially
         relevant geographic areas, including all German-speaking Territories.

(620)   Form CO, paras 2002-5.

6.2.3.3. Conclusion
(623)     In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
          the evidence and information available to it, for the purposes of the present
          Decision, the Commission concludes the Transaction does not give rise to serious
          doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of: foreclosing
          competing third parties from accessing the Parties’ supply of AV content in
          Germany and other German-speaking Territories, or (ii) foreclosing customers by
          making the Parties exclusive suppliers to each other in Germany and other
          German-speaking Territories, considering that the merged entity would not have
          the ability or incentive to engage in such strategies.

6.2.4.    Vertical relationship between the markets for the supply of AV content (upstream)
          and the markets for the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services
          (downstream)
(624)     The Transaction gives rise to vertical links between the Parties’ supply of AV
          content in the potential upstream segments for pre-produced AV content in
          Germany, Austria and all German-speaking Territories and the Parties’ wholesale
          supply of TV channels/AV services in Germany and in a number of potential
          downstream segments in Austria.

6.2.4.1. Notifying Party’s views
(625)     The Notifying Party submits that its activities in supplying AV content and
          providing wholesale TV channels/AV services do not raise any vertical
          competition concerns.
(626)     First, the Notifying Party explains that the combined market share of the Parties
          for both commissioned and pre-produced AV content is below [0-10]% in any
          potential market segment. As a result, the likelihood of foreclosing competing
          wholesale suppliers of TV channels by either ceasing or degrading the terms of
          their AV content supply is minimal. Moreover, the AV content offered by the
          Parties, regardless of any sub-segmentation, is not deemed an essential input. AV
          content distributors can easily replace the content from the merged entity without
          diminishing its appeal to consumers. Consequently, the Parties do not have the
          ability to exclude competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels by withholding
          content that they had previously sold pre-Transaction. (624)
(627)     Second, the Notifying Party explains that the revenue loss from stopping the
          supply of commissioned and pre-produced AV content to third parties could not
          be offset by gains in other levels of the AV value chain. This is because the AV
          content provided by the Parties is not considered “must-have”. Additionally,
          pursuing such a strategy poses the risk that other vertically integrated companies
          may restrict access to their AV content, which could decrease the competitiveness
          of the merged entity. This risk is significant, as most market participants operate
          on a vertically integrated basis. Notable vertically integrated companies with
          major production studios and substantial market presence in AV content supply
          include Amazon, Disney, Paramount, and Warner. These companies are not only
          involved in supplying AV content but also in wholesaling their basic TV and pay-
          TV channels, as well as SVoD services, in addition to retailing paid AV
          services. (625)

(624)    Form CO, paras 2011-13.
(625)    Form CO, para. 2014.
                                             154
6.2.4.2. Commission’s assessment
(628)    The Commission has determined that its investigation revealed no evidence
         indicating that the Transaction would exclude competing wholesale suppliers of
         TV channels/AV services in Germany. Additionally, the market investigation did
         not find evidence that the Parties have the ability or incentive to eliminate rival
         wholesalers or to cease their acquisition of third-party AV content in Germany.
(629)    First, the Commission has previously noted that the supply market for AV content
         is highly competitive in Germany. In a hypothetical scenario where a company
         were to restrict access to a competitor's content, the impact on the market would
         likely be limited. This is because there are numerous other suppliers of AV
         content, providing consumers with a broad range of options. Additionally,
         vertically integrated platforms could respond by offering content exclusively on
         their own services. As a result, it is unlikely that a company would be able to
         capture downstream demand by limiting access to a competitor's content on
         competing platforms. (626)
(630)    Second, as previously noted in Section 6.2.3.2 above, the Commission considers
         that the Parties' AV content is unlikely to be an important input for individual AV
         content distributors. The Parties sporadically make their content available for
         competing TV channels, holding less than [5-10]% of the pre-produced AV
         content market. (627) Many alternative suppliers exist, ensuring rival acquirers
         have access to a broad range of content. Additionally, any revenue loss from
         withholding commissioned AV content from third parties cannot be offset by
         revenue from other areas of the AV value chain. RTL, through Fremantle, offers
         some pre-produced AV content in German-speaking Territories,
         [business/commercial information of RTL]. Sky DACH does not provide
         commissioned content and is minimally involved in pre-produced content.
         Competing acquirers can easily switch to other providers.
(631)    Third, as previously noted in Section 6.2.3.2 above, the Commission considers
         that acquirers of AV content, including wholesalers of TV channels/AV services,
         must offer diverse content to meet customer needs and remain competitive by
         sourcing from multiple producers. The merged entity cannot engage in customer
         foreclosure due to the dynamics of the AV market. For instance,
         [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. Thus, the Parties cannot
         withdraw from the market as content buyers, especially in premium fiction and
         sports. (628)
(632)    Fourth, the results of the Commission's market investigation were inconclusive
         regarding whether the Parties have the ability and incentive to foreclose
         competitors by restricting supply or worsening the terms and conditions for TV
         channels/AV services provided to wholesale purchasers in Germany.
(633)    In the first place, the findings on the Parties' ability to stop supplying or degrade
         the quality of their AV content were not conclusive for Germany, and the same
         applies to the rest of the German-speaking Territories, as the percentage of
         respondents who answered "yes" and "no" was equal. Additionally, the majority
         of respondents had no opinion on whether the Parties would have the incentive to



(626)   Commission decision of 15 March 2022 in Case M.10349 – Amazon/MGM, para. 205.
(627)   Annex 8 to the Form CO.
(628)   Form CO, paras 2017-18.
                                              155
         engage in such strategies. (629) A respondent of the Commission’s market
         investigation explained in more detail: “stopping the supply of content or making
         it less economically attractive would not be a profitable strategy. Licensees would
         just turn elsewhere, which is easy since there’s no such thing as ‘must-have’ AV
         content, most companies can produce content themselves, and as there are plenty
         of other licensors willing to license to Germany anyways. RTL/Sky rely on
         licensing from others, and tactics like these would alienate other integrated
         companies, most of which would probably retaliate against RTL/Sky and stop
         licensing to them. This would lead to a worse content offering for RTL/Sky,
         leading to profit loss.” (630)
(634)    In the second place, when asked about the overall impact of the Transaction on
         the plausible segments of wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services, only
         29% of respondents taking a view expect a negative impact, whereas 10% expect
         a positive impact, and 62% are neutral. (631)

        6.2.4.2.1.      The Commission’s analysis does not change for other potentially
                        relevant geographic areas
(635)    The Commission has found no indications that its competitive assessment
         regarding the vertical effects of the Transaction, particularly concerning the
         relationship between the supply of AV content (upstream) and the wholesale
         supply of TV channels/AV services (downstream), would differ in any of the
         other potentially relevant geographic areas (i.e., all German-speaking Territories
         together, Austria, Luxembourg, Liechtenstein, and South Tyrol).
(636)    More precisely, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that there are
         no specific concerns for any of the other potentially relevant geographic areas. All
         respondents who shared their views indicated that their responses would remain
         the same when considering Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg, and South Tyrol
         in relation to the supply of AV content and the wholesale supply of TV
         channels/AV services. (632) The majority of these respondents reinforced that their
         answers would not change in that context.
(637)    In this context, the Commission concludes that its assessment regarding potential
         input foreclosure concerns for competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV
         services and customer foreclosure concerns—concerning the Parties potentially
         eliminating rival wholesalers or halting the acquisition of third-party AV
         content—applies equally to all other potentially relevant geographic areas,
         including all German-speaking Territories.

6.2.4.3. Conclusion
(638)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
         the evidence and information available to it, for the purposes of the present
         Decision, the Commission concludes the Transaction does not give rise to serious

          competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV services in Germany and other

          of third-party AV content in Germany and other German-speaking Territories,

          engage in such strategies.

6.2.5.    Vertical relationship between the markets for the wholesale supply of TV
          channels/AV services (upstream), and the markets for the wholesale acquisition
          of TV channels/AV services (downstream)
(639)     The Transaction gives rise to vertical links between the Parties’ wholesale supply
          of TV channels/AV services in a number of upstream segments in Austria,
          Germany, all German-speaking Territories, Liechtenstein and Luxembourg and
          the Parties’ wholesale acquisition of TV channels/AV services in a number of
          downstream segments in Austria, Germany, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg and all
          German-speaking Territories.
(640)     RTL and Sky DACH are involved in the wholesale supply of TV channels and
          SVoD services at the upstream level, as well as the retail provision of these
          services at the downstream level. RTL supplies its linear FTA / basic pay-TV
          channels and its SVoD service RTL+ to third-party distributors. In contrast, Sky
          DACH primarily focuses on DtC distribution for its linear premium pay-TV
          channels (while still having some wholesale distribution activities) and serves as
          a platform for both its own and third-party TV channels and SVoD services at the
          downstream level. Sky DACH also supplies its SVoD offering to third-party
          distributors.
(641)     The Commission assesses the effects of these aforementioned vertical
          relationships in detail in the following Section 6.2.6 (vertical links between the
          Parties’ wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services upstream and paid AV
          retail downstream). It does not separately discuss the assessment of the effects of
          the Transaction arising from vertical links between upstream wholesale supply
          and wholesale acquisition of TV channels/AV services. In the present case, the
          Parties’ wholesale acquisition activities serve only as an input for their
          downstream retail offerings. To that extent, the assessment of effects at the
          wholesale acquisition level overlaps with the analysis conducted at the retail level.
          The Commission, therefore, refers for the assessment of the effects of the
          Transaction resulting from vertical links between the wholesale supply of TV
          channels/AV services upstream and the wholesale acquisition of TV channels/AV
          services fully to its assessment in Section 6.2.6.

6.2.6.    Vertical relationship between the markets for the wholesale supply of TV
          channels/AV services (upstream), and the markets for paid AV retail
          (downstream)
(642)     The Transaction gives rise to vertical links between the Parties’ wholesale supply
          of TV channels/AV services in a number of upstream segments in Austria,
          Germany, Liechtenstein and Luxembourg, as well as in all German-speaking
          Territories (633) and the Parties’ paid AV retail services in a number of


(633)    On the upstream level for the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in Germany, the only
         market segment in which the Parties’ market shares exceed 30% is the segment for basic pay-TV
         channels with kids content ([40-50]% market share in 2024, only RTL is active).
                                                  157
         downstream segments (634) in Austria, Germany, Liechtenstein and Luxembourg,
         as well as in all German-speaking Territories. (635)

6.2.6.1. Notifying Party’s views
(643)    The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in an
         input foreclosure strategy regarding SVoD services or TV channels.
(644)    Regarding input foreclosure to the Parties’ SVoD services, the Notifying Party
         submits that it does not have the ability or incentive to foreclose competing
         providers of AV retail services by: (i) refusing to supply RTL+ or WOW Filme
         & Serien or degrading the quality of the service; or (ii) refusing to supply WOW
         Live-Sport; and/or (iii) or degrading the quality of or access to these services.
(645)    First, the Notifying Party submits that the merged entity will not have the ability
         to refuse to supply RTL+ or WOW Filme & Serien to competing aggregators. The
         merged entity would not have a significant degree of market power upstream.
         Furthermore, the Notifying Party submits that WOW Filme & Serien and RTL+
         are not a “must have” for aggregators, as multiple global streaming services offer
         alternative and comparable entertainment content (e.g., Netflix, Disney+, Joyn,
         Warner HBO or Paramount). Moreover, [business/commercial information of
         Sky DACH], permitting access to main competitors in the downstream market to
         the Parties SVoD services post-merger. (636)
(646)    Moreover, the Notifying Party submits that partial foreclosure could only take
         place if the merged entity were to retain entertainment or pre-airing content
         exclusively for its distribution partners. The Notifying Party indicates that the
         merged entity will not degrade the quality of the Parties’ SVoD services by
         reserving this content exclusively. Further to previous arguments, the Notifying
         Party submits that the Parties do not hold a unique content proposition and that
         they are not incentivised to retain content for one distribution partner, as only
         significantly lower average revenues per user could be achieved through third
         party distribution. (637)
(647)    Second, the Notifying Party indicates that the merged entity will not refuse to
         supply WOW Live-Sport as an add-on or degrade commercial terms for access,
         as it would not have a significant degree of market power upstream. Moreover,
         [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. Lastly, for an aggregator it
         would not be necessary to hard bundle SVoD services, as hard bundling of SVoD
         services is only one way of offering SVoD services to consumers and does not
         have a higher value in comparison with soft bundles. (638)
(648)    Moreover, the Notifying Party submits that partial foreclosure could only take
         place if the merged entity were to retain sports content (i.e., regular premium
         sports, football, all-year football and non-premium sports) exclusively for its
         distribution partners. The Notifying Party submits that the merged entity would
         not degrade the quality of the Parties’ SVoD services by reserving this content

        the Parties’ market shares exceed 30% is the segment for pay-TV retail services distributed via
        satellite ([70-80]% market share in 2024, only Sky DACH is active).

         exclusively. Further to previous arguments, the Notifying Party indicates that
         monetising sports rights by exploiting them on RTL+ would not make any
         economic sense due to its retail price (RTL+’s price is €9.99 to €14.99 per month,
         in comparison with WOW Live-Sport that is €34.99 to €44.99 per month)
         [business/commercial information of RTL]. The Notifying Party also submits that
         neither RTL nor Sky DACH tailor their sports content for one distribution partner.
         Lastly, shifting content would result in massive cancellations due to content
         loss. (639)
(649)    Regarding input foreclosure of TV channels, the Notifying Party submits that it
         does not have the ability or incentive to foreclose competing providers of AV
         retail services by: (i) refusing to supply RTL’s and Sky DACH’s TV channels; or
         (ii) degrading the quality of/access to TV channels.
(650)    The Notifying Party indicates that the merged entity will not have the ability to
         refuse to supply RTL’s TV channels. RTL faces strong competition from
         competing private TV channel providers (e.g., P7S1 accounts for [10-20]% in
         viewership in Germany and Austria) and publicly founded broadcasters.
         Furthermore, as an FTA broadcaster, RTL seeks the broadest market penetration
         and is, therefore, dependent on the transmission services of wholesale acquirers.
         Lastly, even if Sky DACH acts as an aggregator, it would be unlikely that the
         merged entity could be compensated by potential gains in the market for retail
         distribution of aggregated AV offers, as its market share is approx. [20-30]%. (640)
(651)    In addition, the Notifying Party submits that the Parties only have a more
         significant market share in a market segment for supplying TV channels,
         including basic pay-TV channels for children, in Germany and Austria, as well as
         in certain segments in Austria. However, their combined market shares in other
         subsegments of wholesale AV/TV channels in both countries and in German-
         speaking Territories are below the 30% threshold, which, according to Point 5 of
         the Notice on Simplified Procedure does not raise any vertical concerns. The
         Notifying Party considers that, even if the Parties have a more significant market
         share in this market segment, this does not prevent other TV channel acquirers
         from obtaining content. Acquirers of TV channels can still source dedicated
         children's programming and other content from various suppliers, regardless of
         any supply restrictions imposed by the merged entity. (641)
(652)    In the second place, the Notifying Party submits that strong competitors remain
         active, notably P7S1 and public broadcasters in Germany and Austria, which offer
         appealing content for kids and families. Major SVoD services also provide a wide
         selection of children’s content, serving as effective substitutes for acquiring TV
         channels in these regions. Aggregators have few limitations in adding kids'
         content to their offerings and can acquire it independently. Additionally, kids'
         content accounts for a small fraction of total costs, so significant price increases
         are unlikely to impact competition. (642)
(653)    The Notifying Party considers that there is no reason to prevent competing buyers
         of TV channels. As an advertising-funded FTA broadcaster, RTL needs broad
         distribution of its basic pay-TV channels, which will remain unchanged after the
         merger. The merged entity will continue to offer these channels to maintain


(639)   Form CO, paras. 2112 to 2139 and 2146 to 2150.

(641)   Form CO, paras. 2160-61.
(642)   Form CO, paras. 2161-64.
