         audience share and advertising revenues. Consequently, a strategy to foreclose
         competition would not significantly impact effective competition. RTL’s reliance
         on widespread TV channel distribution means the merged entity will not raise
         costs for competing providers, ensuring they can still compete effectively with
         ample alternatives available. (643)
(654)    In relation to customer foreclosure, the Notifying Party submits that RTL would
         neither have the ability nor the incentive to become Sky DACH’s exclusive
         supplier of basic TV or premium pay-TV channels: (644)
         (a) With regards to ability, the Notifying Party explains that Sky DACH’s market
             share on the relevant downstream markets/market segments would be well
             below 30%. Only on a segment for the supply of pay-TV services distributed
             via satellite, Sky DACH’s market share would be more sizeable. However,
             the Notifying Party submits that different distribution technologies are
             substitutable from a demand and supply side. The small market share of
             [10-20]% in Germany and [10-20]% in Austria on the overall market for the
             retail provision of paid AV services indicates that there is a sufficiently large
             customer base in addition to the Parties available to competing wholesale
             suppliers of TV channels/AV services.
         (b) The Notifying Party further argues that the merged entity would not have any
             incentive to engage in customer foreclosure, as Sky DACH would seek to
             have a broad portfolio of channels as platform and access provider; any
             degradation of the diversity of the Sky DACH platform would directly benefit
             Sky DACH’s competitors by rendering Sky DACH’s offering less attractive.
             Furthermore, competing broadcasters could retaliate.
         (c) Finally, the Notifying Party also argues that any customer foreclosure
             strategy would not have a significant effect on effective competition in
             Germany and Austria because the Parties’ combined market share on the
             overall market for paid AV retail is well below [10-20]%; this would be
             further supported by the fact that the Parties’ combined demand of TV
             channels on the market for the wholesale acquisition of basic pay-TV
             channels in Germany would only amount to [5-10]%.

6.2.6.2. Concerns raised in the course of the Commission’s investigation
(655)    In the course of the Commission’s investigation, some market participants raised
         input foreclosure concerns in relation to the availability of the merged entity’s
         content for bundles offered by competing retail TV service providers with a
         particular focus on aggregators. (645)
(656)    Market participants expressed concerns that the merged entity could, post-
         Transaction, retain certain content and features for its own platform RTL+,
         making them unavailable on FTA or pre-airing them on RTL+, which would
         decrease the attractiveness of such content on FTA, and thereby foreclose
         competing retail service providers and in particular aggregators. (646)
(657)    Market participants also explained that the merged entity could shift additional
         content from Sky to RTL+ which would increase the competitive disadvantage of

         that market participants compared to [business/commercial information of RTL].
         Furthermore, the merged entity could offer exclusive bundles for TV channels.
         One market participant also expressed concerns that the merged entity could
         leverage its retail TV position to impose unfair access conditions on
         competitors. (647)
(658)    In addition and in response to the Commission’s market investigation, some
         respondents expressed the view that both Sky DACH’s sports content (such as
         WOW Live-Sport) and any other AV content of the merged entity – for example
         RTL’s general entertainment content – could be considered as a “must have” that
         is required for a credible offering from the perspective of a retail TV
         provider. (648) Equally, a number of respondents also considered it highly likely
         or relatively likely that the merged entity will, post-Transaction, have the
         possibility to remove, restrict or degrade the quality of the supply, or increase the
         price, of its exclusive sports content or its other AV content to aggregators. (649)
         Some respondents also stated that it would, in a hypothetical scenario where the
         merged entity would post-Transaction remove, restrict, or degrade access to its
         exclusive sports or other AV content, not be possible to acquire this content from
         other sources. (650)
(659)    Equally, respondents to the Commission’s market investigation raised the concern
         that, post-Transaction, the merged entity would be able to stop supplying or
         degrade the quality of their TV channels/AV services to wholesale purchasers in

(660)    In addition, one market participant whose products are currently distributed by

         and incentive to remove the market participant’s product from the merged entity’s

6.2.6.3. Commission’s assessment

        6.2.6.3.1.       No input foreclosure concerning RTL’s RTL+ SVoD product in
                         Germany
(661)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose competing paid AV retail operators and in particular
         competing aggregators operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail
         services in Germany from obtaining RTL’s RTL+ SVoD product as input.

                                                  161
6.2.6.3.1.1.    No ability to engage in input foreclosure concerning RTL’s RTL+ SVoD
                product in Germany
(662)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction not have
         the ability to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality degradation) foreclose
         the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in particular competing
         aggregators (operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail services in
         Germany) to RTL’s RTL+ SVoD product in Germany.
(663)    First, according to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, for input foreclosure
         to be a concern, the vertically integrated firm resulting from the merger must have
         a significant degree of market power in the upstream market. (654) The
         Commission considers that neither RTL alone nor the merged RTL/Sky DACH
         entity has a significant degree of market power on the German market for the
         wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (and specifically not in the SVoD
         segment):
(664)    In the first place, the Parties’ individual and combined market shares on the
         relevant upstream markets and market segments are low:
         (a) RTL’s market share in 2024 on the market for the wholesale supply of TV
             channels/AV services in Germany was [10-20]%; Sky DACH adds an
             additional [0-5]%, leading to a combined market share of [10-20]%. (655)
         (b) RTL’s market share in 2024 on the market segment for the wholesale supply
             of SVoD services in Germany was [5-10]%; Sky DACH adds an additional
             [0-5]%, leading to a combined segment share of [10-20]%. (656)

6.2.6.3.1.2.    No incentive to engage in input foreclosure concerning RTL’s RTL+ SVoD
                product in Germany
(683)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         will likely not have the incentive to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in
         particular competing aggregators to RTL’s RTL+ SVoD product in Germany.
(684)    First, in the scenario of a refusal to supply RTL+ to competing aggregators or a
         degradation of the quality of the RTL+ product offered to competing aggregators
         post-merger, the Commission considers it unlikely that the extra profits that result
         from a restriction of access to RTL+ content for rival aggregators outweigh the
         foregone wholesale profits from supplying the RTL+ content to the aggregators,
         which makes un input foreclosure strategy unprofitable.
(685)    As regards potential foreclosure strategies involving RTL+, a distinction can be
         drawn between i. (partial or total) foreclosure of RTL+’s availability for inclusion
         in a hard bundle, with one price for the entire bundle, and ii. (partial or total)
         foreclosure of RTL+’s availability for inclusion in a soft bundle, with an add-on

         price for RTL’s SVoD service RTL+ on top of the price for the other services.
         These scenarios are assessed in turn.
(686)    Regarding hard bundles, [business/commercial information of RTL].
(687)    In the first place, by foreclosing access to RTL+, the merged entity would violate
         RTL’s        [business/commercial      information     of    RTL].     Breaching
         [business/commercial information of RTL] might lead to […] claiming financial
         compensation from the merged entity. (683)
(688)    In the second place, foreclosure of access to RTL+ for inclusion in a hard bundle
         is still likely to be unprofitable. [Business/commercial information of RTL]. (684).
         These consumers are unlikely to switch to the retail offerings of the merged entity
         if RTL+ is no longer available in [business/commercial information of RTL]
         which makes it unlikely that the additional revenues for the merged entity in the
         paid AV retail market outweigh the loss in wholesale revenues from no longer
         selling access to RTL+ for inclusion in a hard bundle.
(689)    Regarding soft bundles, it is also unlikely that the merged entity has an incentive
         to foreclose the availability of RTL+. In this scenario, RTL+ remains available
         DtC, without a price difference as compared to RTL+ as an addition in a soft
         bundle. (685) In practice, it is common that retail consumers purchase standalone
         subscriptions to SVoD services in addition to their TV subscription. In Germany,
         [60-70]% of SVoD service subscriptions are standalone subscriptions. (686) Given
         the higher retail prices of WOW and Sky pay-TV, and the possibility to purchase
         RTL+ on a stand-alone basis, which also occurs in practice, it is implausible that
         a sufficient number of consumers would switch from a rival retail offering to
         either WOW or Sky pay-TV after foreclosure of access to RTL+ to make it
         profitable for the merged entity. The foreclosure strategy in which the merged
         entity not only denies access to RTL+ for inclusion in soft bundles but also stops
         supplying RTL+ directly to consumers, is also likely to be unprofitable. In this
         scenario, consumers who want to maintain access to RTL+ have a cheaper option
         with DTAG’s offering instead of WOW or Sky pay-TV. Besides, most RTL+
         subscribers do not seem to have a particular interest in WOW sports content, as
         only [5-10]% of RTL+ subscribers also purchase a WOW sports
         subscription. (687)
(690)    Second, RTL’s internal documents show that it pursues a strategy that relies on

6.2.6.3.1.3.    Conclusion
(693)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential input foreclosure
         concerning RTL+ stemming from the vertical relationship between the markets
         for (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in Germany (and
         specifically in the SVoD segment) (upstream), and (ii) the paid AV retail services
         market in Germany (and specifically in the aggregated AV services segment)
         (downstream).

        6.2.6.3.2.       No input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Filme & Serien SVoD
                         product in Germany
(694)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose competing paid AV retail operators and in particular
         competing aggregators operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail
         services in Germany from obtaining Sky’s WOW Filme & Serien SVoD product
         as input.

6.2.6.3.2.1.    No ability to engage in input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Filme &
                Serien SVoD product in Germany
(695)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction not have
         the ability to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality degradation) foreclose
         the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in particular competing
         aggregators (operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail services in
         Germany) to Sky DACH’s WOW Filme & Serien SVoD product in Germany.
(696)    First, the Commission considers that neither Sky DACH alone nor the merged
         RTL/Sky DACH entity has a significant degree of market power on the German
         market for the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (and specifically not
         in the SVoD segment):
(697)    In the first place, the Parties’ individual and combined market shares on the
         relevant upstream markets and market segments are low:

(698)    In the second place, the Parties are constrained by the presence of various

(702)    In the first place, the Commission considers that various competing SVoD

(704)    In the second place, the Commission considers that content such as the one that

         for streaming time overall if Sky and WOW counted together, approximately 5%
         for streaming time for movies if Sky and WOW counted together and 4% for
         series in Q3 2025 if Sky and WOW counted together). (699)
(705)    In the third place, WOW Filme & Serien only accounts for a small proportion of
         most competing aggregators’ subscriber bases. Specifically, WOW Filme &

6.2.6.3.2.2.    No incentive to engage in input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Filme
                & Serien SVoD product in Germany
(712)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         will likely not have the incentive to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in
         particular competing aggregators to Sky DACH’s WOW Filme & Serien SVoD
         product in Germany.
(713)    First, the vertical link between Sky DACH’s WOW Filme & Serien SVoD
         product and Sky’s own aggregation offer is pre-existing and not merger specific.
         Sky DACH could, therefore, already pre-merger have pursued an input
         foreclosure strategy if it had considered that such a strategy would make economic
         sense.
(714)    This is supported by Sky DACH’s internal documents. [Reference to internal
         documents of Sky DACH]. (710) (711) (712)
(715)    The Commission considers the fact that WOW Filme & Serien is available to
         other aggregators as evidence that Sky DACH has at least in the past not engaged
         in input foreclosure concerning WOW Filme & Serien, thereby providing an
         indication of how much of an incentive RTL would have post-Transaction to
         change this position.
(716)    Second, in the scenario of a (partial) foreclosure of WOW Filme & Serien to
         competing aggregators, the Commission considers it unlikely that extra profits
         that result from a restriction of access to WOW Filme & Serien for rival
         aggregators outweigh the foregone wholesale profits from supplying WOW Filme
         & Serien to the aggregators, which makes un input foreclosure strategy
         unprofitable. The economic rationale for granting access to WOW Filme &
         Serien, and SVoD services more generally, for inclusion in a bundle of competing
         aggregators is to achieve a wider distribution as compared to the situation in
         which the SVoD service would only directly be available to consumers. (713) Pre-
         merger, RTL and Sky DACH hold market shares of [0-5]% and [5-10]%,
         respectively, in the paid AV retail market, indicating a limited direct reach. Post-
         merger, the combined entity would hold a market share of [5-10]%, which
         likewise reflects a limited direct reach. On the market segment for paid retail AV
         aggregation, the post-merger share equals the pre-merger share ([20-30]%)
         because only Sky DACH is active as an aggregator. Hence, a strategy consisting
         of foreclosing access to WOW Filme & Serien would entail a significant loss of
         distribution and, consequently, reduced access to consumers. Accordingly, the
         merged entity’s incentive to engage in foreclosure would not be materially
         strengthened due to the merger.

(717)    Third, RTL’s internal documents show that it intends to continue this strategy of

(718)    Fourth, the Commission considers that the merged entity would, post-
         Transaction, not be incentivised to engage in any input foreclosure strategy
         including for its WOW Filme & Serien SVoD product because it relies on the
         delivery/transmission services offered by competing aggregators for reaching end
         customers (see above paras. (691)-(692)).

6.2.6.3.2.3.    Conclusion
(719)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available to it, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential input foreclosure
         concerning WOW Filme & Serien stemming from the vertical relationship
         between the markets for (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in
         Germany (and specifically in the SVoD segment) (upstream), and (ii) the paid AV
         retail services market in Germany (and specifically in the aggregated AV services
         segment) (downstream).

        6.2.6.3.3.      No input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Live-Sport SVoD
                        product in Germany
(720)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose competing paid AV retail operators and in particular
         competing aggregators operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail
         services in Germany from obtaining Sky’s WOW Live-Sport SVoD product as
         input.

6.2.6.3.3.1.    No ability to engage in input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Live-

         the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in particular competing
         aggregators (operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail services in
         Germany) to Sky DACH’s WOW Live-Sport SVoD product in Germany.
(722)    First, the Commission considers that neither Sky DACH alone nor the merged
         RTL/Sky DACH entity has a significant degree of market power on the German
         market for the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (and specifically not
         in the SVoD segment):

(723)    In the first place, the Parties’ individual and combined market shares on the
         relevant upstream markets and market segments are low:
         (a) Sky DACH’s market share in 2024 on the market for the wholesale supply of
             TV channels/AV services in Germany was [0-5]%; RTL adds an additional
             [10-20]%, leading to a combined market share of [10-20]%. (720)
         (b) Sky DACH’s market share in 2024 on the market segment for the wholesale
             supply of SVoD services in Germany was [0-5]%; RTL adds an additional
             [5-10]%, leading to a combined segment share of [10-20]%. (721)
         (c) WOW Live-Sport had approximately […] subscribers in Germany in
             October 2025, representing a share of less than [0-5]% in the SVoD segment
             of AV retail based on subscribers. (722)
(724)    In the second place, the Parties are constrained by the presence of various
         competing SVoD offerings, including Netflix ([50-60]% market share in
         Germany in 2024), DAZN ([10-20]% market share in Germany in 2024);
         Paramount ([10-20]% market share in Germany in 2024); Disney ([5-10]%
         market share in Germany in 2024) as well as Apple, P7S1 and Warner. (723) There
         are no indications that these constraints will weaken post-Transaction. On the
         contrary, the Commission considers that competition in the market segment for
         SVoD services will remain strong, as for example indicated by Warner’s launch
         of its own SVoD service HBO Max in Germany in January 2026. (724)
(725)    In the third place, the Parties’ internal documents confirm that they are
         constrained by the aforementioned competing suppliers of SVoD services (please
         see above paragraph (666).
(726)    Specifically in relation to sports content, an internal Sky DACH Sports Rights
         Update refers to [reference to internal documents of Sky DACH]. (725)
(727)    In the fourth place, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that the
         Parties are constrained by various competing SVoD service providers and only a
         minority of respondents who expressed a view expect a negative impact of the
         Transaction on a market segment for the aggregation of AV services and the
         overall market for AV retail services (see above paragraph (667).
(728)    Second, the Commission considers for the following reasons that WOW Live-
         Sport does not constitute a particularly important input that competing paid AV
         retail operators and in particular competing aggregators need to carry for
         competing effectively in the German paid AV retail market:
(729)    In the first place, the Commission considers that an increasing number of
         competing SVoD services offer sports content such as the sports content offered
         by WOW Live-Sport. This includes DAZN, Ard & ZDF, DYN, Netflix,
         Paramount+ and HBO Max. In addition, aggregators such as DTAG and Amazon
         Prime acquire sports rights themselves.

         changing.” (727)

         increases through attractive live sports events. However, it is not clear with the

         must have but also more than a nice to have.” (728)

6.2.6.3.3.2.   No incentive to engage in input foreclosure concerning Sky’s WOW Live-
               Sport SVoD product in Germany
(738)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         will likely not have the incentive to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in
         particular competing aggregators to Sky DACH’s WOW Live-Sport SVoD
         product in Germany.
(739)    First, the vertical link between Sky DACH’s WOW Live-Sport SVoD product
         and Sky’s own aggregation offer is pre-existing and not merger specific. Sky
         DACH could, therefore, already pre-merger have pursued an input foreclosure
         strategy if it had considered that such a strategy would make economic sense. The
         Commission considers the fact that WOW Live-Sport is available on other
         aggregators (Amazon, Apple, DTAG, Waipu and O2) as evidence that Sky DACH
         has at least in the past not engaged in input foreclosure concerning WOW Live-
         Sport. In the Commission’s view, this provides an indication of how much of an
         incentive RTL would have post-Transaction to change this position.
(740)    Second, in the scenario of a refusal to supply WOW Live-Sport to competing
         aggregators or a degradation of the quality of the WOW Live-Sport product
         offered to competing aggregators post-merger, the Commission considers it
         unlikely that the extra profits that result from a restriction of access to WOW Live-
         Sport for rival aggregators outweigh the foregone wholesale profits from
         supplying WOW Live-Sport to the aggregators, which makes un input foreclosure
         strategy unprofitable. The change in the incentives for the merged entity involves
         the addition of RTL+, with a market share of [0-5]% and hence limited reach, in
         the paid AV retail market. Post-merger, the combined entity would hold a market
         share of [5-10]%, which also implies that direct access to consumers is limited.
         On the market segment for paid retail AV aggregation, the post-merger share is
         equal to the pre-merger share ([20-30]%) because RTL is not active as an
         aggregator. Given the low market share of the merged entity on the paid AV retail
         market and the fact that there is no change due to the merger on the paid AV retail
         market segment, it is unlikely that the merged entity would attract sufficient
         additional demand to its retail offerings to recoup the foregone wholesale profits
         on WOW Live-Sports. For the same reason, the merged entity is unlikely to have
         the incentive to degrade the quality of the content available on WOW Live-Sports
         for aggregators.
(741)    Third, the Commission considers that third-party distribution of WOW Live-
         Sport important for Sky DACH because a substantial and growing share of WOW
         Live-Sport’s subscribers and revenues originates from aggregators. According to
         Sky DACH, this share currently amounts to […] of its subscribers and revenues.
         In recent years, the revenue growth of WOW Live-Sport via aggregators


(743)   Form CO, Table 42.
                                              175
         [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. (744) (745) Against this
         backdrop, the Commission considers it unlikely that a complete or partial input
         foreclosure of Sky DACH’s WOW Live-Sport product would be economically
         viable.
(742)    Fourth, as set out in detail in paragraph (717) above, Sky DACH’s internal
         documents show that it pursues a strategy that relies on an expansion of third-
         party distribution of its SVoD services. Equally, RTL’s internal documents show
         that RTL plans to adopt the same strategy of expanding the third-party distribution
         of Sky DACH’s WOW products.
(743)    Fifth, the Commission considers that the merged entity would, post-Transaction,
         not be incentivised to engage in any input foreclosure strategy including for its
         WOW Live-Sport SVoD product because it relies on the delivery/transmission
         services offered by competing aggregators for reaching end customers (see above
         paras. (691)-(692)).

6.2.6.3.3.3.    Conclusion
(744)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available to it, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential input foreclosure
         concerning WOW Live-Sport stemming from the vertical relationship between
         the markets for (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in Germany
         (and specifically in the SVoD segment) (upstream), and (ii) the paid AV retail
         services market in Germany (and specifically in the aggregated AV services
         segment) (downstream).

        6.2.6.3.4.      No input foreclosure concerning a combination of the Parties’ SVoD
                        products in Germany
(745)    The Commission considers that the above input foreclosure analysis would not
         change it if were to consider a combination of the Parties’ SVoD products (i.e.,
         by combining RTL’s RTL+ and Sky DACH’s WOW Filme & Serien and/or Live-
         Sport products) as input for competitors’ downstream offering on the market for
         paid AV retail services and in particular on the segment for aggregated AV retail
         services in Germany.
(746)    First, the Parties’ position on the respective upstream wholesale supply markets
         and market segments does not change compared to the separate analyses
         conducted in sections 6.2.6.3.1 to 6.2.6.3.3.
(747)    Second, for the results of the analysis to change for a combined offering, there
         would need to be sufficient customer interest for such a combined offer. As set
         out separately above in paras. (709) and (732), only […] million users subscribe
         to both RTL+ and WOW Filme & Serien and only […] million users subscribe to
         both RTL+ and WOW Live-Sport (out of a total of 66.9 million potential

(748)    Against this backdrop, the Commission considers that there is not a sufficient

         different outcome of the above input foreclosure analysis if it were to consider a
         combination of the Parties’ SVoD products.

        6.2.6.3.5.      No input foreclosure concerning the Parties’ linear TV channel

(749)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose competing paid AV retail operators and in particular
         competing aggregators operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail

6.2.6.3.5.1.    No ability to engage in input foreclosure concerning the Parties’ linear TV
                channel offering in Germany
(750)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction not have
         the ability to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality degradation) foreclose
         the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in particular competing
         aggregators (operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail services in
         Germany) to the Parties’ linear TV channels in Germany.
(751)    First, the Commission considers that neither RTL nor Sky DACH have a
         significant degree of market power on the German market for the wholesale
         supply of TV channels/AV services (and specifically not in the segments for the
         supply of basic or premium pay-TV channels):
(752)    In the first place, the Parties’ individual and combined market shares on the
         relevant upstream markets and market segments are generally low, and the
         Transaction does only lead to an addition of market share in the supply of
         premium pay-TV channels:
         (a) As set out above, the Parties’ combined market share on the market for the
             wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in Germany is [10-20]%
             (RTL: [10-20]%; Sky DACH: [0-5]%). (747)
         (b) On the market segment for the supply of basic pay-TV channels in Germany
             overall, RTL’s market share in 2024 was [20-30]%, while Sky DACH is not
             active in this segment. (748) Only in a narrower segment for the supply of
             basic pay-TV channels with “kids” content, RTL’s market share exceeds 30%
             ([40-50]%), while Sky DACH while Sky DACH is not active in this
             segment. (749)

         (c) On the market segment for the supply of premium pay-TV channels in
             Germany, RTL’s market share in 2024 was [5-10]%, and Sky DACH adds an

(753)    In the second place, the Parties are constrained by the presence of various
         competing linear TV channel suppliers.
(754)    In the supply of basic pay-TV channels in Germany overall, RTL is constrained
         by P7S1 ([30-40]%), ARD and ZDF ([20-30]%), RTL2 (7.1%) and others
         ([10-20]%). (751)
(755)    Specifically in relation to the supply of basic TV channels for kids in Germany,
         RTL is constrained by include offerings from the public broadcasters’ channels
         KIKA, Disney’s the Disney Channel, and Nickelodeon (Viacom), alongside
         premium pay-TV channels such as Warner’s channels Cartoon Network and
         Cartoonito or Viacom’s channels Nick Jr and Nicktoon. (752) In addition, the
         Commission considers that competing SVoD service providers also offer a wide-
         variety of “kids content” which can be selected by choosing “kids” in the user
         profile of each service. Competing SVoD services offering dedicated kids content
         in Germany include: Amazon Prime, Apple TV, Disney +, HBO Max, Joyn,
         Netflix, Paramount + and YouTube. (753)
(756)    In the supply of premium pay-TV channels in Germany, the Parties are
         constrained by Warner ([50-60]%), Disney ([5-10]%), P7S1 ([5-10]%), A&E
         ([0-5]%) as well as Highview, HD+ and others. (754)
(757)    There are no indications that these constraints will weaken post-Transaction.
(758)    In the third place, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that the
         Parties are constrained by various competing providers of linear TV channels. The
         majority of respondents to the Commission’s market investigation who expressed
         a view confirmed that there are sufficient alternatives to the Parties in the market
         segments for the wholesale supply of linear TV channels, the wholesale supply of
         paid TV channels (755) and the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV
         channels. (756) In addition, only a minority of respondents who expressed a view
         expects a negative impact of the Transaction on market segments for the
         wholesale acquisition of TV channels/AV services, the aggregation of AV
         services and the supply of retail TV/AV services to end users. (757)
(759)    Individual respondents confirmed that the merged entity would not have any
         ability to engage in input foreclosure for their linear TV channels offering at
         wholesale level:
         (a)    One respondent explained: “Post-transaction, RTL and Sky DACH would
                not have the ability to stop supplying or reduce the quality of their TV
                channels or AV services to wholesale purchasers. Neither party controls

(760)    Second, the Commission considers for the following reasons that neither Party’s
         linear TV channels constitute a particularly important input that competing paid
         AV retail operators and in particular competing aggregators need to carry for
         competing effectively in the German AV retail market.
(761)    In the first place, at the retail level, RTL’s TV channels have a viewership share
         of [20-30]% in Germany and [10-20]% in Austria. Individually, RTL’s largest TV
         channel holds only an [5-10]% viewership share in Germany and [0-5]% in
         Austria. (761) Sky DACH’s viewership share is [0-5]% in Germany and lower in
         Austria. (762)
(762)    In the second place, the Commission considers that there are sufficient alternative
         channels offering general entertainment content such as the one offered by RTL.
         Public broadcasters (ARD, ZDF and their affiliated channels) account for
         approximately [40-50]% of total TV viewership (compared to [20-30]% of all of
         RTL’s channels together). (763) In addition, other significant suppliers of general
         entertainment channels (P7S1, Warner Bros. Discovery, Paramount and others)
         as well as international channels remain available (see above paragraph (665)).
(763)    This is confirmed by the Commission’s market investigation:
         (a)    The majority of respondents who expressed a view confirmed that there are
                sufficient alternatives beyond RTL and Sky DACH for the wholesale supply
                of TV/AV channels and services with general entertainment content. (764)
         (b)    One respondent referred to “the size and relevance of leading global SVOD
                Services such as Amazon Prime, Netflix, Disney+ or Paramount Plus” for
                general entertainment content. (765)
         (c)    Another respondent simply stated that “there are multiple suppliers”. (766)

         (d)    Another respondent explained that “there is no such thing as ‘must-have’
                content”. (767)
         (e)    And another respondent referred to a “sufficient variety of content on other
                services”. (768)
         (f)    Another respondent to the Commission’s market investigation explained in
                more detail: “General‑entertainment content is highly substitutable, and
                numerous alternative providers are available. Therefore, none of RTL’s or
                Sky’s non‑sports content is essential for downstream retail TV
                providers.” (769) The same respondent also added: “Even if the merged
                entity were to remove, restrict or degrade access to its non‑sports AV
                content, aggregators could readily source equivalent content from
                alternative providers and continue to compete viably. The German AV
                market offers numerous substitutes — including public broadcasters, other
                private channel groups, international thematic channels, pay‑TV providers
                and emerging FAST‑channel ecosystems — and none of RTL/Sky’s
                non‑sports content constitutes a must‑have asset.” (770)
(764)    In the third place, the Commission notes that RTL does not own most of its key
         formats. Most of RTL’s reality formats and other popular content are
         commissioned, and the IP is owned by major international production studios
         (e.g., “Der Bachelor” franchise is owned by Warner). This means that these
         formats can easily move to competitors in case RTL’s reach decreases and content
         owners would be incentivised to change TV channels, to safeguard the popularity
         of their content. (771)
(765)    Third, specifically in relation to RTL’s linear TV channels, the Commission notes
         that RTL is prohibited by law from limiting the supply or worsening the terms of
         its FTA and basic pay-TV channels for wholesale purchasers. According to
         § 87(5) of the Urheberrechtsgesetz, (772) broadcasters must establish
         retransmission contracts on reasonable terms, which applies to RTL’s channels
         (but not to Sky DACH’s). Thus, RTL cannot legally implement a foreclosure
         strategy for these channels. (773)
(766)    Fourth, specifically in relation to Sky DACH’s linear TV channels, the
         Commission notes that Sky DACH does not offer its core channels and packages
         as a wholesale product. Only a small number of Sky DACH channels are made
         available to wholesale purchasers generating EUR […] million, or less than
         [0-5]% of Sky DACH’s total revenues. (774) As a consequence, the Commission

                                                180
         considers that Sky DACH’s core product can as such not be subject to input
         foreclosure at wholesale level.
(767)    Fifth, and relation to the concern that the merged entity could post-Transaction
         shift content away from RTL’s FTA offering to Sky’s premium pay-TV offering,
         the Commission notes that the merged entity is in some instances contractually
         restricted from transferring significant content to Sky's pay-TV platform. Under
         some agreements, RTL has only the FTA right and is contractually prohibited
         from exploiting the content on pay-TV or SVoD services. (775) For entertainment
         licences (e.g. films, series), rights are frequently split between FTA, pay-TV and
         streaming, [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. In addition, the
         Commission expects that rights holders would resist any such shift as they have a
         strong interest in maximising the reach of their content and would be likely to
         license it instead to one of RTL's competitors rather than accept restricted
         distribution on a pay-TV platform with less customer reach. (776)
(768)    Therefore, the Commission considers that the merged entity lacks the ability to
         engage in an input foreclosure strategy concerning the Parties’ linear basic pay-
         TV and premium pay-TV channels vis-à-vis competing paid AV retail services
         providers and in particular aggregators.

6.2.6.3.5.2.    No incentive to engage in input foreclosure concerning the Parties’ linear
                TV channel offering in Germany
(769)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         would not have the incentive to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in
         particular competing aggregators to the Parties’ linear basic pay-TV and premium
         pay-TV channels in Germany.
(770)    First, RTL operates under an FTA and basic pay-TV business model. RTL is
         advertisement financed and relies on reaching as many customers as possible to
         increase the customer reach of its advertisers and hence revenues generated from
         advertisements placed on RTL’s linear channels.
(771)    More specifically, the Commission notes that RTL, if it were to engage in input
         foreclosure of its linear TV channel offering would on the one hand have to expect
         the loss of wholesale licensing revenues (RTL earns €[…] million annually from
         wholesale distribution of its TV channels) (777). On the other hand, RTL would
         have to expect a loss of advertising revenues. (778)
(772)    The Commission considers that, for a foreclosure strategy to be economically
         viable, the merged entity would need to more than double Sky DACH’s current
         revenue (779). The Commission does not consider it likely that such a strategy
         would be successful for the following reasons:
(773)    In the first place, audience profiles differ: Sky DACH targets a niche audience
         willing to pay a premium for specific content like sports, while RTL focuses on
         broad-appeal FTA and basic pay-TV programming aimed at a mass audience.

(775)   For example, RTL's NFL rights are predominantly FTA (the pay-TV rights are held by DAZN), and
        [business/commercial information of RTL] (cf. Notifying Party’s response to RFI 15, para. 210).
(776)   Notifying Party’s response to RFI 15, para. 210.
(777)   Notifying Party’s response to RFI 15, para. 192.
(778)   RTL’s advertising revenues in 2024 were EUR […] million, cf. Notifying Party’s response to
        RFI 15, para. 193.
(779)   EUR 1 205 million in 2024, cf. Notifying Party’s response to RFI 15, para. 192.
                                                 181
(774)    In the second place, there would be a significant difference in price: major
         wholesalers of RTL’s channels offer TV packages for €5–15 per month compared
         to Sky DACH’s €45 per month. It is uncertain if many RTL viewers would pay
         this premium to access RTL content.
(775)    In the third place, the merged entity would face a retaliation risk should it engage
         in input foreclosure. As set out above (see para. (691)), the Parties rely on cable
         network or telecom operators (who may be competing at AV retail level) for
         reaching end customers. Restricting the supply of RTL channels to these operators
         could lead to retaliation, such as deprioritising or delisting.
(776)    Second, in relation to Sky DACH specifically, the Commission notes that its
         wholesale TV channel supply activities are already very limited (see above para.
         (766). The fact that Sky DACH has concluded only very few wholesale
         distribution agreements illustrates that there is no incentive for the merged entity
         to discontinue these arrangements.
(777)    Third, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that the merged entity
         would not have an incentive to engage in input foreclosure of its wholesale TV
         channel supply offering. Out of those respondents who expressed a view, the
         majority states that there are no such incentives. (780)
(778)    More specifically, respondents explained:
         (a)    “Otherwise channels and services would not be bought or watched.” (781)
         (b)    “Competition in Germany is so strong that we do not believe that as a result
                of the merger the market position of the combined entity will be able to
                afford a degradation of the quality or the terms and conditions of their
                supply of AV content.” (782)
         (c)    “RTL and Sky DACH would have no incentive to refuse supply or degrade
                the quality of their wholesale TV/AV channel delivery. As they do not
                possess the ability to foreclose wholesale purchasers and rely on wide
                distribution for reach and revenue, such strategies would not be
                economically rational or feasible.” (783)
(779)    Fourth, the Commission considers that [business/commercial information of
         RTL] confirms a continued and increasing reliance on third-party distribution (as
         opposed to a decrease in revenues generated from third-party distribution in case
         of input foreclosure).
(780)    [Business/commercial information of RTL]. (784)
(781)    The Commission considers, therefore, that the merged entity would not have any
         incentive to change the current business model of RTL and its TV channels by
         limiting its distribution channels and engaging in input foreclosure.

         financed via advertising revenues and hence need to reach the widest audience
         possible. In 2024, the revenues achieved by RTL with distribution fees for Super

6.2.6.3.5.3.    Conclusion
(785)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available to it, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential input foreclosure
         concerning the Parties’ linear TV channels stemming from the vertical
         relationship between the markets for (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV
         services in Germany (upstream), and (ii) the paid AV retail services market in
         Germany (and specifically in the aggregated AV services segment) (downstream).

        6.2.6.3.6.      No customer foreclosure concerns in Germany
(786)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially foreclose competing
         wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV services operating on the corresponding
         upstream market in Germany by restricting access to the downstream market for
         the provision of paid AV retail services and specifically the retail supply of
         aggregated AV services in Germany.

6.2.6.3.6.1.    No ability to engage in total or partial customer foreclosure
(787)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the ability to
         engage in foreclosure by restricting access to the downstream market for paid AV
         retail services (nor for any of its segments including specifically for aggregated
         AV services) for the following reasons.
(788)    First, the Commission considers the merged entity does not have a significant
         degree of market power on the market for paid AV retail services in Germany.
         (a) The merged entity’s market share on the market for paid AV retail services
             in Germany is [10-20]% (RTL: [0-5]% (788); Sky DACH: [10-20]%). Various
             competitors are active in this market, including: Vodafone ([20-30]%);
             Netflix ([10-20]%); DTAG ([5-10]%); DAZN ([5-10]%); Disney ([5-10]%);
             Amazon ([0-5]%) as well as other competitors such as Paramount, HD+ and
             YouTube. (789) The Commission considers that, post-Transaction, all of these

              competitors will remain important alternative acquirers of TV channels/AV
              services in Germany.
         (b) Equally, the merged entity’s market share in a market segment for pay-TV
             retail services in Germany is [20-30]%. (790) Various competitors are and will
             remain active in this segment including Vodafone ([40-50]%) and DTAG
             ([10-20]%). (791) In the segment for pay-TV retail services in Germany
             distributed by satellite, the merged entity’s share is significant ([70-80]%),
             but competitors including HD+ ([10-20]%) and DTAG ([5-10]%) are and
             will remain active also in this narrower segment. (792) The Commission
             considers that this overstates the importance of Sky DACH’s market
             penetration in the segment for pay-TV retail services distributed via satellite.
             This is because the actual number of households with the possibility to
             receive satellite TV services that subscribe to Sky DACH’s services is
             significantly lower, i.e., [10-20]% in Germany in 2024. (793) In addition, even
             if pay-TV retail services distributed by satellite are considered as a separate
             market, these services are subject to considerable out-of-market constraints
             by pay-TV retail services distributed via different infrastructures. In any
             event, the Transaction does not change the existing market position of Sky
             DACH in the German pay-TV market nor in any of its segments, as RTL is
             not active in this segment.
         (c) Along similar lines, the merged entity’s market share in a segment for
             aggregated AV services in Germany is [20-30]%. Various competitors are
             and will remain active in this segment including Vodafone ([40-50]%),
             DTAG ([10-20]%), Amazon ([5-10]%) as well as other aggregators such as
             YouTube, HD+ and Waipu TV. (794) In any event, the Transaction does not
             change the existing market position of Sky DACH in the German aggregated
             AV services market, as RTL is not active in this segment.
         (d) The majority of market participants who expressed a view in the market
             investigation confirmed that there are sufficient alternatives to the Parties in
             the market for the retail supply of paid AV services in Germany as well as in
             the retail supply of aggregated AV services in Germany. (795)
(789)    Second, Second, in addition to RTL’s channels, Sky DACH is already pre-
         Transaction currently an acquirer of several third-party TV channels / AV content.
         This includes various linear TV channels such as those supplied by RTL, P7S1,
         Disney, Warner or Paramount. The carriage agreements for these channels will

(791)    The Commission, therefore, considers that the merged entity cannot, even if it
         wanted to, post-Transaction stop purchasing these competing TV channels/AV
         services prior to their expiry.
(792)    Third, the majority of respondents to the market investigation who expressed a
         view consider that the Transaction would have a positive or neutral impact on
         their business. (800) Similarly, when asked about the impact on the markets for the
         provision of retail TV/AV services to end users and for the aggregation of AV
         services, the majority of market participants that expressed a view consider that
         the Transaction will have either a positive or neutral impact. (801)
(793)    Therefore, the Commission considers that there are sufficient economic
         alternatives for the competing upstream providers of TV channels/AV services to
         have adequate access to customers, without incurring significantly higher costs,
         and that the merged entity lacks the ability to engage in a customer foreclosure
         strategy.

6.2.6.3.6.2.    No incentive to engage in total or partial customer foreclosure
(794)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         will likely not have the incentive to engage in the foreclosure of access to the
         downstream market for paid AV retail services including specifically aggregated
         AV services in Germany by reducing its purchases from the Parties’ upstream
         rivals on the market for the wholesale supply of TV/AV services channels in
         Germany for the following reasons.
(795)    First, Sky DACH’s business model at retail level is based on Sky DACH seeking
         to have a broad portfolio of channels to justify its role as a platform and access
         provider vis-à-vis its subscriber base. (802)
(796)    In the first place, the Commission considers that, based on Sky DACH’s viewing
         statistics, [business/commercial information of Sky DACH]. (803)
(797)    In the second place, [business/commercial information of Sky DACH] show that
         it is in a competitive disadvantage in relation to its channel offering compared to
         its competitors.
(798)    For instance, [reference to internal documents of Sky DACH]. (804) (805) (806)

(799)    The Commission considers that this document shows that Sky DACH has an
         incentive to widen its wholesale supplier base (rather than to narrow it) to close
         the [reference to internal documents of Sky DACH].
(800)    The Commission, therefore, considers that the merged entity would, post-
         Transaction, not have any business incentive to only acquire and show RTL’s and
         Sky DACH’s own content and stop carrying competing suppliers’ content.
(801)    Second, and as explained above, the upstream presence of RTL and Sky DACH
         in the wholesale supply of TV channels and/or AV services in Germany is limited,
         and, therefore, the current incentives of Sky DACH to purchase TV channels or
         AV services from upstream suppliers will remain largely unchanged post-
         Transaction. More particularly, the Commission observes that the Parties would
         bear the costs of a hypothetical customer foreclosure strategy, because consumers
         who prefer watching all content including other general entertainment channels,
         sports content or indeed other SVoD services on one aggregator platform would
         likely move to competitors of the merged entity that offer a full portfolio of TV
         channels and/or AV services.
(802)    Third, if the merged entity were to engage in full or partial customer foreclosure
         post-Transaction by either only carrying RTL and Sky channels or by reducing its
         offer to only the most important channels of upstream suppliers, the Commission
         considers that many of these suppliers could retaliate at other market levels. For
         instance, some wholesale suppliers of TV channels are vertically integrated into
         AV content production and licensing. Warner, Viacom/Paramount, Disney, as
         well as ARD and ZDF, are licensors or sub-licensors of AV content purchased by
         Sky DACH. If the merged entity were to unreasonably cease purchasing the
         wholesale channel offerings of these suppliers, they could in turn restrict access
         to attractive AV content. (807) Equally, various wholesale suppliers offer not only
         one but multiple TV channels (including ARD, ORF, ZDF, P7S1, Viacom,
         Warner, Disney, A&E and High View). (808) If the merged entity pursued a partial
         foreclosure strategy by carrying only some (such as the most attractive channels)
         from a supplier's bundle, the supplier could respond by withdrawing its entire
         portfolio.

6.2.6.3.6.3.    Conclusion
(803)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available to it, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential customer foreclosure
         stemming from the vertical relationship between the markets/market segments for
         (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV content in Germany (upstream), and
         (ii) the provision of paid AV retail services and particular of retail pay-TV and
         aggregated AV services in Germany (downstream).

        6.2.6.3.7.       Commission’s analysis does not change for other potentially
                         relevant geographic areas
(804)    The Commission has no indications that the results of its competitive assessment
         concerning the vertical effects of the Transaction in relation to relationship
         between (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services (upstream),

         expressed a view confirmed that all of their responses would be the same if
         considering solely Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg and South Tyrol in
         relation to the retail supply of AV services. (818)

6.2.6.3.7.1.   No input foreclosure concerning RTL’s wholesale supply of basic pay-TV
               offering in Austria
(808)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose competing paid AV retail operators and in particular
         competing aggregators operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail
         services in Austria from obtaining RTL’s basic pay-TV channels as input.

6.2.6.3.7.1.1. No ability to engage in input foreclosure concerning RTL’s wholesale basic
               pay-TV offering in Austria
(809)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will post-Transaction not have
         the ability to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality degradation) foreclose
         the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in particular competing
         aggregators (operating on the downstream market for paid AV retail services in
         Austria) to RTL’s basic pay-TV channels in Austria.
(810)    First, the Commission considers that RTL (Sky DACH is not active in this
         segment) does not a significant degree of market power on the Austrian market
         for the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels (and specifically not in the
         segments for the supply of basic pay-TV channels with (i) general entertainment
         or (ii) news content.
(811)    In the first place, RTL’s market share on the relevant upstream market segments
         in the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels are not significantly above
         30%, and the Transaction does not lead to an addition of market share in these
         segments, as Sky DACH is not active in these segments in Austria:
         (a) As set out above, the Parties’ combined market share on the market for the
             wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services in Austria is [10-20]% (RTL:
             [10-20]%; Sky DACH: [0-5]%). (819)
         (b) On the market segment for the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels in
             Austria overall, RTL’s market share in 2024 was [30-40]%, while Sky DACH
             is not active in this segment. (820)
         (c) In the narrower segments for the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels
             with “general entertainment” content in Austria, RTL’s market share is
             [30-40]%; and for the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels with
             “news” content, RTL’s market share exceeds [30-40]%. (821)
(812)    In the second place, RTL is constrained by the presence of various competing
         suppliers of basic pay-TV channels in Austria.

(815)    Second, the Commission considers that RTL’s basic pay-TV channels do not
         constitute a particularly important input that competing paid AV retail operators
         and in particular competing aggregators need to carry for competing effectively
         in the Austrian AV retail market. This is evidenced by the low viewership share
         of RTL’s TV channels in Austria (see above para. (761)), compared to the
         significantly higher viewership share of the public broadcasters operating in
         Austria of approximately [40-50]%. In addition, other significant suppliers of
         general entertainment channels and news content remain available (see above
         paragraph (812)).
(816)    Third, the Commission notes that RTL is prohibited by law from limiting the
         supply or worsening the terms of its FTA and basic pay-TV channels for
         wholesale purchasers. According to §§ 59b(2) and 59b(3) UrhG (AT), (830)
         broadcasters must establish retransmission contracts on reasonable terms, which
         applies to RTL’s channels. Thus, RTL cannot legally implement a foreclosure
         strategy for these channels. (831)
(817)    Therefore, the Commission considers that the merged entity lacks the ability to
         engage in an input foreclosure strategy concerning RTL’s basic pay-channel
         offering vis-à-vis competing paid AV retail services providers and in particular
         aggregators.

6.2.6.3.7.1.2. No incentive to engage in input foreclosure concerning RTL’s wholesale
               basic pay-TV offering in Austria
(818)    For the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged entity
         would not have the incentive to either fully or partially (e.g., by way of quality
         degradation) foreclose the access of competing paid AV retail operators and in
         particular competing aggregators to RTL’s basic pay-TV channels in Austria.
(819)    First, RTL operates under an FTA and basic pay-TV business model. RTL is
         advertisement financed and relies on reaching as many customers as possible to
         increase the customer reach of its advertisers and hence revenues generated from
         advertisements placed on RTL’s linear channels.
(820)    More specifically, the Commission notes that RTL, if it were to engage in input
         foreclosure of its basic pay-TV channel offering would on the one hand have to
         expect the loss of wholesale licensing revenues (RTL earns €[…] annually from
         wholesale distribution of its basic pay-TV channels in Austria) (832). On the other
         hand, RTL would have to expect a loss of advertising revenues. (833)
(821)    The Commission considers that, for a foreclosure strategy to be economically
         viable, the merged entity would need to almost double Sky DACH’s current

         would be successful for the following reasons:
(822)    In the first place, audience profiles differ: Sky DACH targets a niche audience
         willing to pay a premium for specific content like sports, while RTL focuses on
         broad-appeal FTA and basic pay-TV programming aimed at a mass audience.
(823)    In the second place, the merged entity would face a retaliation risk should it
         engage in input foreclosure. As set out above (see para. (691)), the Parties rely on
         cable network or telecom operators (who may be competing at AV retail level)
         for reaching end customers. Restricting the supply of RTL channels to these
         operators could lead to retaliation, such as deprioritising or delisting.
(824)    Third, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that the merged entity
         would not have an incentive to engage in input foreclosure of its wholesale TV
         channel supply offering. Out of those respondents who expressed a view, the
         majority states that there are no such incentives. (835) The results equally apply to
         the Austrian market. (836)
(825)    Fourth, the Commission considers that [business/commercial information of Sky
         DACH] confirms a continued and increasing reliance on third-party distribution
         (as opposed to a decrease in revenues generated from third-party distribution in
         case of input foreclosure) (see above paras (779) to (781)). RTL submits that is
         has never removed a main FTA or basic pay TV channel from any wholesale
         purchaser since it commenced its operations. (837) (837)

6.2.6.3.7.1.3. Conclusion
(826)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the results of the market
         investigation and the evidence and information available to it, the Commission
         concludes that the Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its
         compatibility with the internal market as a result of potential input foreclosure
         concerning RTL’s basic pay-TV channels stemming from the vertical relationship
         between the markets for (i) the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels in
         Austria (including in particular general entertainment and news content)
         (upstream), and (ii) the paid AV retail services market in Austria (and specifically
         in the aggregated AV services segment) (downstream).

6.2.6.4. Conclusion
(840)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
         the evidence and information available to it, the Commission concludes the
         Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the
         internal market in the vertical relationship between the markets/market segments
         for (i) the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV content (including the segments
         for the wholesale supply of SVoDs, basic pay-TV channels and premium pay-TV
         channels) in Germany and other German-speaking Territories (upstream), and (ii)
         the provision of paid AV retail services (including the segments for retail pay-TV
         and aggregated AV services) in Germany and other German-speaking Territories.

6.2.7.   Vertical relationship between the markets for the acquisition of AV content
         (upstream) and the markets for the wholesale supply of TV channels/AV services,
         and the markets for paid AV retail (downstream)
(841)    The Transaction gives rise to vertical links between the Parties’ acquisition of AV
         content in a number of potential segments for commissioned non-scripted other
         AV content and pre-produced AV content in Austria, Germany and German-
         speaking Territories and the Parties’ wholesale supply of TV channels/AV
         services in a number of potential downstream segments in Austria, Germany, as
         well as in all German-speaking Territories.
(842)    Moreover, the Transaction also gives rise to a vertical link between the Parties’
         acquisition of AV content in a number of potential segments for commissioned
         non-scripted other AV content and pre-produced AV content in Austria,
                                             194
         Germany, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg as well as in all German-speaking
         Territories and the Parties’ paid AV retail in the potential downstream segments
         for aggregation and supply of paid AV retail in Austria, Germany, Liechtenstein,
         Luxembourg as well as in all German-speaking Territories.

6.2.7.1. Notifying Party’s views
(843)    The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in an
         input foreclosure strategy for the following reasons.
(844)    First, in their role as acquirers, the Parties do not have direct influence on the
         availability of AV content; this power lies with production studios and licensors
         of AV content. (844)
(845)    Second, licensors split licenses between several licensees to maximise revenue
         (e.g., the Bundesliga is licenced to Sky DACH and DAZN) or offer several
         packages in tenders (e.g., UEFA splits media rights for Europa League and
         Conference League into two packages). The Notifying Party submits that it
         considers implausible that the merged entity would bid on several packages, due
         to increasing prices and the Parties’ financial difficulties. Therefore, competitors
         do not depend on the merged entity for the access to any AV content. (845)
(846)    Third, the merged entity depends on AV content to be supplied by original
         licensors. Moreover, it will not be able to raise competitors’ costs above market
         value, significantly increase investments post-merger or pay higher prices. The
         Parties submit that they could not win a “bidding war” due to their size and global
         footprint. For instance, in recent UEFA tenders, neither RTL nor Sky DACH
         could compete against other established players (e.g., DAZN, Amazon Prime
         Video or new market entrants such as Paramount+). (846)
(847)    Fourth, the merged entity would not be able to focus a significant portion of its
         budget on “most-appealing” content, [business/commercial information of the
         Parties]. Moreover, the Parties face strong financial restrictions compared to their
         competitors on a global scale (e.g., Netflix global budget is […] times larger than
         RTL’s). (847)
(848)    Fifth, the Parties’ market share in the market for acquisition of commissioned AV
         entertainment content amounts to [10-20]% in Germany and only exceeds 30% in
         certain subsegments which their delineation does not reflect the market realities,
         as the overall market for the acquisition of commissioned AV content must be
         taken into account to assess an alleged buying power of the Parties. The Parties’
         combined market share in the market for acquisition of pre-produced AV content
         amounts to [20-30]% in relation to film content and [10-20]% in relation to other
         AV content in German-speaking Territories (and similarly in other national
         markets). Moreover, although the Parties’ market share exceeds 30% the
         acquisition of first window for non-sport AV content and first window for films
         and second window for other AV content in German-speaking Territories, the
         Parties do not agree on such a market delineation. (848) Regarding the acquisition
         of sports content and several of its potential segments, the Parties’ market share
         is above 30%; however, the Parties face intense competition, and their market

(844)   Form CO, para. 2196.
(845)   Form CO, para. 2197.
(846)   Form CO, para. 2198.
(847)   Form CO, paras 2199 and 2201.
(848)   Form CO, para. 2200.
                                             195
         shares are subject to constant change due to regular tender procedures. In this
         context, market shares are not very meaningful in bidding markets because they
         often reflect past contracts instead of the current competitive ability of the market
         participants. (849)
(849)    Sixth, the Parties lack the incentive to pursue such strategy, as expenditure for
         additional AV content could only be compensated by additional revenue in the
         downstream market (i.e., a price increase for their wholesale products), which
         would not be feasible. An increase of the wholesale price would result in acquirers
         dropping the Parties’ channels and SVoD services or in price increases, which in
         turn would result in customers switching to other AV products. (850)
(850)    Seventh, the pursue of such strategy would not impact the downstream market, as
         competitors on the wholesale level have many alternatives to invest in, the merged
         entity would not be able to acquire all or even a large part of the most appealing
         AV entertainment content and most large production studios are vertically
         integrated offering their own SVoD services. Moreover, specifically for the sports
         content segment, the markets are characterised by tender procedures, and the
         Parties face strong competitors (e.g., DAZN). (851)
(851)    The arguments submitted by the Notifying Party in relation to the vertical
         relationship between the acquisition of AV content (upstream) and wholesale
         supply of TV channels/AV services (downstream), as set out above, are also
         applicable to the vertical relationship between the acquisition of AV content
         (upstream) and paid AV retail (downstream).

6.2.7.2. Commission’s assessment
(852)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will, post-Transaction, neither
         have the ability nor the incentive to foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of
         TV channels/AV services or paid AV retail operators in the potential downstream
         segments for aggregation and supply of paid AV retail in Germany.
(853)    First, the Commission considers that the merged entity will, post-Transaction, not
         have the ability to foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV
         services or paid AV retail operators.
(854)    In the first place, the Commission notes that the Parties are constrained by the
         presence of various strong competitors (e.g., in January 2026, the Netflix-Sony
         licencing deal was announced, under which Sony’s theatrical feature films will,
         on an exclusive basis, stream on Netflix worldwide; (852) also in 2026, Warner
         Bros. Discovery launched HBO Max in Germany and Austria, terminating its
         agreement with Sky DACH; (853) and in April 2026, Disney+ announced the
         launch of ESPN in Europe), (854) there is increasing competitive pressure from
         global streaming platforms (such as Netflix, Amazon, etc.) and there are low
         barriers to entry.

         competitors' access to acquire AV content in Germany as the majority of
         respondents did not have a favourable or unfavourable opinion in this regard. (856)
         Additionally, some respondents consider that the merged entity will not have the
         market power to engage in such a strategy. More specifically, a respondent stated
         that: “Even with their market share they wouldn't be the biggest player (s-VOD),
         so probably their bargaining power will not be strong enough” and another
         respondent explained that: “The position of the combined entity will not be such
         that it changes the competitive situation for the acquisition of AV content.” (857)
(856)    In the second place, according to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, input
         foreclosure may raise competition problems only if it concerns an important input
         for the downstream product. (858) As indicated in Section 6.2.3, the Commission
         considers that it seems unlikely that the Parties’ content can be considered an
         important input and neither Party is a significant supplier of AV content in the
         German-speaking Territories. The Parties currently make their own AV content
         only sporadically available for inclusion in competing TV channels or retail TV
         services. Their combined market shares in pre-produced AV content are under
         [0-10]% in any plausible market subsegment and many other suppliers exist. (859)
(857)    In the third place, according to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, the
         merged entity would only have the ability to foreclose downstream competitors
         if, by reducing access to its own upstream products or services, it could negatively
         affect the overall availability of inputs for the downstream market. (860) In this
         case, competitors in the downstream markets (i.e., wholesale suppliers of TV
         channels/AV services or paid AV retail operators) do not depend on the merged
         entity for the access to AV content. Licensors are normally the ones that decide
         who obtains and in what terms the AV rights (either by tenders or by direct
         negotiation) and there are enough alternatives in the market to choose from.
(858)    Second, for the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged
         entity will, post-Transaction, not have the incentive to foreclose competing
         wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV services or paid AV retail operators, as
         foreclosure would not be profitable.
(859)    Most market participants did not know if the merged entity will have incentive or
         considered it would not have. However, a few respondents expressed concerns in
         relation to the incentives of the merged party to acquire more AV content in order
         to: “secure differentiated content offerings or to reinforce the attractiveness of the
         merged entity’s distribution platforms” (861) or increase the prices of the merged

        D.B.C.1.

        D.B.C.1.1.

(859)   Annex 8 to the Form CO, and Form CO para. 1989.
