(861)   Replies to Questionnaire “04_Wholesale and Retail TV channels/AV services (Phase 1)”, question
        D.B.C.2.1.
                                                 197
         party’s AV retail services. (862) In contrast, another respondent stated that: “we do
         not think that the combined entity can afford to engage in such strategies because
         the market position will not increase sufficiently.” (863) Nonetheless, the
         Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the incentive to engage
         in such a foreclosure strategy.
(860)    In the first place, the merged entity will probably not be incentivised to secure
         additional AV content to reinforce the attractiveness of the merged entity’s TV
         channels or AV retail. Expending more in the acquisition of AV content will
         probably result in price increases downstream, which would probably result in
         customers dropping the merged entity’s products.
(861)    In the second place, wholesale acquirers of TV channels have enough alternatives
         in the market to switch to (e.g., major competitors are the public broadcasters,
         P7S1, RTL2, but also streaming services such as Netflix or DAZN, Paramount
         and Warner).
(862)    In the third place, in the case of AV retail, end customers will have a wide variety
         of choices to switch to (e.g., streaming platforms). For the same reasons as above,
         the merged entity will not be incentivised to raise its prices downstream, even if
         its content is more appealing.
(863)    Third, the Commission considers that the markets for AV entertainment content
         in the German-speaking Territories have a sufficient volume of available content.
         Even if the merged entity increases its investment in entertainment, competitors
         at both the wholesale and retail levels have enough alternatives to remain
         competitive. The Commission considers that it is unlikely that the merged entity
         could exclusively acquire a significant share of appealing content, as most large
         production studios are vertically integrated and offer their own subscription
         SVoD services. For instance, in the sports content sector, tender procedures
         promote competition and ensure a steady supply of sports rights. Licensors can
         address any increase in the bargaining power of the merged entity, while
         competitors like DAZN will remain viable alternatives post-Transaction.
         Although live sports boost media consumption, they are not essential for AV
         services, and competing wholesalers will continue to be competitive, even with
         increased investment in sports content by the merged entity. Additionally, RTL
         and P7S1, though successful in TV channel wholesaling, have a minimal presence
         in sports content (especially premium sports content). Sports rights are often
         offered as add-ons rather than basic packages, further indicating that they are not
         necessary for a competitive aggregated AV service. (864)

        6.2.7.2.1.      The Commission’s analysis does not change for other potentially
                        relevant geographic areas
(864)    The Commission has found no indications that its competitive assessment
         regarding the vertical effects of the Transaction, particularly concerning the
         relationship between the acquisition of AV content (upstream) and the wholesale
         supply of TV channels/AV services, and paid AV retail (downstream), would
         differ in any of the other potentially relevant geographic areas. These areas

        D.B.C.5.
(863)   Replies to Questionnaire “04_Wholesale and Retail TV channels/AV services (Phase 1)”, question
        D.B.C.2.1.
(864)   Form CO, paras 2207-9.
                                                 198
          include all German-speaking Territories together, Austria, Luxembourg,
          Liechtenstein, and South Tyrol.
(865)     More precisely, the Commission’s market investigation confirmed that there are
          no specific concerns for any of the other potentially relevant geographic areas. All
          respondents who shared their views indicated that their responses would remain
          the same when considering Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg, and South Tyrol
          in relation to the acquisition of AV content and the wholesale supply of TV
          channels/AV services, and paid AV retail. (865) The majority of these respondents
          reinforced that their answers would not change in that context.
(866)     In this context, the Commission concludes that its assessment regarding potential
          input foreclosure concerns for competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels/AV
          services as well as competing retail providers of AV services applies equally to
          all other potentially relevant geographic areas, including all German-speaking
          Territories.

6.2.7.3. Conclusion
(867)     In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
          the evidence and information available to it, the Commission concludes the
          Transaction does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the
          internal market in the vertical relationship between the markets/market segments
          for the acquisition of AV content (upstream) and the markets for the wholesale
          supply of TV channels/AV services, and the markets for paid AV retail
          (downstream) in Germany and other German-speaking Territories.

6.3.      Conglomerate effects

6.3.1.    Legal framework
(868)     The Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines acknowledge that, whereas in the
          majority of circumstances conglomerate mergers will not lead to competition
          concerns, in certain scenarios they may significantly impede effective
          competition. In particular, the main concern in the context of conglomerate
          mergers is that of foreclosure. (866)
(869)     According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, the combination of products
          in related markets may confer on the merged entity the ability and incentive to
          leverage a strong market position from one market to another by means of tying
          or bundling or other exclusionary practices. In certain circumstances, these
          practices may lead to a reduction in actual or potential rivals’ ability or incentive
          to compete. This may reduce the competitive pressure on the merged entity
          allowing it to increase prices. (867)
(870)     In assessing the likelihood of such a scenario, the Commission examines, first,
          whether the merged firm would have the ability to foreclose its rivals, second,
          whether it would have the economic incentive to do so and, third, whether a
          foreclosure strategy would have a significant detrimental effect on competition,




(865)    Replies to Questionnaire “04_Wholesale and Retail TV channels/AV services (Phase 1)”, question
         D.B.C.6.
(866)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, paras 18 and 93.
(867)    Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, para. 93.
                                                  199
          thus causing harm to consumers. In practice, these factors are often examined
          together as they are closely intertwined. (868)

6.3.2.    Overview of the conglomerate effects
(871)     A number of conglomerate relationships between neighbouring markets can be
          found in several German-speaking Territories.
(872)     However, the vast majority of such conglomerate links are pre-existing. (869) Non-
          preexisting links only arise in relation to the market for the acquisition of AV
          content, among different market segments, and the market for the wholesale
          supply of TV channels/AV services, among different market segments. (870)
          Furthermore, in the market investigation, market participants only raised concerns
          in relation to these markets. (871)
(873)     Consequently, the Commission will only focus on its assessment in the markets
          which have non-preexisting links and for which concerns were raised.

6.3.3.    Market for the acquisition of AV content
(874)     A number of conglomerate relationships between neighbouring markets at the
          level of the market for the acquisition of AV content can be found in several
          German-speaking Territories.
(875)     However, the vast majority of such conglomerate links are pre-existing. (872)

6.3.3.1. Notifying Party’s views
(876)     The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in a
          foreclosure strategy for the following reasons.
(877)     First, the merged entity is not able to leverage its market share with respect to pre-
          produced sports content to the market segments for the acquisition of other sports
          content, film or other AV content in the German-speaking Territories. This is due
          to their moderate combined market share with respect to sport content ([reference
          to the market shares of the Parties]) and the fact that suppliers of sports content
          are distinct from suppliers of films and other AV content (i.e., there is no common
          supplier or customer base). (873)
(878)     Second, the merged entity is not able to leverage its market share with respect to
          pre-produced sports content for the exhibition on SVoD/pay-TV to the market
          segments for the acquisition of sports content for the exhibition on FTA in the
          German-speaking Territories. The Parties have a moderate market share in the
          overall market for the acquisition of sports rights ([reference to the market shares
          of the Parties]). Furthermore, there is no scope for the merged entity to leverage
          its position across exhibition windows, as licensors typically issue separate
          tenders for different modes of exploitation. Moreover, the merged entity would
          lack any incentive to pursue such strategy, due to high acquisition costs and the
          risk of losing rights if licensors disagree to bundle them. Additionally, markets

(872)    Form CO, para. 2215.
(873)    Form CO, paras 2218 and 2221.

         are highly competitive, and prices have been increasing, so it would be doubtful
         that the merged entity bundles its demand using more expensive licence
         packages. (874)
(879)    Third, the merged entity is not able to leverage its market share with respect to
         non-sports content to the market segments for other non-sports content in the
         German-speaking Territories. The Parties’ combined market share does not
         convey any purchasing power, as supply-side substitutability is very high.
         Moreover, the market is highly competitive and engaging in such strategy will
         only result in potential losses of license deals. (875)

6.3.3.2. Commission’s assessment
(880)    The Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the ability or
         incentive to foreclose competing acquirers of AV content by leveraging its market
         power in adjacent segments in the market for the acquisition of AV content in
         German-speaking Territories.
(881)    First, most conglomerate links are pre-existing and not merger specific, and
         would therefore remain in place regardless of the Transaction. The only non-pre-
         existing links refer to: (i) the acquisition of regular pre-produced sports content
         (regular premium and non-premium) and the neighbouring segment for irregular
         premium sports content in Austria; and (ii) the acquisition of pre-produced films
         (1st window) and the neighbouring segment of acquisition of pre-produced non-
         sports content for FTA TV in Germany, Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg,
         South Tyrol, and all German-speaking Territories. (876) Therefore, the following
         assessment will focus on the non-preexisting links.
(882)    Second, the Commission considers that the merged entity will, post-Transaction,
         not have the ability to foreclose competing acquirers of AV content.
(883)    In the first place, the Commission considers that the merged entity does not have
         a significant degree of market power in the German-speaking Territories in the
         market for the acquisition of AV content (or its potential segments). As indicated
         in Section 1.1.1, the Commission notes that the Transaction generally gives rise
         to moderate combined market shares with moderate increments in the overall
         market for the acquisition of AV content (including further segmentations), the
         Parties are constrained by the presence of various competitors (e.g., in January
         2026, the Netflix-Sony licencing deal was announced, under which Sony’s
         theatrical feature films will, on an exclusive basis, stream on Netflix
         worldwide; (877) also in 2026, Warner Bros. Discovery launched HBO Max in
         Germany and Austria, terminating its agreement with Sky DACH; (878) in
         April 2026, Disney+ announced the launch of ESPN in Europe), (879) there is
         increasing competitive pressure from global streaming platforms (such as Netflix,
         Amazon, etc.) and there are low barriers to entry.

(884)    Even when considering the market for the acquisition of sports content and
         potential segments thereof, where the Transaction gives rise to higher market
         shares, the Commission found, as already set forth in Section 1.1.1, that the
         Transaction is unlikely to lead to an increase in market power, in view of the
         generally minimal increments, the existence of sufficient alternative purchasers,
         as well as increased competitive pressure coming from global streaming
         platforms, combined with low barriers to entry and evidence of recent entry.
(885)    In the second place, for foreclosure to be a potential concern, there must be a large
         common pool of customers (880) or, in the case of an acquisition market, a large
         common pool of suppliers.
(886)    Firstly, for most acquisition potential market segments, there is no common pool
         of suppliers. Suppliers of sports content (i.e., sports leagues, federations, and
         event organisers) are different from suppliers of other AV content (i.e., film
         studios, independent producers and distributors).
(887)    Moreover, within different sport segmentations, the leagues licensing sports rights
         for football are distinct from licensors of other sports rights; in fact, when
         comparing the top licensors based on 2024 figures in both categories, there are no
         overlaps (for football, DFL, UEFA, DFB, Austrian Football League, Swiss
         Football League, LaLiga, Premier League, Serie A, Ligue 1, Women’s Super
         League; and for other sports, Formula 1, IOC, FIS, Swiss National Ice Hockey
         League, Int. Biathlon Union, Matchroom / various, NFL, PDC, French Open and
         Austrian Skiing Federation). When comparing top licensors for premium and non-
         premium sports rights, there are only four overlaps (i.e., DFL, UEFA, DFB and
         FIS), and the rest of the top licensors do not overlap (for premium, IOC, Austrian
         Football League, Swiss Football League, Swiss National Ice Hockey League,
         Austrian Skiing Federation and for non-premium, Formula 1, Int. Biathlon Union,
         Matchroom / various, NFL, LaLiga, PDC). Lastly, when comparing top licensors
         for regular and non-regular sports rights, there is only one overlap (i.e., UEFA)
         and the rest of the top licensors do not overlap (for regular DFL, DFB, Formula 1,
         FIS, Austrian Football League, Swiss Football League, Swiss National Ice
         Hockey League, IBU and Matchroom / various and for non-regular, IOC, Europ.
         Handball Fed./ Int. Handball Fed. and FC Bayern München). (881)
(888)    Additionally, suppliers of commissioned and pre-produced non-sports AV
         content do not usually overlap (for commissioned content the main players are
         Leonine, Banijay, Constantin, Beta, Studio Hamburg, ITV Studios, Bavaria,
         All3Media and a German production arm of Warner and pre-produced content is
         usually supplied by the large US studios such as Warner, Universal, Paramount,
         Disney, MGM and Sony), with limited exceptions (i.e., Constantin, Warner and
         Leonine). (882)
(889)    Therefore, the Commission considers that, in most cases, there is no large
         common pool of suppliers for these segmentations.
(890)    Secondly, for other acquisition potential market segments where there is a
         common pool of suppliers, the merged entity has no ability to leverage a strong
         market position across different tenders. Licensors decide how to split the supply
         of license rights and tend to issue tenders with distinct packages for different
         sports content (e.g., UEFA splits media rights for Euro League and Champions

(880)   Para. 100 Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines.
(881)   Notifying Party’s response to RFI 20, question 7.
(882)   Notifying Party’s response to RFI 24, para. 18.
                                                   202
         League with a number of packages in each tender) and for different modes of
         exploitation (i.e., free TV/FTA vs pay-TV/SVoD) (e.g., the most recent DFL
         tender for 1. and 2. Bundesliga and DFB tenders for DFB-Pokal). (883) Therefore,
         the Commission considers that for most of these segmentations, there is no ability
         to leverage a strong market position, as winning a tender in one market segment
         will not provide the merged entity with an advantage in a tender in another market
         segment.
(891)    Several market participants have responded to the market investigation in a
         similar manner, indicating the difficulties to leverage a strong market position
         across tenders or offers: “[t]o our knowledge premium sports rights (e.g.
         Bundesliga) are sold by tenders. So, in a very simple way the highest bidder gets
         the rights. In such cases a strong market position in itself is not helping to acquire
         such rights”; (884) and “[t]here is no such thing as a “strong market position” in
         the acquisition of sports AV content, as prior acquisitions of rights have little
         bearing on subsequent acquisitions. The determinative factor on who acquires
         rights is the terms the acquirer is willing to accept, e.g. the price”. (885)
(892)    Moreover, while some tenders include different packages of rights for FTA and
         pay-TV, the majority of market participants have indicated that it is not important
         for suppliers that the same purchaser of sports rights is able to acquire both FTA
         and pay-TV broadcasting rights (886) and have explained that: “we sell to the
         player who pays best”; “[w]e are just aiming for the best result for FTA and
         PayTV rights, regardless of whether the rights are awarded to the same or various
         buyers”; “we can sell them separately as we do at the moment or grant different
         level of exclusivity”; and “[t]o us, it is important to achieve a broad range of
         potential buyers, a diverse market demand as well as reaching different target
         groups.” (887)
(893)    In the case of non-sports content, there is also no ability to leverage a strong
         market position across negotiations on an individual basis. For example, pre-
         produced content can be licensed on an individual title basis (e.g., the series [name
         of the series] was negotiated as an individual title). As regard commissioned
         content, the majority of agreements cover individual titles rather than volumes or
         libraries (e.g., RTL’s agreement with […] or Sky DACH’s agreement with
         […]). (888)
(894)    Thirdly, while in the case of non-sports content there is more often a common
         pool of suppliers, the Commission notes that it is a common market practice that
         these suppliers bundle AV content by offering volume deals including licenses
         for several titles and sometimes across more than one type of content (for instance
         films and other AV content, such as series). (889) For example, for pre-produced
         content, RTL’s large volume deals include agreements with [business/commercial
         information of RTL]. Sky DACH’s [business/commercial information of Sky

         DACH]. (890) This is particularly relevant for suppliers holding a large amount of
         content, such as Paramount, Sony and Universal, who have significant
         countervailing power. In addition, the Commission considers that the market is
         sufficiently fragmented on the acquisition side. (891) Therefore, in this case, the
         Commission considers that the merged entity has no ability to leverage its position
         in the market to foreclose competing acquirers of AV content, either through
         bundling or through other strategies.
(895)    Third, for the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged
         entity will likely not have the incentive to foreclose competing acquirers of AV
         content by bundling AV content. Such strategy does not seem to be profitable for
         the merged entity.
(896)    In the first place, bundling rights would result in larger and more expensive
         license packages. In the last years, there have been increasing prices across sports
         rights, [business/commercial information of the Parties] These rising costs may
         result     in   non-participation      in    tenders    due    to    high     prices
                                                              892
         (e.g., [business/commercial information of RTL]) ( ) and in being out-bid by its
         competitors       (e.g.,   RTL        [business/commercial       information      of
                   893 894 895 896
         RTL]). ( ) ( ) ( ) ( ) Moreover, there is an increased competitive pressure
         from global streaming platforms (e.g., Paramount+ has recently acquired the
         rights for UEFA Champions League for the period from 2027 to 2031, which had
         been held until recently by DAZN). (897) Due to these trends, the Commission
         considers it improbable that the merged entity outbids its competitors each time,
         even if it implements a bundling strategy.
(897)    In the second place, engaging in such a strategy may reduce the merged entity’s
         flexibility in selecting the content that it wants to acquire and may lead to over-
         acquisition of rights that are not commercially optimal for the merged entity or
         that are low-value content. (898) Therefore, the Commission considers that it is not
         probable that the merged entity would engage in such a strategy.
(898)    In the third place, if the merged entity tried to force suppliers which negotiate
         bundled packages (e.g., in the case of non-sports rights), to supply additional
         content to the merged entity (e.g., at a better price or under more favourable
         conditions), licensors would have enough alternatives to switch due to the highly
         competitive market of the acquisition of AV content. In particular, the market is
         characterized by having multiple competitors (e.g., P7S1, Netflix, Amazon, ARD
         and ZDF) and an increased competitive pressure from global streaming platforms

         (such as Netflix, Amazon, etc.). (899) Therefore, the Commission considers that it
         is not probable that the merged entity would engage in such a strategy.
(899)    In the fourth place, the Commission acknowledges the Parties’ argument that post
         Transaction, [business/commercial information of the Parties and reference to
         internal documents]. (900) (901) (902)
(900)    Nonetheless, as this argument refers to future plans of the merged entity which
         have not taken place yet, no definite conclusion can be obtained from such
         statement.
(901)    Fourth, most market participants that have expressed concerns in the market
         investigation refer to a “greater bargaining power” (903) of the merged entity or
         “increase network effects and economies of scale”. (904) Such concerns are linked
         to a potential strengthening of the Parties’ buyer power in the markets for the
         acquisition of sports and/or non-sports AV content. However, as indicated in
         Section 1.1.1, the Commission considers that the Transaction is unlikely to lead
         to an increase of buyer power by the merged entity, or otherwise elimination of
         significant competitive constraints, which would result in a significant
         impediment of effective competition in relation to overall AV content, non-sports
         and sports content.
(902)    Fifth, the Commission has no indications that the results of its competitive
         assessment concerning the conglomerate effects of the Transaction in relation to
         the acquisition of AV content would change for any of the other potentially
         relevant geographic areas, i.e., all German-speaking Territories together, Austria,
         Germany, Luxembourg, Liechtenstein and/or South Tyrol. Most market
         investigation respondents who expressed a view confirmed that all of their
         responses would be the same as for Germany if considering solely Austria,
         Liechtenstein, Luxembourg and South Tyrol. (905)

6.3.3.3. Conclusion
(903)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
         the evidence and information available to it, for the purposes of the present
         Decision, the Commission concludes the Transaction does not give rise to serious
         doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of a conglomerate
         foreclosure strategy in the market for acquisition AV content, considering that the
         merged entity would not have the ability or incentive to engage in such a strategy.

6.3.4.    Market for the wholesale supply of TV channels and services
(904)     A number of conglomerate relationships between neighbouring markets at the
          level of the market for the wholesale supply of TV channels and services can be
          found in several German-speaking Territories.
(905)     However, the vast majority of such conglomerate links are pre-existing. (906)

6.3.4.1. Notifying Party’s views
(906)     The Notifying Party submits that it lacks the ability or incentive to engage in a
          foreclosure strategy for the following reasons.
(907)     First, the merged entity lacks the ability and incentive to foreclose competing
          wholesale suppliers of basic pay and premium pay-TV channels and SVoD
          services in Germany and Austria, as it lacks a significant degree of market power
          (its market share does not exceed 30%). Moreover, it lacks the incentive; if the
          merged entity forced acquirers to take additional channels, customers could
          decide to find alternative content. Alternatively, if costs were passed on
          downstream, end-customers could decide to switch to the retail offerings of the
          merged entity, but it is improbable that they would do so due to the Parties’ low
          market share in the downstream market. Additionally, this strategy would lead to
          severe risk of retaliation, as acquirers of AV services are generally network
          providers (e.g., DTAG or Vodafone). (907)
(908)     Second, the merged entity lacks the ability and incentive to foreclose competing
          wholesale suppliers of basic pay and premium pay-TV channels by genre in
          German-speaking territories. The Parties’ combined market share only exceeds
          30% in several narrow segments: (i) wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels
          for kids content (in Germany and Austria combined and separately as well as in
          the German-speaking Territories); and (ii) wholesale supply of basic pay-TV
          channels for general entertainment, news content and of premium pay-TV
          channels for sports content (in Austria). According to the Notifying Party, such
          channels are not a “must have” and they would not establish any market power
          for the merged entity. Moreover, this strategy would lead to severe risk of
          retaliation from DTAG or Vodafone, which hold significant countervailing buyer
          power. Additionally, it lacks the incentive, as customers could decide to find
          alternative content (both acquirers of TV channels and end customers). (908)

6.3.4.2. Commission’s assessment
(909)     The Commission considers that the merged entity will not have the ability or
          incentive to foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels by
          leveraging its market power in adjacent segments in the market of wholesale
          supply of TV channels in any of the German-speaking Territories.
(910)     First, most conglomerate links are pre-existing and not merger specific, and
          would therefore remain in place regardless of the Transaction. The only non-pre-
          existing links refer to: (i) the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels in
          Austria (for both genres general entertainment and news) and the neighbouring
          segment for the wholesale supply of premium sports pay-TV channels in Austria;
          (ii) the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels in Germany, Austria and
          German speaking territories (for the genre kids) and the neighbouring segment for

(906)    Form CO, para. 2215.
(907)    Form CO, paras 2240-2244.
(908)    Form CO, paras 2245-2259.
                                             206
         the wholesale supply of premium sports pay-TV channels in Germany, Austria,
         Liechtenstein and German speaking territories; and (iii) the wholesale supply of
         premium sports pay-TV channels in Austria and the neighbouring segments for
         (a) the wholesale supply of basic pay-TV channels (kids, general entertainment
         and news) in Austria, and (b) the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV channels
         (general entertainment) in Austria. (909) Therefore, the following assessment will
         focus on the non-preexisting links.
(911)    Second, the Commission considers that the merged entity will, post-Transaction,
         not have the ability to foreclose competing wholesale suppliers of TV channels.
         According to the Non-Horizontal Merger Guidelines, for foreclosure to be a
         concern, the merged entity must have a significant degree of market power in one
         of the markets concerned. (910) The Commission considers that the merged entity
         does not have a significant degree of market power in the market for the wholesale
         supply of TV channels (or its potential segments) in any of the German-speaking
         Territories.
(912)    In the first place, as indicated in Section 6.1.4, the Commission considers that the
         merged entity does not have a significant degree of market power in the wholesale
         supply of premium Pay TV channels carrying general entertainment content in
         Austria.
(913)    In the second place, the Commission considers that the merged entity does not
         have a significant degree of market power in:
         (a)       The wholesale supply of basic general entertainment pay-TV channels in
                   Austria. The Commission notes that the Transaction gives rise to
                   moderate combined market shares with no increment brought by the
                   Transaction (i.e., RTL’s market share in 2024 in Austria was [30-40]%
                   and Sky DACH was not present in the market), RTL’s total viewership
                   is only c. [10-20]%, (911) the Parties are constrained by the presence of
                   various competitors (P7S1, public broadcasters and RTL2, among others
                   such as Red Bull Media House, High View, Paramount, and Warner
                   Bros. Discovery), (912) there is increasing competition from SVoD
                   services and AV retailers seeking to acquire TV channels are able to
                   switch between wholesale suppliers of TV channels.
         (b)       The wholesale supply of basic kids pay-TV channels in Germany,
                   Austria and German speaking territories. The Commission notes that
                   although the Transaction gives rise to combined market shares over 30%
                   (i.e., RTL’s market share in 2024 in Germany was [40-50]%, in Austria
                   was [50-60]% and in German-speaking territories was [40-50]% and Sky
                   DACH was not present in the market), (913) the Parties are constrained
                   by the presence of various competitors (e.g., public broadcasters),
                   competing SVoD service providers also offer a wide variety of “kids’
                   content” which can be selected by choosing “kids” in the user profile of
                   each service (i.e., Amazon Prime, Apple TV, Disney +, HBO Max, Joyn,
                   Netflix, Paramount + and YouTube) (914) and AV retailers seeking to

                   Commission considers it improbable that the merged entity would be
                   able to exert a significant degree of market power in this segment.
(914)    Third, for the sake of completeness, the Commission considers that the merged
         entity will likely not have the incentive to foreclose competing wholesale

          investigation indicate that the merged entity could leverage a strong market
          position in the wholesale supply of basic TV channels to increase its position on
          the market for the wholesale supply of premium pay-TV sports channels (951) in
          German-speaking Territories, (952) as a result of which it could “completely stop
          or degrade the wholesale supply of its channels to make its owned direct to
          consumer platforms the only destination for consumers”. (953) The Commission

          assessment concerning the conglomerate effects of the Transaction in relation to
          wholesale supply of TV channels would change for any of the potentially relevant
          geographic areas, i.e., all German-speaking Territories together, Austria,
          Germany or Liechtenstein. Most market investigation respondents who expressed
          a view confirmed that all of their responses would be the same as for Germany if
          considering solely Austria, Liechtenstein, Luxembourg and South Tyrol. (954)

6.3.4.3. Conclusion
(925)     In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
          the evidence and information available to it, for the purposes of the present
          Decision, the Commission concludes the Transaction does not give rise to serious
          doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of a conglomerate
          foreclosure strategy in the market for wholesale supplying of TV channels,
          considering that the merged entity would not have the ability or incentive to
          engage in such a strategy.

6.3.5.    Market for the supply of AV advertising space
(926)     A number of conglomerate relationships between neighbouring markets at the
          level of the supply of advertising space can be found in Germany and Austria
          when considered separately or combined.
(927)     However, the vast majority of such conglomerate links are pre-existing given that
          RTL is active in almost all markets or segments in the supply of advertising. Only
          when considering narrower market segments based on sales channel in Germany
          would there be new conglomerate links created by the Transaction, namely
          between the market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (direct sales) and the
          market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (sold via intermediation). (955)

                                                    212
(928)    In the present Section, the Commission will assess whether RTL could leverage
         its market position in the market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (direct
         sales) in Germany into the potential neighbouring market for the supply of FTA
         TV advertising (intermediated sales) and whether Sky DACH could leverage its
         market position in the market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (sold via
         intermediation) in Germany into the potential neighbouring market for the supply
         of FTA TV advertising (direct sales).

6.3.5.1. Bundling the supply of FTA TV advertising (direct sales) with FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales)

        6.3.5.1.1.       Notifying Party’s views
(929)    In relation to the potential leveraging of RTL’s position in the market for the
         supply FTA advertising (direct sales) by bundling such services with FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales) in Germany, the Notifying Party submits that
         RTL’s ability to pursue such a strategy does not change through the Transaction.
         While RTL currently does not have intermediated FTA TV advertising sales in
         Germany, it could easily enter that segment. The Transaction does not provide
         RTL with any technical capabilities or know-how it did not already possess,
         particularly given that RTL already sells third-party FTA advertising inventory in
         Austria and its own FTA TV inventory in Germany. Further, RTL has never
         attempted such a bundling strategy in the past nor is it aware of any market
         participants doing do. In fact, [business/commercial information of RTL]. (956)
(930)    The Notifying Party submits that the merged entity would not have the ability to
         engage in such a bundling strategy. (957)
(931)    First, there is no plausible way to engage in such bundling in a manner that could
         generate realistic demand. With regards to a potential strategy of pure bundling,
         TV inventory is sold via individual 30-second slots that are booked independently
         by advertisers/media buying agencies, and the merged entity has no mechanism
         to offer multiple slots as a single bundled product. As for a strategy of mixed
         bundling, while technically possible, it would fail commercially. Media agencies
         have a strong interest in retaining autonomy over the specific slots they book in
         order to optimise campaigns to their client’s needs. Further, Sky DACH’s FTA
         TV advertising activities in Germany are […] related to the sales of High View
         inventory. (958) Bundling this inventory with RTL’s FTA TV advertising (direct
         sales) would not be a commercially attractive offer to media buying agencies
         because such inventories serve different purposes and attract different types of
         advertisers. While RTL reaches a broad audience and the majority of its bookings
         comes from FMCG, financial services, telecommunications, automotive and other
         consumer-facing sectors, High View’s channels cater to niche audiences of
         specific music genres (e.g. rap or “Schlager”) which lack the same broad appeal
         and attracts a different profile of advertisers. (959)


        has not been considered in the past by the Commission. Further, the results of the market
        investigation confirmed that such market segmentation is not relevant. Therefore, the Commission
        considers that there is no potential market for advertising in sports from which the merged entity
        could leverage market power.
(956)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 55
(957)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 57.
(958)   An operator of small FTA channels for music videos (Deluxe Music, Schlager Deluxe, DELUXE
        DANCE and DELUXE RAP).
(959)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, paras 58-61.
                                                   213
(932)    Second, RTL does not have market power in the market for FTA TV advertising
         (direct sales) in Germany. Media buying agencies have alternative suppliers of
         FTA TV advertising (direct sales). Further, advertising budgets are shifting
         materially from TV advertising to SAVoD, YouTube and social media and RTL
         has lost [30-40]% of its linear TV advertising revenues since 2019. This exerts
         significant out-of-market constraints and forcing any unwanted bundles would
         only accelerate this trend. Media buying agencies hold significant market
         power. (960)
(933)    The Notifying Party submits that the merged entity would not have the incentive
         to engage in such a strategy. The merged entity retains the entirety of the revenue
         from direct sales while it earns only a commission on intermediated sales. Even a
         marginal percentage loss in direct sales could not be offset by gains in
         intermediates dales. It is not clear how such losses could be offset by gains in the
         market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) given the size
         of the market ([…] Euro in annual revenues representing less than [0-5]% of all
         FTA TV advertising sales). This is unlikely to change as TV broadcasters prefer
         to sell their own inventory (giving them greater control over pricing) and already
         have the expertise to sell their inventory without using a third-party. Further,
         smaller broadcasters have TV slots with lower value (less reach) and relatively
         high fixed costs (e.g. handling, client relationships) for a smear volume of
         revenue. (961)
(934)    The Notifying Party submits that such strategy would not have an impact on the
         market for FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) because ad sales houses
         currently selling their own FTA TV inventory can easily expand into selling third-
         party inventory as there are no barriers to entry or expansion. (962)

        6.3.5.1.2.      Commission assessment
(935)    For the reasons set out below, the Commission considers, for the purposes of the
         present Decision, it cannot be excluded that the merged entity could have the
         ability to bundle the supply of FTA TV advertising (direct sales) and FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales). However, the Commission considers that the
         merged entity would not have the incentive to bundle the supply of FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales) and FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales).
         Therefore, for the purposes of the present Decision, the Commission considers
         that the question whether there would likely be a significant detrimental effect on
         competition on the market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated
         sales), is not relevant and can be left open.
(936)    In relation to the merged entity’s ability to bundle the supply of FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales) and FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales), the
         Commission considers that RTL does not have market power in the market for
         the supply of FTA TV advertising (direct sales) in Germany. In this market, RTL
         has a market share of [40-50]%. However, RTL’s competitor P7S1 has a market
         share of [30-40]% and there are additional alternatives in the market such as ARD,
         ZDF or RTL2. Further, as explained above in Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, the market for
         linear TV advertising (including FTA advertising) is decreasing and under
         growing competitive pressure from SAVoD platforms or YouTube.


(960)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 62.
(961)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, paras 63-65.
(962)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 66.
                                                 214
(937)    The Commission also notes that the customers that directly acquire RTL’s
         advertising space in Germany are not necessarily the same customers that acquire
         intermediated advertising space in Germany. Most current intermediated sales of
         FTA TV advertising sales in Germany are related to the sales of High View
         inventory (by Sky DACH). […]% of the top 20 TV advertising customers of RTL
         and High View differ. Further, RTL has more than […] times as many TV
         advertising customers as High View ([…] vs. […]) which would mean that over
         […]% of RTL’s customers cannot be High View’s customers. Further, the type
         of advertisers that acquire space from RTL and High View are different. RTL’s
         FTA TV channels are broad-reach and sought after by advertisers active in
         FMCG, financial services, telecommunications, automative and other general
         consumer-facing sectors. In contrast, High View’s channels are niche music
         channels addressing small and specialised audiences. (963) (964)
(938)    However, given that several advertisers and media buying agencies purchase
         advertising inventory from both RTL and High View, the Commission concludes
         that it cannot be excluded that the merged entity could bundle the supply of FTA
         TV advertising (direct sales) with FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales).
(939)    Even if the merged entity would have the ability to bundle the supply of FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales) with FTA TV advertising (indirect sales), the
         Commission considers that the merged entity would not have the incentive to
         engage in such a strategy.
(940)    The Commission considers that, in case there would be a post-merger incentive
         to sell more advertising space (namely by bundling), the merged entity’s incentive
         would be to sell more of its own advertising inventory (to the extent it is still
         available) and not additional third-party advertising inventory. This would be the
         clear profit maximizing strategy for the merged entity, namely because it would
         retain the entirety of the revenues generated by the additional direct sales of
         advertising space as opposed to only earning a commission on additional
         intermediated sales. The merged entity would only have an incentive to sell third-
         party inventory as an eventual ad-on (and not a bundle) to its own advertising
         inventory. This is further evidenced by the fact that the only […] advertisers
         among the top 20 customers that overlap with RTL and High View channels
         ([reference to customers]) purchase high amounts of advertising inventory from
         RTL and High View’s inventory is only a marginal ad-on: in 2025, [reference to
         customer] spent […] million EUR on the purchase of RTL’s TV advertising
         inventory and […] EUR of High View’s advertising inventory; in the same year,
         [reference to customer] spent […] EUR on RTL and […] on High View
         advertising inventory. (965)
(941)    For the purposes of this Decision, it is not necessary to assess whether the
         bundling of FTA advertising (direct sales) with FTA advertising (intermediated
         sales) would have a significant impact on the market for the supply of FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales). However, the Commission notes that, in the
         market investigation, the majority of market respondents that expressed a view
         considered that bundling by the merged entity could not have a negative impact
         on the market for the supply AV advertising in Germany. (966)


(963)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 19, paras 74 and 75.
(964)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 19, paras 76 and 77.
(965)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 19, para. 77.
(966)   Replies to Questionnaire “02_Advertising - Germany (Phase 1)”, question E.B.B.5.
                                                 215
        6.3.5.1.3.      Conclusion
(942)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of bundling of FTA TV advertising (direct sales) with FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales), considering that the merged entity would not
         have the incentive to engage in such a strategy.

6.3.5.2. Bundling the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) with FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales)

        6.3.5.2.1.      Notifying Party’s views
(943)    In relation to the potential leveraging of Sky DACH’s position in the market for
         the supply FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) in Germany by bundling
         such services with FTA TV advertising (direct sales), the Notifying Party submits
         that no such bundling is currently being done, nor would it be merger-specific for
         the same reasons set out above in paragraph (929) of Section 6.3.5.1.1. (967)
(944)    The Notifying Party submits that the merged entity would not have the ability to
         engage in such a bundling strategy. (968) First, even if the merged entity somehow
         sold all of its intermediated volume in a bundle, the volumes are too small to
         matter. Sky DACH’s intermediated FTA TV sales are around […] EUR in 2025,
         less than [0-5]% of the approximately […] EUR of FTA TV advertising sold in
         Germany and Austria. It is not credible that bundling <[0-5]% of niche music-
         related inventory would lead to any material reduction in agencies’ spend on rival
         direct sellers. Second, there is no reason why customers would accept such a
         bundle. Sky DACH’s intermediated inventory is not a particularly important
         product (it relates to 4 High View channels that are commercially insignificant
         and gave a viewership of below [0-5]%). When confronted with an unwanted
         bundle, customers could easily purchase inventory from one of the many rival
         suppliers of direct or intermediated FTA TV advertising inventory instead. (969)
(945)    The Notifying Party submits that the merged entity would not have the incentive
         to engage in such a strategy as it would lead to the likely loss of the merged
         entity’s existing intermediated revenues. First, customers purchase FTA TV
         advertising on a spot-by-spot basis and if forced to acquire additional, unwanted
         spots as part of a bundle, they may simply decline to purchase altogether. Second,
         High View could easily switch to a competing intermediary or take sales in-house
         if the merged entity no longer optimises its sale efforts (in particular as it co-sells
         inventory for […] of the 4 intermediated channels already and the contract term
         is […] years). Therefore, engaging in such strategy would not be profitable. (970)
(946)    The Notifying Party submits that such strategy would not have an impact on the
         market for FTA TV advertising (direct sales) which is highly competitive. RTL
         faces significant out-of-market constraints from advertising budgets shifting to
         online channels, and a number of strong competitors remain, including P7S1,
         ARD, ZDF, El Cartel Brothers and a range of smaller broadcaster. Further, any




(967)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 68.
(968)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 67.
(969)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, paras 70 and 71.
(970)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 72.
                                                 216
         bundling involving volumes representing <[0-5]% of the market cannot have a
         material effect on competition. (971)

        6.3.5.2.2.       Commission assessment
(947)    For the reasons set out below, the Commission considers that, for the purposes of
         the present Decision, the merged entity would not have the ability to bundle the
         supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) with FTA TV advertising
         (direct sales) in Germany. Therefore, for the purposes of the present Decision, the
         Commission considers that the question whether the merged entity would have
         the incentive to bundle the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales)
         with FTA TV advertising (direct sales) and whether such strategy would likely
         have a significant detrimental effect on competition on the market for the supply
         of FTA TV advertising (direct sales), is not relevant and can be left open.
(948)    In relation to the merged entity’s ability to bundle the supply of FTA TV
         advertising (intermediated sales) with FTA TV advertising (direct sales), the
         Commission considers that Sky DACH does not have market power in the market
         for the supply of FTA advertising (intermediated sales) in Germany. In this
         market, Sky DACH has a market share of [80-90]%. However, for the same
         reasons set out above in Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.2 (in relation to the market for the
         intermediated supply of FTA TV advertising in Germany and Austria combined),
         the Commission considers that Sky DACH does not have market power in the
         market for the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) in Germany.
         In particular, the Commission takes into account that: (i) FTA TV broadcasters
         can sell their inventory directly to customers, (ii) FTA TV broadcasters wishing
         to sell their advertising inventory through intermediation can turn to other
         broadcasters which are already active in intermediation services in Germany such
         as Goldvertise or Visoon, (iii) other broadcasters such as ARD, ZDF, RTL2 or
         Warner Bros. Discovery, can easily enter the market for FTA TV intermediation,
         and (iv) media buying agencies can purchase advertising inventory for trading
         without allocating it to specific customers/advertisers for subsequent resale which
         is analogous to the intermediation by ad sales houses. Further, as explained above
         in Section 6.1.5.2.1.2.1, the market for linear TV advertising (including FTA TV
         advertising) is decreasing and under growing competitive pressure from SAVoD
         platforms or YouTube.
(949)    The Commission further notes that Sky DACH’s high market share likely reflects
         a lack of demand for intermediation of FTA TV advertising in Germany. More
         than 99.5% of all FTA TV advertising space in Germany is not sold via
         intermediation. In 2025, Sky DACH generated around […] EUR through the sale
         of this advertising space (of the approx. […] million EUR of FTA TV advertising
         sold in Germany), resulting in c. […] EUR of revenue for Sky DACH in
         commissions. Broadcasters typically prefer to sell their advertising space without
         intermediation, namely because outsourcing to a third-party entails the payment
         of a commission on the revenue generated (instead of retaining the full revenue
         generated) and less autonomy on ad placement and pricing. As mentioned above,
         advertisers and media buying agencies looking to acquire advertising space in
         specific slots of particular FTA TV channels in order to reach their target audience
         don’t need to do so via intermediation services: they can purchase such
         advertising space directly from the relevant FTA TV broadcasters.


(971)   Notifying Party’s response to Commission’s RFI 18, para. 72.
                                                 217
(950)    This is especially relevant when taking into consideration the particular FTA TV
         intermediation inventory of Sky DACH in Germany. Sky DACH’s market share
         in the supply for FTA TV advertising in Germany results from a single
         intermediation agreement for the sale of 4 small channels of High View
         (i.e., Deluxe Music, Schagler Deluxe, DELUXE DANCE and Deluxe Rap). High
         Views’ channels each have a viewership share of [0-5]% which would indicate
         that such inventory is not viewed by many customers as particularly important.
         Further, currently High View already directly co-sells the inventory for […] of
         the 4 mentioned intermediated channels [reference to customers] which means
         that customers can buy such advertising space directly from High View. The
         intermediation agreements with Sky DACH have a [number of years]. If the
         merged entity were to adopt such a bundling strategy, High View could easily
         make its inventory directly available to customers. In particular, High View
         recently acquired Goldvertise, an ad sales house, which will allow High View to
         sell its TV advertising space directly in the future.
(951)    The Commission also notes that the customers that directly acquire RTL’s
         advertising space in Germany are not necessarily the same customers that acquire
         intermediated advertising space in Germany. Most current intermediated sales of
         FTA TV advertising sales in Germany are related to the sales of High View
         inventory (by Sky DACH). […]% of the top 20 TV advertising customers of RTL
         and High View differ. Further, RTL has more than […] times as many TV
         advertising customers as High View ([…]) which would mean that over […]% of
         RTL’s customers cannot be High View’s customers. Further, the type of
         advertisers that acquire space from RTL and High View are different. RTL’s FTA
         TV channels are broad-reach and sought after by advertisers active in FMCG,
         financial services, telecommunications, automative and other general consumer-
         facing sectors. In contrast, High View’s channels are niche music channels
         addressing small and specialised audiences.
(952)    Therefore, for all the reasons above, the merged entity would not have the ability
         to bundle the supply of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) with FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales).

        6.3.5.2.3.    Conclusion
(953)    For the reasons set out above, the Commission concludes that the Transaction
         does not give rise to serious doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market
         as a result of bundling of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales) with FTA TV
         advertising (direct sales), considering that the merged entity would not have the
         ability to engage in such a strategy.

6.3.5.3. Conclusion
(954)    In view of the above considerations and in light of the market investigation and
         the evidence and information available to it, for the purposes of the present
         Decision, the Commission concludes the Transaction does not give rise to serious
         doubts as to its compatibility with the internal market as a result of the bundling
         of: (i) FTA TV advertising (direct sales) with FTA TV advertising (intermediated
         sales) considering that the merged entity would not have the incentive to engage
         in such strategy, or (ii) the bundling of FTA TV advertising (intermediated sales)
         with FTA TV advertising (direct sales), considering that the merged entity would
         not have the ability to engage in such strategy.


                                             218
7.      CONCLUSION
(955)   For the above reasons, the European Commission has decided not to oppose the
        notified operation and to declare it compatible with the internal market and with
        the EEA Agreement. This decision is adopted in application of Article 6(1)(b) of
        the Merger Regulation and Article 57 of the EEA Agreement.
